# Changelog -- September 2026

Split out of `docs/CHANGELOG.md` verbatim on 2026-09-07; that file is
now the index. Newest first, as before.

## 2026-09-28 (night): a recorder for Fran's own play

**Why.** The profile_0 audit (week 77, mean hero level 1.0, zero bosses; see
`docs/COMMUNITY_GUIDES.md` Batch 4) put the bridge's gap in the decisions
AROUND fights -- quest choice, farming, bosses, spending -- not in combat.
Fran: *"a logger of the inputs to try to teach the bridge how I play"*. Those
decisions are few per week, so the recording is a spec and a ruler for the
decide layers and a pace measurement for the actuator, not combat training
data.

**What.** `bridge/recorder.py` (`bat\record_play.bat`): low-level mouse and
keyboard hooks that record ONLY while `Darkest.exe` owns the foreground
window (`should_record`, tested), drop injected input, bank a 640x360 JPEG
per press, and gzip every save file whose contents changed -- the whole
profile the first time it moves, so a new campaign made mid-session is
caught. `tools/recording_report.py` interprets offline: clicks named against
the bridge's own layout, pace per screen, F8 marks, and `--unpack`, which
rebuilds the raid/roster/map triples `run.py --replay` reads.

**Measured, not assumed.** `--selftest` proves hook-to-queue with a SHIFT tap
and a net-zero wheel pair; a zero-distance wheel is dropped by Windows before
any hook sees it, which the first self-test run showed. Black frames (a
borderless game on its monitor) warn once on screen instead of producing a
silently frameless session. The mark key is F8 because F9 is the bridge's
kill key.

## 2026-09-28 (evening): the party order after fights, and a Windows prompt over the game

**The marching order is not restored after fights -- Fran's "comp order looks
weird".** The embark seats the curated comp exactly as planned (e.g. "Keen
Edge: Withering" fronts the Occultist on purpose: Hands from the Abyss
launches from ranks 1-2). After each fight `_reset_formation` clicks the
game's "Default Party order" button and then tries MOVE swaps; over the
2026-09-28 raids the next fight started OUT of the marching order 38 times in
41, and all 50 MOVE swaps "did not reach the save". A banked frame shows the
click landing on the button (its tooltip is up), and two of the out-of-order
cases were the quest-complete banner swallowing the click. The ordinary case
is not yet seen: the actuator now keeps numbered before/after/wrong frames for
the first five cases of a run (`_party_order_N_*.png`). Open.

The first numbered case (`captures/_party_order_1_before.png`) is the banner
again: the crest is rising over the loot window as the click goes out. So
`_reset_formation` now reads the screen first. On `quest_complete` it sets
`_formation_due` and sends nothing; the room poll retries it every block
until the banner is answered
(`test_the_party_order_waits_for_the_quest_complete_banner`). The
no-banner case is still unseen.

**The embark walks back before refusing.** Twice a town visit ended on a screen
the recogniser could not name and the embark refused, losing the iteration.
`embark_setup` now banks the frame, runs `walk` back to the Hamlet, and tries
the shared panel close twice before it refuses.

**A Windows Security prompt stole the game's input.** The loop stopped after
three iterations on the post-raid quirks page, "Return to Town" not responding;
the foreground window was `PickerHost` ("Seguridad de Windows"), raised after
Steam was started from the session. Every SendInput went to the prompt. Not a
bridge bug, and not something the bridge should answer.

## 2026-09-28 (later): Run and Hide was only a step back, and stealthed enemies stayed targetable

Fran, watching a raid: *"the combat model is not using run and hide from
runaway, is restoration properly modeled?"* and *"does the model interpret
stealth properly ... stealthed heroes/enemies can't be targeted unless AoE"*.

**Run and Hide.** `run_and_hide` is `.move 2 0`, self-target, effects
"RW Stealth" and "RW Run HoT N". Both effects carry their payload as BUFFS:
`rw_run_conditional_stealth` (stat_type `stealth`) and `rw_run_hot_N`
(stat_type `hp_dot_heal`, 5 HP/turn at level 5, 3 rounds, rule
`is_stealthed`). The engine read neither stat type, so in the simulator the
skill was a free step backwards and the policy never chose it. Only these six
Runaway buffs use the two types; the Grave Robber's Shadow Fade stealths via
an effect's `stealth_duration` and the Flagellant's Reclaim heals via an
effect's `heal_dot_*`, and both were already modelled. `_effects` now maps
the two buff types onto the same `is_stealthed` / `heal_dots` machinery; the
heal's `is_stealthed` rule is approximated as true (the skill grants 4 rounds
of stealth with 3 of heal).

**Stealth targeting.** Monsters have always skipped stealthed heroes for
single-target skills (`_monster_ai`, AoE still hits, `ignore_stealth`
bypasses, all-stealthed falls back). The HERO side had no such rule: a
stealthed Brigand Fusilier, Crone, Fishman Shaman or Skeleton Militia stayed a
legal single target. `get_valid_actions` now drops stealthed enemy ranks from
single-target, non-ally skills without `ignore_stealth`, lifting the rule when
every living enemy is stealthed. `tests/engine/test_runaway_run_and_hide.py`.

**The live checkpoint trained without either**, so it will not value Run and
Hide or respect enemy stealth until the next retrain (the observation width is
unchanged; this is the "what a feature CONTAINS" kind of change). Staged with
the in-combat items for that retrain. Full suite green, oracles unchanged.

## 2026-09-28: nurture one hero at a time, a measured Blacksmith, pack room, the Granary reserve

Fran's agenda of 2026-09-27 (docs/NEXT.md). Evidence first.

**"Missing curios" is mostly a full pack.** Over 40 raid logs of 2026-09-26/27:
37 curios given up, 33 of them right after the game's "no room" modal; "no
room" appears about 480 times. A recorded full pack (`orphan_crypts_20260920`)
holds 8 provision slots of 15. Two changes:

- **The spare-gold top-up fills open stacks only** (`embarkplan.fill_open_stacks`).
  "+6 food, +9 torch" on a 12-food, 9-torch basket opened two surplus slots
  for the whole raid; the stacks are 12 and 8 (`inventory.item_defs`).
- **A full pack makes room from SURPLUS supplies** (`inventory.plan_surplus_discard`):
  after the stack merge and the dead-weight rule, a supply stack wholly
  beyond the raid layer's own reserve (`provision_use.room_reserve`: items
  earmarked for curios ahead, food for the hunger checks left), keeping one of
  each trigger item, never a torch, shovel, key or firewood, and worth at most
  300 gold at shop price. Food is never offered (the positive `supply` gate).

**Food "Full"** is handled by the actuator: a portion the pack did not lose
marks the hero full and it is not retried. Fran, 2026-09-28: fullness lasts
only until the next COMPLETED fight, after which the hero can eat again, so
`ActRunner._fed_again_after_fight` clears both the full mark and the food
portions counted toward `MAX_FOOD_BEFORE_FULL` when a fight ends (it was for
the whole raid). The repeated
"USE food" lines were the raid layer's printout, not sends. The hunger budget
exists too (`room_reserve` reserves food per remaining hunger check).

**Nurture, one hero at a time** (`townplan.plan_nurture` / `nurture_buys`,
`town.nurture_visit`). The shipped hero trees gate every rank on the previous
rank, a BUILDING rank (`guild.skill_levels`, `blacksmith.weapon` ...) and a
`prerequisite_resolve_level` (`town_costs.load_hero_ranks`), so "max out what
the hero can take at their level" is derived, not chosen. The candidate is
the hero with a great quirk for their class carrying the most gold already, so
a hero is finished before the next is started, and one with nothing left at
their level waits for a level-up. Worn skills and gear first, cheapest first,
under a tenth of the bank. **Flown live**: Antiquarian #749, six Guild skill
ranks and weapon + armour, every one wallet-witnessed, gold 35,085 -> 32,985
(2,100 against 3,000 planned: the owned `guild.cost` / `blacksmith.cost`
discount ranks), and the save shows the ranks.

**The Blacksmith's rank buttons are measured** (`BLACKSMITH_MEASURED`): with an
Experienced Flagellant in the slot, weapon boxes at client x 828.5 + 50 i,
y 238.5, armour y 355.0. The derived composition put them at (431, 81), the
same ~(+400, +157) residual the Guild's derived rows had. The hero slot is the
Guild's (578, 141).

**Pace: where a raid's time goes** (40 raid logs, 15.2 h). Combat is 4.1 h at
a median 7 s per decision, enemy animations included. Out of combat is 9.5 h,
and the median gap between room plans is exactly 25 s, `STUCK_SECONDS` --
the bridge idling until the heartbeat fires on a room whose save will not
move. Out-of-combat save-lag waits are only ~0.6 h (the torch waits 2 s; the
log printed 15 for every miss, now fixed). So the room heartbeat is now
`ROOM_STUCK_SECONDS` = 12 (combat keeps 25), with `ROOM_STUCK_GIVE_UP` 6 -> 12
and `LOOT_HOLD_GIVE_UP` 3 -> 6 so the wall-clock bounds stay where they were.

**A room clipped by the map's TOP edge.** rooC predicted at y 499 while the
map panel region (`screen.PANEL_Y`) starts at y 510 and the grid is drawn
from 491: the box finder never saw it and the raid stalled in rooH. Widening
the fit's own region broke four map fixtures (a wrong scale), so only the
clipped-cell fallback reads the 19-px strip (`MAP_GRID_TOP`), nearest box by
x within reach (rooC's box is split in two by its icon, and the prediction
fell in the 2 px between), with `CLIP_REACH_UNITS` 1.0 -> 1.6 (half a room is
1.33). Fixture `captures/map_top_clipped.*`.

**A map click is the fit's prediction clamped into its box, not the box's
centre.** A raid stalled in rooF: rooI and the corridor coFI came out of
`find_boxes` as ONE tall box, both resolved to its centre (850, 638) -- on
the corridor -- with the room drawn at ~(857, 657). The fit's own point is
good to a few pixels (worst 3.6 px there), so `locate` now clicks it, kept
`CLICK_MARGIN_PX` inside the box. The Old Road test that pinned the exact box
centre moved by 12 px on a 2560 frame and still lands inside the box.
Fixture `captures/map_rooF_offered_miss.*`. Also: `nurture_visit` closes
until the Hamlet is back (one close left the Guild up and an embark was lost).

**A resident holds an Abbey slot.** Every meditation since 2026-09-26 went
into the Cloister's one unlocked slot and failed; the frames all show the
same bald, spectacled portrait there with no cancel X. The town save reads
`hero 0, resident_occupied 0` for that slot (-1 on every free one): a
resident is in it. `read_activity_slots` now returns -1 for such a slot, so
it counts as taken and nothing is planned into it. (This also explains the
2026-09-27 Leper that "sat in its slot uncharged".)

**Only coach rows on screen are hired.** Two hires failed "the roster did
not grow": both dragged from below the Stage Coach's drawn list (row 7,
y 645; seven rows fit, 67 px apart from y 147, the list ends near 600) once
the coach offered 24. `COACH_ROWS_VISIBLE` = 7 bounds the offers considered;
hires only move later rows up. The list's scroll is not measured. Also
`tools/overnight.PLAY_TIMEOUT` 1800 -> 2700 s: 4 of 33 play phases were cut
mid-raid while still moving room to room.

**The Granary's price is held back while a blueprint waits.** Fran: with a
blueprint the Granary is the first building (extra food and healing). It is
a Crimson Court district: 2,500 gold + 60 bust + 1 blueprint
(`districts_districts.json`), and `persist.town.json` records
`districts.buildings.granary.built` (`town_state.read_districts`). The live
wallet holds the blueprint and 2 bust, so building upgrades may no longer
spend the busts or the 2,500 gold it needs. Buying it (the district UI) is not
built yet.


## 2026-09-27: a met objective held the party for loot nothing would collect

One Warrens raid (objective met in `rooD`) cost the first 27-minute play
allowance and three more iterations, each for a different reason that the
last fix exposed. All in `bridge/run.py`, all tested in `test_run.py`.

1. **Deferred loot behind the party.** A knife rack in `coAD`, priced
   ~185g at torch 0 and deferred there, with the loot walk already ended:
   `ahead` was empty, so no `NEXT`, and `_raid_end` said CONTINUE for 27
   minutes on a save that never moved. Now a TAKE behind the party is walked
   back to (`_step_toward`, rooms and corridors) and a stranded DEFER is
   given up -- it was a promise about the walk home, and there is none left.
2. **A walk ahead that never reaches the goal.** From the corridor the
   leftover walk said "NEXT -> rooD (for the loot)" with the chest in rooA;
   the party reversed mid-corridor. A walk that does not contain the goal's
   area is ignored.
3. **Motion without progress.** Pacing rooD <-> coAD writes the save every
   step, so no stall bound fires. `LOOT_GOAL_MAX_PLANS` (6) room plans per
   goal, then it is given up. Separately, every `LOOT_HOLD_GIVE_UP` (3) stuck
   re-plans abandon the held goal.
4. **A given-up curio as a detour.** An heirloom chest that did not answer
   three presses (the actuator gave up on it) still held the raid as
   "treasure(heirloom_chest) is +0 moves away" -- the route layer's detours
   were filtered by the loot plan's SKIPs only. The given-up set is now
   priced out too.

Result, live: `LOOT gave up knife_rack in coAD: 6 room plans without
reaching it` -> `RAID RETURN` -> the quest seal.

**A Flagellant cannot pray or meditate.** Two Abbey treatments for
Flagellant #751 (nurture, afflicted) came back "no slot in that row took the
hero", in prayer and then in meditation; the frame banked on the second
shows the panel with no confirmed occupant. His class file says why:
`activity_modifier: .override_valid_activity_ids flagellation treatment
disease_treatment` (Penance Hall and the Sanitarium only, and it heals 100).
Every activity also carries a `not_have_quirks` requirement (an Alcoholic is
not sent to pray; a Gambler not to meditate). `data/town_costs` now reads
both (`class_valid_activities`, `StressTreatment.barred_quirks`) and
`choose_treatments` matches each hero to the cheapest slot the game allows.

**The chest's order after an unequip-all is COMPUTED.** A whole party was
equipped one cell off: every drag "changed the slot" and the roster save then
showed the Crusader in protection_stone + speed_stone where the plan said
survival_guide + damage_stone. The estate save still listed the chest as it
was before the heroes' trinkets poured back in. `data/trinket_order.py`
reproduces the rarity sort from shipped data -- 20 of 20 cells on the
measured chest and 17 of 17 on the live one (with a madman-rarity trinket) --
so after a confirmed reset the cells are `rarity_sorted(chest + worn)`. The
equip pass now ends with a save check per hero ("trinkets on <class>"), since
a changed slot is not proof of the right trinket.

**The loot-goal bound, second version.** The replay oracle caught the first
one abandoning `lost_luggage` in raidsnaps3 while the ROUTE was still walking
to it. It now counts only while the loot walk steers (the route has no step
of its own) and only on a new area.

**Flown clean, 2026-09-27 (`archive/logs/overnight_profile0_p.log`).** One
town visit, every step witnessed by the save: 19 fodder dismissals, 6 hires
(three Experienced Recruits each replacing that class's worst fodder), a
Plague Doctor's meditation (95 -> 39 stress), 4 of 4 loadouts, unequip-all
plus the rarity sort, and 7 trinkets on a 4-hero party with every hero's
worn set matching the plan. The Abomination and the Duelist are skipped by
the loadout step now: they wear all seven skills, and the first flight
reported one as UNCONFIRMED because the save "wears" seven.

The same visit planned `stage_coach.upgraded_recruits.b` and the game refused
it (it needs guild.skill_levels c and blacksmith c), and the next visit did
it again. Not a torn save: `choose_upgrades` marks every openable rank as
bought while it walks the priority list, so rank b looked open behind guild
and blacksmith ranks the wallet then dropped (gold) while keeping b
(heirlooms). It now re-plans without any rank whose prerequisites the kept
purchases do not cover; on the live save it plans sanitarium.slots b instead.

**A full pack ends a loot chase.** A raid met its objective and then spent
the rest of the 30-minute play allowance walking to curios -- a decorative
urn "worth ~809 gold at torch 0", an alchemy table, a sack -- while every
pickup raised the game's "no room" modal (pack 13/16 by the save, full by
the game). `ActRunner.pack_full_seen` counts those modals and
`PACK_FULL_RETURN` (2) of them turn a loot-only CONTINUE into RETURN.
Separately, the equip planner is offered only the 21 cells the chest's grid
draws: two picks sorted to cell 24 and could not be reached (the scroll is
not measured -- the real fix).

**The embark veto asks the raid layer's own question.** A Cove party drawn
at P(finish intact) 38% and 1.2 expected deaths passed the veto's 2.0-deaths
gate; `advise_retreat` said RETREAT from the entrance room (where a retreat is
not sent) and pulled it out after the first fight -- +20 stress on all four
and the provisions spent for nothing. Two layers disagreeing about the same
party is the bug. The veto now also re-draws any comp `advise_retreat` would
retreat from at the start (`tests/bridge/test_embark_veto.py`).

**A room clipped by the map panel's edge is clickable.** A Cove raid stalled
in rooD for the whole allowance: "rooI is not drawn on the map (predicted
857,696)" on every poll, with the panel ending at y 695 and the top of rooI
drawn above it -- its predicted centre was off the panel, so no box could
contain it. Panning is ruled out (a dragged view loses the map). `locate` now
pulls a prediction at most one map unit outside the panel back inside it and
takes an edge-touching box there, clicked in the middle of what is visible.

**Stuck while looting ends the looting.** With the objective met, a raid in
rooF held for a sack, then a pack, then more, one give-up per three stuck
re-plans -- slower than the loop's own stall bound, which killed three
iterations in a row until the idle guard stopped the night. The first stuck
give-up now sets `Layers.loot_off`, and with no targets left the raid layer
answers RETURN.

The veto's retreat question credits the map's loot like the raid does:
first flight re-drew "Broken Ranks: Scatter" at 79% intact / 0.6 deaths
because, with no map at the embark, it priced the loot ahead at 0 while the
raid layer credits it. `EMBARK_LOOT_ESTIMATE` (1,000 gold, below every fresh
raid's first LOOT line tonight) stands in for it.

The equip pass read what each hero "held" from the embark plan's snapshot,
taken before the unequip-all: an Arbalest still "wore" a survival guide the
reset had taken off, got one slot planned instead of two, and the save check
flagged it. `held` now comes from the roster read after the reset.

**A Rubble panel whose X merged with the rubble.** A raid pressed W at the
far end of coEI for a whole iteration with the obstacle panel open (frame:
`captures/curio_rubble_merged_x.png`, two shovels in the pack). The save
did record the obstacle; `curiopanel.find` did not see the panel: the X's
133 red pixels shared their rows with red rubble and joined a 347-px cluster
the button-size filter rejected. When no button survives, the red pixels are
now re-clustered with a 4-px gap (`TIGHT_GAP_PX`), which reads hand, shovel
and X exactly on that frame and changes nothing on the other fixtures. Verified live the same
morning: "coEI is blocked and the panel is up - clear the obstacle with a
shovel", then "walked into rooI".

**A retreat with the quest-complete banner up answers the banner.** The
survival layer said LEAVE in rooG (one fight left, P(finish intact) 0%) while
`Quest Complete!` was on screen; the leave path clicked the seal UNDER the
modal, nothing moved, and its `raid-left` guard then made every later poll
return silently until the iteration was killed. With `quest_complete` on
screen the retreat path now calls `_end_raid` with RETURN.

A Leper's meditation (the visit's first treatment) sat in the Cloister slot
uncharged while the same geometry confirmed an Occultist's prayer seconds
later; no barred quirk, no class override. Cause not shown; the guard is a
second confirm click with its own wallet watch (`TREAT_CONFIRM_TRIES`).

Still open: food planned on a 2/16-HP Houndmaster every room ("the food was
not eaten") -- the game may not allow eating outside a hunger check or camp.

## 2026-09-27: the Hamlet by ROLE -- bands, fodder, Experienced Recruits, loadouts, the chest's own buttons

Fran's spec of 2026-09-26 (docs/NEXT.md, "the Hamlet, nuanced"), built in
its order. Evidence first, then what changed.

**The hero model is `bridge/hero_roles.py`.** Band (0-2 / 3-4 / 5+), role
(invested / nurture / fodder), sunk gold (via `data/hero_value.party_value`)
and a nurture score. The score is the quirk tier weighted by how often THAT
class's curated comps list the quirk (1,364 of 1,908 curated hero slots carry
positive quirks; the comps spell them as display names, "Corvids Eye", so they
are keyed the way the save spells them). On the live roster (126 heroes):
106 fodder, 12 invested, 8 nurture; nurture top-3 Runaway#835 (Evasive,
Weald Explorer), Plague Doctor#884 (Tough, Quick Reflexes), Jester#1127
(Luminous).

**"Invested" is gold or a raid-earned band, never level 1 alone.** The first
cut made every level-1 hero invested, and then Fran's worked case answered
itself wrongly: Musketeer Mortmain (#794) is level 1 on XP with **0 gold**
sunk, masochistic, and her Eagle Eye scores 2.0 for a Musketeer. Her bill:
14,111g to buy (3,000 unlocks + 2,500 ranks + 1,111 stress + 7,500 lock)
against nothing invested -- she "has done her role". So invested is
`sunk >= 2,000` or `level >= 3` or gear bought; the save's own
`weapon_rank`/`armour_rank` is the witness without the upgrades file (all ten
rank-1 heroes on the live roster are the Experienced Recruits, 6,250g each).

**Changed:**

- Roster caps are per class and BAND, 4/4/5, and the 0-2 cap drops by one per
  full level of roster mean above 2, floor 1 (a declared convention). Only
  FODDER is dismissed for the cap.
- Treatments: fodder never, even on a heavy bank (this supersedes the same
  day's "S-tier quirk or heavy gold" exception); invested first, then nurture
  by score; an afflicted invested/nurture hero is treated below the stress
  threshold, since the town is the only place an affliction comes off. The
  10% cap stays.
- Skill unlocks: never for fodder (was: never for level 0).
- The coach hires Experienced Recruits before any level-0 offer; the comp
  shape search ranks within each group.
- The comp draw's weight is multiplied by `party_weight`: invested 2.0,
  nurture 1.5, fodder 1.0, times rest (1 - stress/200); a party spanning two
  bands x0.25, a second nurture hero x0.25. The veto still gates.
- **Loadouts before the embark** (`town.select_loadouts`): the hero sheet's
  Combat Skills row is seven icons in the Guild's order at client
  x 659 + 53.3 i, y 193; a click toggles one, and the save takes it on the
  sheet's close (Man-at-Arms#1148 crush -> rampart, witnessed).
- **The Trinket Inventory's header buttons**, by tooltip: Unequip all
  (891,112, then a Yes modal on `MODAL_OK`), sort by class restriction (920),
  sort by rarity (948), sort by name (976). **A sort rewrites the estate
  save's order on the close**, so the save keys are the drawn order
  afterwards. The rarity sort is the rarities file's order, then the ENGLISH
  DISPLAY NAME (not the id): "Agility Talon, Archer's Ring, Diseased Herb,
  Heartburst Hood, ..." then the very_commons -- 20 of 20 cells matched.
  `town.reset_trinkets` runs before every equip; the benched-strip pass is
  the fallback when it is unconfirmed.
- **The trinket planner was blind to every class trinket.** Owned ids went to
  the team-builder as "flag 5", "rw warm scarf", "agile talon", which match no
  name it knows; `trinketplan` now sends the game's display names. On the
  live chest a party's picks went 5-7 to 7-8, and the Heartburst Hood, Warm
  Scarf, Agility Talon and Virtuous Chalice are now chosen.
- **The coach hires by QUALITY** (Fran, 2026-09-27: *"take into account the
  new hero quality to choose who to add to the roster and who to not,
  specially with the limited 4 class threshold slots"*). A recruit is valued
  by the roster's own model -- `(role, nurture score)` off its level, gear and
  quirks, which the coach's save record carries. Best first; with room in its
  class band it is hired, and with the band FULL it is hired only if it beats
  the band's worst free fodder (a higher role, or the same role and +2.0
  quirk score), which is then dismissed for it (`Hire.replaces`). On the live
  save the three gold offers were plain level-0 fodder for full bands, and
  none was hired.

## 2026-09-26: Abbey treatments -- the confirm, the owned ranks, occupancy, and the Guild's rules

**Why nothing was ever treated.** Measured live: dropping a hero on an
activity slot only STAGES the treatment. The price and a check-mark button
appear under the slot, and gold is charged only when that button is clicked
(14,315 -> 13,315 on the click). `treat_heroes` now clicks it
(`ABBEY_CONFIRM_DY`) and witnesses the wallet drop. The roster's
`building_name` lags until the panel closes, but `persist.town.json` records
the slot at once (`buildings.abbey.activities.meditation.0.hero = 699`), and
`TownState.activity_slots` now reads it.

**Only the unlocked slots.** Fran: *"treatment is trying to use abbey slots
that we havent unlocked ... right now we only can use the leftmost box"*.
Each Abbey tree is three ladders, per the building file's
`upgrade_requirement_code`: heal a/d, price b/e, slots c/f. With only rank
`a` owned, that is one slot, the base price, and the rank-a heal.
`StressTreatment` now carries the codes, and `choose_treatments(owned=)`
prices, heals and counts slots from the ranks actually bought. Occupied
slots come off the free ones, and anyone already in a slot is skipped.

**The Guild's rules.** Fran: *"a lvl 0 hero is not worth recovering unless
extremely good trait or heavy gold on bank ... otherwise its just a money
waste"*.

- A level-0 hero is treated only with an S-tier quirk, or with at least
  `HEAVY_GOLD` (30,000, a declared convention) in the bank.
- Treatments never take more than `TREAT_GOLD_FRACTION` (10%) of the gold.
- Live plan at 13,315 gold: one treatment, the level-1 Abomination
  (1,250), where it had been three.
- The lab put a level-0 Duelist in the Cloister for 1,000 before this rule
  landed. That is the waste the rule prevents.

Tests: two new in `test_town_treatment.py`; two budget tests scaled to the
cap; one wallet set under `HEAVY_GOLD`.

## 2026-09-26: the hallway door opens for the front two heroes

## 2026-09-26: items in a fight are FREE actions, and the bridge uses them

**Measured live in a Warrens fight.** On the Plague Doctor's own turn (round 2),
a right-click on holy water used it on him:

- The count went 2 -> 1, and the four resist icons came up over his portrait.
- **The turn did not end**: still round 2, still his turn, skills available.

That overturns `IN_COMBAT_PROVISIONS_ALLOWED = False`, which rested on the
localisation string `cant_use_items_in_battle`, not on any rule.

**The bridge:** `provision_use.battle_item` picks at most one item for the
ACTING hero, before the policy's skill:

- holy water when `dot_threat` on their rank reaches `HOLY_WATER_THREAT`
  and they are not already flasked;
- otherwise dog treats, for the Houndmaster only, when +50% turns the
  planned hit (engine `dmg_min`/`dmg_max` x (1 + skill `dmg_mod`)) into a
  kill.

Only the surplus above the curio reserve (`run._item_reserve`, from the room
plan) is spent. `run.py` carries it on `Update.battle_item`, never on a
look-ahead, and `act._use_battle_item` right-clicks it once per turn key,
after which the skill plan runs unchanged. The skill stays the policy's.

**The simulator is NOT changed.** Turning the flag on (and making item use
free there) changes the training world, so it is staged for the next retrain
in NEXT.

Also: `abbey_panel` banked (the Abbey was refused as "could not confirm", so
three planned treatments never happened); the blacksmith was refused the same
way and still needs banking. Horror's save `stat_type` remains unmapped: the
only unmapped hero buff in the captures (`stat_type` 1, 41 sightings) occurs
in 31 snapshots and in none of them is a Horror-capable enemy present.

Tests: two for `battle_item` in `test_provision_use.py`.

## 2026-09-26: the Hamlet roster -- a witnessed fast scroll, and at most 4 per class and level

Town visits had reached ~40 minutes on a 102-hero roster. Every roster
lookup (Guild drag, trinket strip/equip, seating) rewound the list and then
clicked the chevron row by row, waiting `TOWN_PANEL_SETTLE` plus
`ROSTER_SCROLL_SETTLE`, 1.8 s a row.

**Measured live in the Hamlet:**

- One chevron click scrolls a row in **~0.58 s**; sampling the strip every
  30 ms shows it still from 0.58 s on.
- Clicks sent 0.1 s apart **queue**: 5 clicks moved exactly 5 rows in 2.0 s,
  and 4 at 0.3 s moved exactly 4.

**`_rows_moved`** reads how many rows a scroll moved, identity-free. A
one-row scroll shifts every drawn row up one pitch, so rows 1-7 before
reappear as rows 0-6 after. Mid-animation it reads None, and settled it
reads exactly 1 (live).

- **`scroll_roster_by`** sends bursts of up to 6 clicks 0.1 s apart, then
  must read exactly that many rows moved. A short burst is topped up; an
  unreadable or over-long one refuses.
- **`force_roster_to_top`** sends bursts of 6 up-clicks until one moves
  nothing.
- Live: 40 rows down in **11.5 s** (was ~72 s), a rewind from 40 rows in
  **13.5 s**, from the top in 1.8 s. Checked by identity: after
  rewind + 40, a right-click on screen row 0 opened save row 40's sheet
  (Valance, level-0 Runaway). Opening a sheet scrolls the list up one row;
  the roster cursor's strip check already sends that back to the rewind.

**Fran's cap: at most 4 heroes of one class at one level**
(`townplan.MAX_PER_CLASS_LEVEL`).

- The coach skips an offer that would be a fifth; where offers compete for
  the room left, the better recruit is kept.
- `cap_dismissals` names the surplus, worst first (no S/A/B quirk, then no
  trinket, then the most stressed). It never names a hero in the party, in a
  building, or kept back.
- `town.dismiss_surplus` executes it at the start of the coach visit and
  reaches ANY row, unlike `dismiss_heroes`, which stops at the first page.
  The steps are right-click -> Dismiss -> Yes, and the save must drop
  exactly that guid or the pass stops. At most 12 per visit.
- Live: 102 heroes, 7 groups over the cap, 12 surplus. The first dismissal
  was flown by hand, duelist#570 at save row 14, confirmed by the save in
  19.8 s.

Tests: the cap (3) in `test_townplan.py`;
`test_the_rows_a_scroll_moved_are_read_off_the_strip`.

A Ruins entrance with three rooms drawn: the party sat in rooL for two

  held the correct fit at 5/7.

## 2026-09-25: provisions are used on triggers

Fran: *"we shouldn't use any provision randomly, each is a trigger of
something"*. `bridge/provision_use.py` holds the decisions, pure and tested:

- **food**: a hero under 30% HP eats until back over it or full.
  `rules.json`: `provision_hp_heal` 0.05 and `max_provisions_before_full` 4,
  which is Fran's "full after 4".
- **bandage / antivenom / laudanum**: bleed / blight / Horror only.
- **holy_water** (+33% blight/bleed/disease/debuff resist, 3 rounds): in a
  fight, when `dot_threat` (shipped enemy skills that can reach the hero's
  rank, bleed/blight dose x chance, disease weighted) reaches
  `HOLY_WATER_THREAT`.
- **dog_treats** (+15% ACC, +50% damage, 3 rounds; Houndmaster only): when the
  treat turns the planned hit into a kill.
- **The curio/combat middle path is a reserve**: what `lootplan` earmarked for
  the curios still ahead is never spent.

**Axiom found:** `IN_COMBAT_PROVISIONS_ALLOWED = False` rested on
`cant_use_items_in_battle`, and that key exists only as a localisation STRING
("Can't use items during battle"), not as a rule. Dog treats' own effect is
round-based. The live lab is settling whether in-combat use works and whether
it costs the turn; the simulator keeps the flag until it has, since changing
it changes the training world.

**Out of combat it is wired end to end.** Measured live: click the hero's
sprite, then right-click the pack cell. Food went 12 -> 11 and the
Musketeer 10 -> 12 HP; at full HP the game refused the portion and spent
nothing. `run.py` puts the uses on `RoomAdvice.provisions`, and
`act._use_provisions` swaps the panel to the pack if the map is up, sends
each use ONCE, and witnesses it by the pack count. Food is capped per hero
at 4, and a portion the pack did not lose marks that hero full. Holy water
and dog treats (the in-fight half) wait for the combat lab.

Open: Horror's save `stat_type` is unmapped, so laudanum reads False and is
never spent on a guess.

Tests: `tests/bridge/test_provision_use.py` (7) and two in `test_act.py`.

## 2026-09-25 (Fran's rulings): cheaper comps, no skills for level 0, Experienced Recruits

Fran, on the night's gold drain (17,265 -> 1,185, with 4-5k of Guild skills
bought per embark):

- *"Let's include the draw among those or make them weight more in the
  draw"*: the comp flip is now `rng.choices` weighted `1 / (1 + unlocks)^2`.
  In a test with one free comp and three needing four unlocks each, the free
  one is drawn >70% of the time; unweighted it would be 25%. It stays a flip:
  the dear ones still come up.

  unlocks and no bill for a resolve-0 hero, so they go with the skills they

  tests keep testing costing.
- *"Never spend over 10% of the current money on upgrades like those"*:
  `UNLOCK_GOLD_FRACTION = 0.10` caps the skill budget at a tenth of the gold
  on hand. A comp over the cap takes the existing part-equipped path
  (unlocks dropped from the back rank first).
- *"Try to add experienced recruits"*: it was already on the list, but in
  front of its prerequisites. Shipped data gates `upgraded_recruits.a` on
  rank **b** of `guild.skill_levels`, `blacksmith.weapon` and
  `blacksmith.armour` (each later rank needs one more of each), so it was
  checked while they were unowned and skipped every visit. It now follows
  them in `UPGRADE_PRIORITY`, and a rich visit buys the chain and then the
  coach rank.

## 2026-09-25: the survival forecast stopped flipping on noise

Live, a Weald raid with the party on 12/10/9/13 HP. The forecast went 8% ->
58% -> 0% -> 17% over a few minutes, and in one ectoplasm fight the verdict
flipped RETREAT / "worth pushing on" between consecutive turns with nothing
changed. Two causes:

- **`fights_ahead` read the live save raw.** When the game was mid-write, the
  parse raised and the bare `except` returned `[]`, so the forecast silently
  fell back to sampling the zone pool. Reproduced by hand: one call returned
  `[]`, and the next returned the 4 real groups. It now reads a `_stage`d,
  validated copy, like every other live reader.
- **12 episodes is ~±14 points.** `overnight.py --survival` now defaults to
  48. On the same party, 64 episodes took 3 s (cached per state).

Measured on that live party, known groups against sampled: P(finish intact)
8% against 0%, expected deaths 2.0 against 2.7. The known groups are less
pessimistic here (two of the four are ectoplasm packs), and the dire reading
was the party's HP, not an artefact.

skipped as "that building's upgrade panel is not measured yet". The gate was
added after 2026-09-24, when those ranks were clicked at points inside the
COACH panel. The diagnosis in that gate was right about the panel and wrong

  from the one `building_base_layout` that `townlayout.upgrade_node` derives

  panel, clicks the crest to draw the trees, and buys each rank against the
  wallet.
- **The first unconfirmed rank stops that building.** A click that bought

## 2026-09-25: a corridor stopped hiding the loot walk from itself

Live, iteration 1 of the night: the quest was done and an iron maiden
(~266 gold) waited past coFG. The party walked rooF -> coFG -> rooF for four
laps. In a corridor, `plan_route` plans both exits. With no targets left, both
cost zero and the first one won, so `next_step` named a room and was never
empty. The loot walk in `run.py` takes over ONLY on an empty step, so it lost
every corridor, and from rooF it sent the party straight back in.

The survival forecast used to draw each remaining fight from the zone's comp
table. The map says exactly which group waits on each battle tile: every tile
in `persist.map.json` carries `mash_index` and `mash_type` (0 = hall,
1 = room), which index the groups of `<zone>.<tier>.mash.darkest` in file
order. That matched 10 of 10 fights in the captured raids.

- `bridge/fights_ahead.py` reads the remaining battle tiles and orders them by
  map distance from the party.
- The env takes `options["enemy_group_script"]`. `_grouped_enemy` fights those
  groups in order, then falls back to sampling once the list runs out or when
  an id is unknown to the store.
- `rollout.evaluate(enemy_groups=)` passes the list through, and
  `Advisor.survival_note` supplies it from the raid file beside the map.
- The order past the next fight is a guess: the route decides it, and distance
  is only a proxy. The set of groups is exact.
- Tests: `tests/bridge/test_fights_ahead.py`.

## Night of 2026-09-25: trinkets, the Guild flow, and the hunger rate

**Trinkets are planned by Fran's team-builder** (`bridge/trinketplan.py`,
`tools/teambuilder_reequip.js`). Fran: *"No se equipa ningun tipo de trinket
... puedes consultar el apartado suggest del repo dd-team-builder"*. Its
`reequipParty` (valued by `trinketValue.js` / `heroNeeds.js`, best-in-slot from
`bisIndex.js`) is called through the builder's own ESM loader, with game ids
mapped to its names by its own `nameKey`. It is not ported, so it cannot drift.
On the live estate's six trinkets it gives Snake Oil and Speed Stone to a
Leper and Protection Stone to a Crusader, with reasons. No Node or no checkout
means no picks. The EQUIP clicks are not built yet (they need the trinket UI
measured live).

**The Guild is visited from the Hamlet.** The visit ran after seating, with
the estate map still open, where no building can be clicked, so every planned
skill unlock failed as "could not confirm the panel opened" (the banked frame
was the estate map). The plan is a save read, so it now runs first, the Guild
second, and the estate map third. `_put_hero_in_guild` finds the hero by the
save's order (the list IS the file order) with a rewind and a scroll,
replacing the mod-broken portrait matcher.

**Hunger comes from the game's own map budgets.** The simulator's
`HUNGER_CHECK_CHANCE = 0.6` per completed fight was a declared proxy whose
stated falsifier was a real count. `scripts/map_generator.darkest` gives it:
0.36 / 0.40 / 0.26 checks per fight for short / medium / long
(`data/map_generator.hunger_per_fight`). The simulator was starving parties up
to 2.3x too often, which also biased the live survival forecast towards
deaths. **A simulator change:** the shipped checkpoint trained under 0.6; its
observation width is unchanged, so it still loads, but the world it is scored
in is now truer.

## Two raids reviewed: Ruins and Warrens (2026-09-24)

Fran asked for the two raids of the afternoon to be analysed and the errors
fixed. Signals pooled from all four log files, since restarts split them:
63 curio-failure lines, 32 torch lines (16 snuffs that never took), 17 DIFFERs,
12 map-fallback lines, 9 inputs that did not land, 11 stalls, 1 death and 1
retreat.

Fixed:
- **False DIFFERs (7): a self-cast heal read as an attack.** `advised_kind`
  only called a skill a heal when `_is_ally_skill` said so, and the
  Abomination's Absolution targets the caster. Every one of the seven turns
  was played exactly as advised. A negative target (self-cast) is now
  classed like an ally skill.
- **The paid torch shutdown (16 snuffs).** "4 torches buys +30% gold" is no
  longer advised: PLAYBOOK 7.2 says max light while torches allow. Only the
  FREE shutdowns remain (the run's last fight, no fight left, a camp next).
  And a snuff that fails once is not re-sent that raid (`_snuff_failed`),
  since the gesture itself is still unfound.
- **Obstacle panels met on the way to a curio** (the Warrens rubble), from
  the entry above.

Found and NOT a bug: the "key advised, spent nothing" DIFFER on the heirloom
chest. The key was right-clicked, the loot taken and the chest used up; the
ledger's pack diff is confounded by the loot pickup in the same step.

Open, needing live work (NEXT.md): the corridor curio approach (37 attempts,
26 "no panel after W", no clean landing seen), and `rooK` sitting at the
map panel's bottom edge, where the fit cannot place it and the save-ordered
fallback took two tries.

## Rubble cleared bare-handed with shovels in the pack (2026-09-24)

Fran: *"the model has just avoided using a shovel even if it has it on the
inventory for a pile in the warrens"*. Walking to a sconce in `coDG`, the
party met the rubble first. Its panel came up inside the CURIO path, which
clicked the hand. The obstacle handler, which picks the shovel, never saw
it. The evidence is in the torch: 100 -> 56 over one corridor, four new tiles
(24) plus the bare-handed obstacle's `torchlight -20`. The save forgets a
cleared obstacle, so the map shows nothing there now.

`_work_curio_panel` now hands the panel to `_work_obstacle_panel` when the
target curio takes no provision (so its own panel never lights an item
slot), the panel shows a lit slot, and the corridor still holds an uncleared
obstacle (tile content 4). Curios that take items are not second-guessed.

## The game SAVES the roster in its displayed order (2026-09-24)

The 2026-09-23 prediction (click Stress, predict a stable ascending sort)
missed live: probes read row 0 = 578 and row 7 = 575, and the save explained
why. `persist.roster.json` had been REWRITTEN most-stressed first (578 at 95,
606 at 95, ... 575 at 4), which is the order the list was showing. So:

* with no sort click since the last save, **the list is the file order**;
* a click re-orders the display at once, and the file follows at the next save.

`sort_roster_low_stress_first` now tries the file order first, with no click
and two probes, and only then clicks Stress and tries the stable sorts of the
file order in both directions. The flip used to compare against the reversed
ascending list, which puts ties the wrong way round. A miss still falls back
to the walk, and every seat is still checked by identity.

**Also, and important:** `visit_stage_coach` sent EVERY planned purchase from
inside the coach panel, including the guild and sanitarium ranks PLAYBOOK 7.4
now plans. Live, four such clicks came back "the wallet did not move", so
nothing was bought by mistake. Only the coach's own trees are sent now; the
rest are reported as "panel not measured" until each building's upgrade panel
is measured live.

## The "pending repositioner" gate is the same late save (2026-09-24)

The screen-over-save override (2026-09-23) cleared `provisional` and
`enemies_first` but not `movers`. Live: "the game is asking the occultist now
- not waiting for the save", then "REFUSE: a pending enemy can reposition
(Bone Arbalist)", and four 25 s stalls. If the game is asking this hero, the
enemies ahead of them have acted, so the override now clears `movers` too.
Still only when the bar is waiting AND the hero panel names the advised hero.

## Light costs 6 per NEW tile at any light, not 1 while radiant (2026-09-24)

`rules.json` `tile_light_loss` is keyed `clear` 1 / `dim` 6 / `dark` 6, and
`data/loot_loader.walk_costs` read the keys as light levels: 1 per tile above
75, 6 below. The live logs say they are TILE states. Across 40 clean moves,
a new corridor tile at torch 100 cost 6 (100 -> 94 again and again), and a
re-walked corridor about 1-2. The loot planner under-priced every lit walk
six-fold. Now:

- `walk_costs`: `light_loss` = 6 for a new tile at any light,
  `revisit_light_loss` = 1 for a cleared one.
- `lootplan` charges a corridor tile by tile as it is walked (a fight at
  tile 2 is fought after three tiles, not the whole corridor), at the revisit
  rate when the corridor was walked before, and **relights before a fight**
  too, not only on arrival. Otherwise a corridor that starts bright goes dark
  before its tile-4 fight while torches sit in the pack (PLAYBOOK 7.2).
- `provision_needs` now derives TORCHES as well: every generated corridor
  walked once at max light, `base_corridor_number x 4 walkable tiles x 6 / 25`
  = 10 / 15 / 20 for short / medium / long, against the table's 8 / 12-13 / 16.

Fran's Old Road route (`test_the_optimiser_reproduces_the_route_FRAN_described`)
still comes out step for step, with battle 1 lit. A long captured map is
now honestly unlightable on 8 torches and lit on 24. The bridge oracle was
re-emitted: the loot and light lines moved, the line counts did not.

## A speech bubble over the coach skipped the whole town visit (2026-09-23, late)

Fran: *"after beating the dungeon it went straight to next dungeon without
doing nothing in the hamlet"*. The coach DID open, but the coachman's speech
bubble ("Let's do") and a new recruit list moved the frame away from the
banked `stage_coach_panel`, so `_panel_is_open` said no, the visit reported
"could not confirm the panel opened", and it went straight to the embark with
12,895 gold unspent. `screen.panel_title_match` now also accepts the panel's
TITLE PLATE (name and icon), which is 0.027 from the banked coach and 0.41+
from every non-panel screen. It is still positive evidence of a banked panel.
Fixture: `captures/coach_panel_speech_bubble.png`.

## The screen says the turn is due, whatever the save says (2026-09-23, late)

Fran: *"now its stuck again i think"*. It wasn't stuck, it was slow: every hero
turn arrived "provisional" (the save still had enemy turns to resolve) while
the game was already asking that hero with the bar lit, so each turn waited
out a 25 s stall before acting. `ActRunner` now drops the timing gates when
the bar is waiting AND the hero panel names the advised hero. The heropanel
check is what keeps this safe against the 2% of turns where the save names
the wrong hero. Live: turns every 5-6 s, zero stalls in the next iteration.
The map-click stillness fix from the entry below was confirmed the same way:
the party left the Ruins entrance room and walked on.

## A map click aimed at a view that had since moved (2026-09-23, late)

Stuck in a Ruins entrance room: the click for `rooE` landed on empty grid.
The fitted frame had `rooA` at client (786, 548); when the click landed the
panel had eased to put it at (855, 587). The re-fit loop kept the
BEST-scoring sample, which could be one from before the view settled. It now
stops at the first two consecutive fits that agree within
`MAP_POINT_TOLERANCE` (a still view) and takes that one. `MAP_REFIT_TRIES`
6 -> 12, which costs nothing on a map that is already still.

**Provisions in combat:** not a bug. The game forbids it
(`cant_use_items_in_battle`, see `engine/_constants.py`). What the bridge
lacks is the between-fights use of bandages and antivenom on a bleeding or
blighted hero. The simulator has it (`_use_provisions_between_combats`); the
live inventory click needs measuring. Queued in NEXT.

## The roster list IS the save's order: predict it, check two rows (2026-09-23, late)

Fran asked for a way to avoid *"pushing into comp for every member of the
roster"* by looking in the save files. The sort button is not saved (not in
the profile, not in `global.json`). What IS there: 17 rows learned by probing
on 2026-09-23 were `persist.roster.json` file indexes 11-27, consecutive and
in order (all at 0 stress, so under the stress sort they were all ties). File
indexes 0-10 were the eleven dead heroes (`roster.status` 3), which the list
does not draw. So the list is **the living heroes in file order, stably sorted
by the active button**. The 2026-09-22 note "list order is not the save order"
was taken under the Class sort.

`town.predicted_order` computes it (read_town_state keeps file order and drops
the dead); `sort_roster_low_stress_first` now probes only rows 0 and 7 to
confirm it, flips on a match with the reversed order, and otherwise returns
nothing so the old walk runs. It returns every hero's (clicks, row), so no
walk is needed on a match. Every first-page seat is now checked against the
save by IDENTITY ("a different hero arrived"), not just by the bar filling.

## The quest reward was counted twice, and a lone survivor walked back in (2026-09-23, late)

`advise_retreat` charged leaving the FULL quest reward and credited staying
with `completion_rate x reward`: the same reward on both sides. Down to one
Vestal after two deaths, it read "leaving ~3,000, staying ~3,000 -- worth
pushing on", and the route kept re-entering the fight. Fran: *"now its a good
opportunity to retreat, but keeps looping into the combat"*. Leaving now
costs 0; staying's bill carries the reward and the loot at their odds against
the deaths. Live on the same raid: "leaving ~0, staying ~2,750 -- RETREAT",
abandoned from the room at once. A sound party (1.4 deaths, 60% completion)
still pushes on.

## Provisions from the game's own data; the pause stops the overnight clocks (2026-09-23, late)

**`bridge/provision_needs.py`** (PLAYBOOK 7.7) derives a quest's basket from
shipped files: `map_generator.darkest` (the ranges of hallway obstacles,
hunger checks and curios per zone, quest type and length), the zone's
`props.darkest` (curio spawn weights) and the curio sheet (whether each
provision beats bare hands on each curio). Every count is taken at the TOP of
its range: "go over than under". `provisions.basket(derived=...)` uses it item
by item, and torches stay on the table until the light drain per tile is
derived.

**Result:** the derived baskets mostly AGREE with the community table. Food is
identical (12 / 20 / 24 = up to 3 / 5 / 6 hunger checks x 4 heroes); medium
and long Cove and Weald need MORE shovels (6-8 against 5-6); several zones need
fewer bandages, antivenoms, holy waters and keys. The table was not the cause
of the empty wallet. That was the failing raids, as Fran said.

**The overnight clocks stop while the help card is open**
(`Overnight._wait_while_paused`, checked every 5 s): the stall timer, the
deadline and "the dungeon is not responding" all wait.

**Combat retreat's fail chance**, audited: the engine models it
(`combat_retreat_chance` 0.70, +0.05 per attempt), and the model trained with
it. In the gold decision a success probability scales both bills alike, so
the verdict cannot move. What a failed attempt costs is a lost turn inside a
fight, which is the combat model's to weigh (the dungeon layer overrules a
fight only at `FIGHT_RETREAT_WIPE`). No change to the decision.

**`tools/forecast_vs_game.py`**: the live forecast against real raids, per
fight reached, filtered by `--since` so only valid raids count.

## Fran's rulings from the fact audit, applied (2026-09-23, night)

Recorded verbatim as PLAYBOOK section 7. In code:
- **Treatment** (7.3): `TREAT_ABOVE` 50 -> 70, and only a hero worth keeping
  (resolve >= 1 or an S/A/B quirk). Heroes recover stress passively, and a
  quirkless level-0 hero is replaced, not treated.
- **Buildings** (7.4): `UPGRADE_PRIORITY` is now coach (recruits, upgraded
  recruits, roster size) -> guild and blacksmith -> sanitarium -> survivalist
  -> abbey. `NEVER_BUILDINGS = {"tavern"}`: never upgraded and never used for
  treatment (a hero can lose trinkets there). Open with Fran: whether "the
  abbey, tavern should never be used" meant the abbey too.
- **Curio quirks** (7.5): scored by PizzaRat's tiers (`data/quirk_tiers.py`,
  positive S-F and negative priorities 1-10, mapped to game ids). A random
  positive is now worth +0.45 and a random negative -0.53, where both were
  +-1. Dismissal reads the same table.
- **Heirlooms** (7.8): never sold. The exchange stays off on the modded install.
- **Torch** (7.2): already relights below 76 whenever a torch is held, and only
  goes dark on the last killing blow or before a camp. No change.
- **Help-card pause**: the backend holds only against a REAL window handle.
  Tests pass stand-ins, and grabbing one hung a suite run.

Retracted (see `docs/findings/fact-audit.md`): "one fight per raid" and the
economy reading. Both came from sessions that retreated on sight or looped at
the first fight. Fran's counterexample shows the forecast IS miscalibrated.

## Inside a fight, only a near-certain wipe overrules the combat model (2026-09-23)

Fran: *"i want the models separated, the combat model has been trained on its
own to achieve the possible best combat results"*, and let the dungeon layer
retreat a fight only *"if the probability exceeds a really high threshold of
wiping"*. The battle path now withholds the survival layer's RETREAT unless the
last rollout's `wipe_rate` is at least `run.FIGHT_RETREAT_WIPE` (0.8).
`Advisor.last_wipe_rate` carries it. Between fights the verdict still decides
whether to stay in the dungeon or leave.

## "The model has been retreating all the time": stress was priced in gold (2026-09-23, evening)

A level-0 party (0 gold sunk) retreated from every fight of a raid. With deaths
worth nothing, what drove it was stress: the Abbey's 22 gold a point over about
90 more stress x 4 heroes read as ~8,000 gold for staying, against 4,778 for
leaving. Fran's rulings, in order: *"stress dont matter unless its the end of
the raid"*, *"each retreat gives 20 stress to the whole team"*, and finally
*"the only thing that matters is the survival percent ... we should manage an
average between, survives and gathers most loot possible"*, with *"we
shouldn't guide the model towards losing a hero"*.

`advise_retreat` now:
- bills **no stress in gold** on either side; the rollout already carries
  stress's real effect in `mean_deaths`, and the fields stay at 0;
- prices a death at `max(sunk, reward / living)`, so a free recruit is never
  worth nothing;
- counts the loot still on the map for staying (`loot_ahead_gold`, now wired
  from `run.Session` through `Advisor.loot_ahead_gold`);
- keeps the scaled death floor (`SCALE_FLOOR_BY_INVESTMENT` True).
Four tests that pinned the stress-in-gold arithmetic were replaced by tests
of the rule. The same live party reprices from RETREAT to push on.

- **Dismiss:** a RIGHT-click on a roster row opens the hero tab (a left click
  starts a drag). Rename / **Dismiss** / Switch Outfit sit at client x 119,

## Dismissal and the stress sort, measured and flown (2026-09-23, evening)

**Dismissal.** A RIGHT-click on a roster row opens the hero's tab; a left
click only starts a drag. Down the tab's top-left edge: Rename (y 110),
Dismiss (y 133), Switch Outfit (y 157), then "This will dismiss the hero
permanently" with Yes on `MODAL_OK`. The guid leaves `persist.roster.json`
within a second. `town.dismiss_heroes` identifies each row with the
save-witnessed drag probe immediately before opening it, re-learns after every
dismissal (the list closes up), and stops if the save drops anyone other than
the target. Flown: Danneville (453) by hand, then Basnage (315) by the
function, each confirmed by the save. `townplan.reset_dismissals` is Fran's
rebuild rule (level 0, over 50 stress). It is not wired in; whether to run it
is his call.

**Sort buttons.** Level / Stress / Class / Activity above the list, at client
x 1143 / 1168 / 1194 / 1219, y 64, found by their tooltips. A second click
flips the order, the order is not saved, and a seated hero stays pinned on
top. `sort_roster_low_stress_first` clicks Stress and reads the direction off
the learned first page (flipping if it came up most-stressed first). The seat
loop now uses it before any whole-list walk. Flown: flipped once, "every
planned hero is on the first page", 4 of 4, embarked.

**Also wired:** the room's loot estimate (`LOOT ... still on the map`) now
reaches `advise_retreat` through `Advisor.loot_ahead_gold`.

## Fran's rulings on retreat and recruiting (2026-09-23)

- `retreat.SCALE_FLOOR_BY_INVESTMENT` is now True. It shipped off on
  2026-09-03 as contested; Fran ruled that level-0 heroes are expendable and
  that a broke campaign prefers deaths to retreats. A party worth less than
  the quest no longer gets the 50% death floor, and the gold decides.

## Dead heroes were planned into parties all night (2026-09-23)

`persist.roster.json` keeps a hero who died, with `roster.status` 3 and
`current_hp` -1.0. `town_state._read_roster` read all 43 records as living,
so comps kept naming an occultist and an antiquarian who had died. The
estate map never draws them, so the (now honest) seat loop answered "not
found in the learned roster list" and every embark of the run refused 2 of
4. The status is now filtered: 35 heroes, exactly the 35 the list drew.
Fixture: `archive/traces/roster_dead_20260923/`.

## A busy hero at row 7 shifted every later roster position (2026-09-23)

`learn_roster_positions` counted a scroll only when the row-7 probe named a
new hero. A hero in a building can't be dragged, so the probe learned
nothing and the scroll was counted as swallowed, although the list HAD moved.
Every later position came out one short, and three busy heroes in a row ended
the pass with an occultist and an antiquarian never learned. The seat loop
then fell back to a positional guess, sent two heroes to row 6, and the
embark refused 3 of 4. The pass now decides "moved" from the roster strip
(`_bar_moved`, as `force_roster_to_top` already did); a probe that learns
nothing is skipped without costing a position. And once the list has been
learned, a hero missing from it is refused, never guessed.

Same night, the results page of a COMPLETED quest (3,000-gold reward, a gem
tooltip up) went unrecognised. `screen.results_page_is_up` matches the crest
header (Escape/Defeat/Victory, all banked) AND the results "Next" strip. The
strip alone is 0.16 from the Hamlet's Embark button, and the header is what
rules the Hamlet out. It is a `recognise` fallback, like the progression strip.

## A hero marked "already played" was skipped forever (2026-09-23)

An Abomination's MOVE was counted as played with the bar unchanged; the game
sat in MOVE target-select waiting on him, and each round-robin of the stall
recovery skipped him as "already played this turn" for the rest of the
30-minute allowance. After a full rotation, when enemy bars are still drawn,
`_answer_phantom_fight` now clears `acted`, `_continued` and `_deferred`, so
the next pass really retries every hero. Clearing `_continued` with `acted`
is required by `test_the_free_action_count_is_cleared_WITH_the_turns`.

## A dim map palette drew its cells under INK (2026-09-23)

A Cove map's cells are filled at 20-40 luminance, with only broken edges
above `mappanel.INK` (40). Rooms were detected half-height, a vertical
corridor shredded into 41x4 slabs, and `fit` refused: "could not fit the map
to the 14 cells drawn", six replans, and the raid stalled in its room until
the iteration ended. At 25 the same frame fits 15/15. `locate` now retries at
`INK_DIM` = 25 **only when the normal fit fails**, so no map that already
worked changes. Fixture: `captures/map_dim_cove.png` + `.map.json`.

Method note: `captures/_map_panel.png` is rewritten on every failure by the
running bridge, and two of my reads disagreed because they were of different
frames. Copy it before measuring.

## A curio's journal page is a modal too (2026-09-23)

"Blood Soaked Pages Torn From A Journal (1/6)", a parchment modal with its own
close X, sat over a Cove corridor. The route walked into it for six minutes
("the door did not open", three times per replan) and the raid ran out its
30-minute allowance. `screen.journal_page_is_up` matches the X and the wax
seal together (each 0.505+ from every other frame; `captures/journal_page.png`
is the reference), and `ActRunner._answer_journal` closes it ahead of every
update, the same way the hunger check runs. Live: closed on the first poll,
and the party moved on.

## The progression page is recognised by its fixed strip (2026-09-23)

The Escape page is drawn over each dungeon's backdrop with its quirk shields
animating in, and it stalled two iterations as an unbanked variant, over the
Cove and then over the Weald. `screen.recognise` now falls back to
`progression_strip_is_up`, which matches the fixed "Return to Town"/"Next"
strip: every banked progression page scores <= 0.095 on it and the nearest
other screen 0.236. Banking the Weald frame whole instead cut the corpus's
worst separation from 0.305 to 0.224, so that frame was not kept.

## A heal aimed at a hero the game would not target froze the fight (2026-09-23)

A Musketeer's PATCH UP on the Bounty Hunter (rank 2). In target-select DD
brackets legal targets in green, and he had none. The click did nothing, the
miss was read as "the game is asking another hero" (the usual cause), and ten
minutes went on heroes it was not asking while her self-heal was legal
throughout. `ActRunner._retarget_to_a_legal_hero` now sends the skill key
alone, reads the green under each hero rank (legal 0.022-0.071, illegal
exactly 0.0), and swaps to an alternative with the same skill whose target is
legal. Live, the next turn: *"rank 2 is not a legal target on screen (legal
[1, 3, 4]) - hero rank 1 instead"*, confirmed by the save in 1.3 s.

Also added: `screen.title_card_is_up`, which matches the card's fixed lower
strip, so every region's title card is recognised (the Weald was the second
region to stall on it); and a `phantom_fight` nudge, which takes one step
when the save says a fight is on and no enemy health bar is drawn
(`screen.enemy_bars_drawn`). That nudge has NOT fired live yet: the case that
prompted it turned out to be this targeting bug, with a spider still alive on
a sliver of hp (0.0039).

## Ten combat retreats in a row killed three heroes by stress (2026-09-23)

Every combat retreat WORKED (the save shows +27 stress each time and the party
back in the corridor). What killed them was the gate in `Session.room` that
holds a raid-level RETREAT back on "the entrance room" -- it keyed on
`battles_completed == 0`, and a fight you retreat from is never completed. So
the raid-level verdict was suppressed, the route walked back into the same
fight, and the loop repeated until houndmaster, abomination and plague doctor
died at 200 stress. The gate now also requires that no fight was SEEN this
raid (`Session._fought`, set in `_battle_update`). With it, the next raid
retreated once and abandoned by the X from the corridor, as intended.

**The map click does move the party.** The same session walked rooE -> coDE
-> ... -> coCF through `mappanel.locate`; the earlier "click did not move"
never recurred once the title card below was handled.

**Two unbanked screens.** The Cove's title card (`dungeon_intro@cove`) stalled
a raid for seven minutes on "cannot tell which panel is up"; the route now
presses SPACE when it recognises a title card. The post-abandon "Escape"
page is `raid_progression_quirks@escape`.

## `--play-profile-0` alone made a NEW campaign (2026-09-23)

Run without `--resume --slot 0`, the flag was accepted and the default boot
ran the new-campaign flow on slot 1: empty letter, DLC screen, Darkest, the
scripted Old Road -- in `profile_1`. `profile_0` was not touched (last write
03:25, before the run). The flag now implies `--resume --slot 0`. The stray
`profile_1` was swept by the next run's own orphan sweep, which deletes only
a profile it can prove it created.

## A modded heirloom exchange: no trades are planned (2026-09-23)

Workshop 1679135290 (Garphyield's Exchange Heirlooms/Shards for Gold)
replaces `heirloom_exchange.json` and adds heirloom -> gold rows, so the
vanilla `exchange_to_row` aims at the wrong row; the name check refused
every confirm and the step spun each visit. `townplan.exchange_is_modded`
drops the rates, so only what the wallet covers outright is bought.

## A roster of 18 seated from row 7 four times, and the bar took 3 (2026-09-23)

`learn_roster_rows` only learns the eight rows the estate-map list draws. At
18 heroes every planned hero outside page 0 fell back to scroll-and-guess, all
four drags came from row 7, and the embark went with three. `town.
learn_roster_positions` now learns the WHOLE list: page 0 whole, then one
scroll click at a time, probing only row 7 (each click reveals exactly one new
hero). A click that re-reveals a known guid was swallowed and does not
advance the count. The seat loop replays `force_roster_to_top` +
`scroll_roster_by(k)` for a later-page hero and confirms against
`persist.roster.json` that THAT guid arrived, and rewinds to the top before
any page-0 seat, since the learning pass leaves the list scrolled down.

Same night: `reset` from the estate map pressed ESC once, landed on the
Hamlet (ESC closes the overlay), and wrote the iteration off as stuck; it now
presses again through `ESC_STEPS_BACK`. And `test_mod_heroes` failed three
ways because Fran's 17 current mods include no hero class; its gate now
globs for what the loader globs for (`*/**/heroes/*/*.info.darkest`).

## Dismissal is PLANNED at the real cap, not yet actuated (2026-09-23)

`townplan.choose_dismissals`: Fran's rule -- no S/A/B quirk on PizzaRat's
tiers (`QUIRK_TIER`, keyed by game id) AND (level 0 OR below the roster mean),
most stressed first, never a hero in a building or the party. `advise` only
reports it (`TownAdvice.dismissals`); the freed slots are NOT counted as room,
because counting a slot that no click has freed re-creates the "plan a hire
the game refuses" loop. Actuation needs the dismiss button found live.

## Nobody was ever hired, and two separate things caused it (2026-09-22)

Fran: *"you didnt recruit anyone as i told, so now you're stuck with a
limited pool of characters and all stressed out"*. He was right, and it was
not the recruiting code -- that was correct the whole time.

### 1. The roster cap was read as 9 against a real cap of 1017

`roster_cap` composes the vanilla `stage_coach.rostersize` tiers out of
`GAME_DIR` and knows nothing about anything else. So it answered **9** for a
roster of **11**, `choose_recruits` correctly reported "room for 0", and
every week hired nobody while twelve recruits -- a Plague Doctor, a Crusader,
a Highwayman among them -- sat in the coach.

DD composes the cap as the upgrade tier PLUS every
`*.roster.additional_variables.json` it can see. Two sources were missing:

* **the mods** -- `Expanded Roster: 1000` ships
  `{"additional": {"roster_size": 1000}}`. Fran: *"there is no cap here, i
  enhanced the cap with a mod, the cap should be checked by the ingame
  info"*.
* **the DLC** -- Shieldbreaker, Duelist and Runaway ship one slot each.
  Fran: *"dlc files also increase roster slots"*. The `modes/` copies
  (bloodmoon, new_game_plus) are a DIFFERENT campaign and are skipped, or an
  ordinary campaign gains one per mode per class.

`workshop_roster_bonus` now sums both, giving 1003 and a cap of 1012 against
the game's own counter of 1017. **The remainder is in the vanilla tier this
file computes, and a cap that is a little LOW is safe where one that is high
is not** -- low under-reports room, high would hire into a roster that
cannot hold it.

### 2. A failed heirloom trade abandoned the hires

Even with room, the visit never reached the hire loop. A misfired trade
returned out of `visit_stage_coach`, and the reason was real: the coach panel
is CLOSED for a trade, because the exchange toggle sits on the bar underneath
it, so the hire drags went into a live Hamlet.

But the answer to a closed panel is to open it. Measured: the plan wanted
`stage_coach.numrecruits.c` at 15 crest + 16 deed against a wallet holding
**one** deed, the trade for it could not complete, and `abomination` and
`plague_doctor` -- both already chosen, and both FREE -- were dropped. Fran:
*"if you dont have enough heirlooms to unlock level, the heirlooms+upgrades
step is useless"*.

The remaining purchases are still abandoned, which is the part that was
right: they were costed against a wallet that no longer exists. The hires are
not, because a hire costs nothing and is the most valuable thing in the
visit. Verified live -- same failing trade, then `OK hire houndmaster`,
`OK hire duelist`, roster 16 -> 18.

### What is still wrong here

The heirloom exchange itself misfires: `to-row 2 reads deed (d=21, margin=7),
not crest`. That is the same pixel-row identification this install breaks
everywhere else, and it is why the upgrade never lands. And the RECRUIT row
indices from the save do not match the drawn rows, so hand-picking a class
grabs its neighbour -- the coach list compacts upward as heroes are taken,
which `visit_stage_coach` already tracks but a direct caller does not.

## A map click ON the room did not move the party (2026-09-22, corrected 2026-09-23)

With the seating fixed the bridge fielded four heroes and started a raid --
and never left the entrance room. One room block for the whole raid (`rooH`),
**eleven** `leave roo` commands, **zero** battles, and the party standing
still for twenty minutes while hunger and the dark took them from fresh to
177/171/186/119 stress.

**RETRACTED: "the click lands on empty grid".** The first version of this
entry measured the rooms off `captures/_map_panel.png` in CLIENT pixels --
party (855, 587), left room (784, 587), right room (927, 587), room above
(856, 515) -- and compared them against the logged click `(818, 614)`,
concluding it fell between two cells and 27px low. That logged click is a
SCREEN coordinate: the hover probe resolves its target through
`win.to_screen`, and every `CLICK` line in these logs is screen space. With
the client origin at (34, 31) it is client **(784, 583)** -- the centre of
the left room. The aim was right. Two instruments disagreeing is a bug
signal, and this time the bug was in the reading.

So the open question is the opposite one: **a click that lands on an
adjacent room does not move the party.** Candidates, none yet tested live --
a panel (a loot window after the `sack` curio in the same room) swallowing
the input; the corridor to that room carrying the red X drawn beside the
party token; or the click registering as a hover rather than a travel order.
The one real defect found on the frame is separate: `panel_rect` starts at
y 510 while the panel's top row of rooms is drawn from about y 497, so every
top-row room is sliced to a 10-14px fragment (three of eight boxes on
`03_ruins_map_panel.png`, three of eighteen here). That corrupts the scale the
fitter derives, but it cannot explain a click on a MIDDLE-row room failing.

## The roster row is ASKED now, not inferred -- and the party seats 4 of 4 (2026-09-22)

The seating had been 3 of 4 for two sessions. Three separate theories were
tried and each was falsified by the next measurement; what finally worked
was to stop inferring the answer and run an experiment.

**What the estate-map roster list actually does**, measured rather than
assumed:

* A seated hero **stays in the list**, at the same row, and gains a red
  marker. The list does not close up behind them. Dragging that row again
  grabs the SAME hero and merely moves them between ranks -- reproduced
  deliberately: one hero seated, then three more drags from row 0 into ranks
  2, 3 and 4, and the bar read 1 after every one.
* The 33.7 change in a row's patch after a seat -- which the "the list
  re-flows" theory rested on -- was that MARKER appearing.
* The list order is **not** the save order. Ceauce is save index 5 and draws
  at row 3.
* The portrait matcher that used to recover the order compares against art
  `Amara's Character UI` replaces, so it returns confident wrong answers
  (26-57 mean levels, runner-up gaps of 0.02 against a required 2.0).

So there was no sound way left to say which hero is on which row.

**`town.learn_roster_rows` asks the game instead.** It drags a row onto the
bar, reads `persist.roster.json` to see whose `in_party` just turned on, and
puts them back. The save is the one channel the mods cannot touch, and it is
the same evidence `clear_party` and the seat count already trust. One pass
per town visit, eight drags out and back.

Live on Fran's roster it recovered every row, and the answer was nothing
like the save order:

    row 0 -> guid 2      row 1 -> jester 8        row 2 -> bounty_hunter 7
    row 3 -> grave_robber 27   row 4 -> leper 31  row 5 -> bounty_hunter 29
    row 6 -> hellion 28  row 7 -> man_at_arms 26

**The seating then worked first time**: rows 1, 2, 5 and 7 into ranks 3, 2,
4 and 1, four OK and no `UNCONFIRMED`, `OK embark`, and a four-hero party --
man-at-arms, bounty hunter, jester, bounty hunter -- in a raid at torch 62.
That is the first full party this bridge has fielded on this save.

**The supporting instruments, both measured:**

* `party_slot_filled` reads a slot's TEXTURE. Empty is frame art at std 7.9
  and identical across all four; occupied carries a portrait at std
  42.0-69.5. `clear_party` now says `already empty` or `the hero is STILL on
  the bar` instead of "the bar did not change", which could never tell those
  apart -- and that ambiguity was hiding heroes left on the bar, whom the
  planner then picked and nothing could drag.
* A seat must make the filled count go UP. A drag that moves an already
  seated hero changes the bar without adding to it, and used to report OK.

## The party bar can be READ now, and the quest click could not be (2026-09-22)

A second autonomous session, spent entirely on why the bridge could not put
four heroes on the bar and then embarked on the wrong dungeon.

### "The bar did not change" was never an answer

`clear_party` reported `UNCONFIRMED ... (the bar did not change (an empty
slot looks like this))` for every slot it failed on AND every slot that was
already empty. Those are opposite facts and the difference is a raid.

**They are separable by TEXTURE.** Measured on the estate map with the bar
filled and then emptied, same slots, same screen:

| state | mean | std |
|---|---|---|
| empty | 15.2 | **7.9** (identical across all four -- it is frame art) |
| occupied | 32.5-74.5 | **42.0-69.5** |

`town.party_slot_filled` uses the STD rather than the mean, because a dark
portrait and a lit frame can share a mean. 20 sits five times clear of both
sides. The clear now reports `already empty` or `the hero is STILL on the
bar`, and neither is a guess.

**What it exposed immediately**: the planner was choosing heroes who were
ALREADY on the bar. Measured -- plan wanted guids 26, 7, 8, 29 while
`in_party` held [2, 8, 7, 27], so two of the four could not be dragged from
a roster list that does not draw them, every iteration, for hours.

### A seat has to make the bar FULLER

A drag can grab a hero who is ALREADY seated, who then MOVES from one rank to
another. The bar changes, the drag reports OK, and the party is one short.
Measured: rows 3, 3, 6, 7 into ranks 3, 2, 4, 1, all four "OK", bar took 3
of 4.

**RETRACTED, the re-flow explanation.** This first read as the list closing
up behind a seated hero, so a row aimed at twice would hold two different
heroes. It does not: measured on the estate map, a seated hero STAYS in the
list at the same row and gains a red marker, and dragging that row again
grabs the same hero. Reproduced deliberately -- one hero seated, then three
more drags from row 0 into ranks 2, 3 and 4, and the bar read 1 after every
one. The 33.7 change in the row's patch after a seat, which the re-flow
theory rested on, was the MARKER appearing.

So the `live_rows.pop` that came with that theory is gone; indices do not
shift. What is still unknown is the list's ORDER -- it is not the save's
(Ceauce is save index 5 and draws at row 3) and the portrait matcher that
used to recover it is broken by `Amara's Character UI`. Reading the list
rather than inferring it is the next piece of work, and it is a design
change rather than a patch.

The seat loop now counts filled slots either side of each drag and requires
+1, and waits for the re-flow before the next one. **The drag itself was
never at fault**: dragging rows 0-3 into the four ranks one at a time took
the bar from [15.2, 15.2, 15.2, 15.2] to [34.9, 74.5, 39.3, 32.5] first try.

### The quest click was hitting nothing, and the last quest stayed selected

**This is the one that could have ended the campaign.** The plan chose
`warrens cleanse`; the raid that started was `farm wave` -- the endless
Farmstead, with ONE hero in the party.

`quest_point` derives one token per DUNGEON from shipped geometry. This save
holds **ten** quests because of `More Available Quests` -- four of them in
the Ruins alone -- and the derived token resolved to client (583, 359) where
the Warrens emblem actually sits at **(548, 325)**. A click 35x34 off the
emblem hits nothing, and DD leaves the previous selection alone.

The save carries no `selected` marker to check against -- `persist.quest.json`
lists the quests and nothing says which is chosen -- so the evidence has to
be the SCREEN, and picking a quest always redraws the left-hand panel.
Measured mean absolute difference over the whole frame:

    idle, no click at all      0.023
    a click on empty map       0.042
    a click on a quest emblem  1.247

`pick_quest` now refuses when the frame does not move, rather than embarking
on whatever was selected before. The list holds `darkestdungeon` at
difficulty 6 and the Farmstead, so the cost of not checking is a level-0
party in content that ends a campaign -- which is exactly what happened.

### Four more unbanked variants, and the count is not converging

`provision@farmstead`, `stage_coach_panel@upgrades`,
`raid_results@farmstead_defeat`, plus the victory pages from the night
before. Every one stalled the loop dead. The reference set is now 38 and the
variant count is bounded by mods x DLC x animation state, not by the number
of screens. `screen.hunger_panel_is_up` remains the pattern that retires the
class rather than the instance.

## The Guild buys skills now, and the roster list is not the save (2026-09-22)

A night of autonomy spent on one chain: the comp the embark plan pays for
could not be given its skills, and the party it seated was not the party it
planned.

### The Guild, measured and PROVED BY SPENDING

`GUILD_MEASURED` was False and the derived tree points were the reason to
say so. They compose to client (427, 65) for row 0; the panel draws it at
**(757, 198)** -- 330x133 out, the same shape of residual the stage coach
needed (133.5, 35.3 design) a live frame to correct.

**The files were right about the pitch and wrong only about the origin**,
which is why this is a residual and not a rewrite: `skill_spacing 0 91` is
60.7 client against a measured 60.8 down the rows, and
`requirement_spacing 75 0` is 50.0 against a measured 50.2 across the ranks.

Three things had to be true at once and none was:

1. **The building had to be reachable.** `building_button` centred the
   navigation strip on `base_size` (128, the STRIP) instead of the 68px
   button, resolving to client x 89 when the icons sit at 52-80. Every click
   missed. Half a button is 34, design x 104, client 69 -- inside the sprite.
