2. **A missing scene point had to stop being a dead end.** The strip is not
   drawn on the Hamlet, so it cannot open the FIRST building -- that lesson
   from 2026-08-31 stands and is now pinned by a test that the strip may
   never be the first click. But the coach's scene point has been measured
   since then, and once any panel is up the strip reaches every other
   building. `plan_open_building` opens the coach and hops.
3. **A hero had to be in the panel.** The tree is not drawn at all until one
   is dropped in the slot, which is why this needed a live frame and why
   `_put_hero_in_guild` exists.

The row index is the store's skill index, verified rather than assumed:
Dismas's padlocked rows were 1, 3 and 6 and his class order puts
`wicked_slice`, `point_blank_shot` and `duelist_advance` at exactly those
positions. Clicking row 0 took the wallet **10,865 -> 9,865**, the exact
1,000 `embarkplan` prices an unlock at.

### The roster LIST is not the roster SAVE

Fran, watching a night loop: *"the seating is seating a leper on the back
which is really sus"*. The plan had him at rank 1. The drag did not.

**A hero standing in the party bar is not drawn in the roster list.** DD
moves them out of it, so a roster of 11 with 3 seated draws exactly 8 --
which is also `ROSTER_ROWS_VISIBLE`, so nothing can scroll and the chevron
is not drawn. `_roster_offset` matched the 11-long SAVE sequence against 8
drawn portraits, reported **offset 2**, and the seat for save row 0 was
refused as "not on screen" while Dismas was sitting in the top row.

That is the failure this function's own docstring warned about: a wrong
offset does not miss, it drags a DIFFERENT hero and the party bar confirms
it happily. `drawn_roster` is now the one definition of what the list holds.

**And the save answers late, so the flags could not be trusted either.**
Indexing a snapshot taken before `clear_party` dropped exactly the heroes
the loop was about to seat; re-reading afterwards still described the bar as
it WAS, because `persist.roster.json` is written late. The clear is the
truth: it has just emptied every slot it could, so the seats are computed
against a roster with `in_party` cleared. Two separate 3-of-4 embarks
(highwayman#2 both times) are what taught that in order.

### Every building panel closes the same way

A night stopped mid-town-visit leaves a panel open, and `to_town` had no
move for `stage_coach_panel` -- three iterations dead in under ten seconds
each. DD draws ONE close glyph for all of them (`CLOSE_GLYPH_CENTRE` already
records that the activity log's and the building base's `close_pos` are both
(1496, 144)), so the walk matches the `_panel` suffix and the guild, abbey
and tavern come along free.

### The standing weakness this night exposed

**Whole-frame references do not survive this install.** Four screens stalled
the loop in one night purely by being unbanked VARIANTS of screens we
already had: the victory rewards page, the victory progression page (which
also animates -- the quirk names fade in, so one frame is not enough), the
hunger panel over an unbanked room, and the provision screen for a Farmstead
quest. The cause is structural: Fran runs 17 mods including four that change
the UI, and DLC zones bring their own art, so the number of variants is not
bounded by the number of screens.

`screen.hunger_panel_is_up` is the pattern that generalises -- match the
PANEL's own box and the room behind it stops mattering. Measured over that
box, the three banked hunger frames score 0.000-0.002 and the nearest thing
that is not hunger scores 0.792. Doing the same for the provision and
results pages would retire the whole class of stall; banking variants one at
a time will not.

## The Guild was unreachable, so the comp was never the comp (2026-09-21)

Fran, on a party that died: *"the former comp didnt truly use the potential
of the comp"*. He is right, and the cause is one wrong coordinate.

`fieldable_comps` already chooses a comp WITH the cost of giving each hero
that comp's own four skills -- the embark plan budgeted `unlocks 3000` for
exactly that. Then the town visit printed:

    SKIP unlock 3 skill(s) (the guild's scene point has never been measured)

So the skills were priced, the gold was reserved, and nothing was bought. The
party embarked carrying a comp it had never been given.

**`base_size` IS THE STRIP, NOT THE BUTTON.** `townlayout.building_button`
composed the navigation strip's button as `base + start + spacing*index +
(base_size[0]/2, spacing[1]/2)`. `base_size` is (128, 1000) -- the frame
around a column of ten -- while each button is `button_spacing` square,
68x68. Half the strip is 64 and puts the click at design x 134; half a
button is 34, design x 104. Measured live: the icons sit at client x 52-80
at 1280x720 and the old point resolved to **89**, outside every sprite. The
y was right all along (design 400 for the Guild, client 267 against a
measured centre of 261).

**A missing scene point is no longer a dead end.** The strip is not drawn on
the Hamlet -- that lesson from 2026-08-31 stands and is now pinned by a test
that the strip may never be the FIRST click. But the stage coach's scene
point has been measured since then, and once ANY panel is open the strip
reaches every other building. `plan_open_building` now opens the coach and
hops, which was verified live: coach, strip, and the Guild panel came up.
That is two clicks to a building nobody has measured in the scene, and it
un-refuses the Blacksmith, Abbey, Tavern and Sanitarium at the same time.

## The same tired four went out twice (2026-09-21)

Fran: *"after the first expedition we immediately went onto the 2nd one with
the same comp without changing heroes even if they were stressed out ... each
week lets redo the comp"*.

`_rested` benched at `RESOLVE_CHECK` (100) and nothing else re-picked: the
comp choice is deterministic, so an unchanged roster returns an unchanged
party. At 100 a hero is already afflicted; the module deliberately refused
to be "cleverer than the game" below that. What it missed is that *legal to
send* and *worth sending* are different questions.

`STRAINED = 50` is now tried first, with `RESOLVE_CHECK` as the fallback and
the whole pool as the last resort, so a thin roster still fields four. The
team-builder repo filters on the same 50 (`STRESS_CONFIG.STRAINED`), reached
independently there from a roster study -- two instruments agreeing from
opposite directions.

Measured on the campaign that prompted this: an Occultist at 60 fielded at
rank 1 on a 19 HP body, a second embark on week 2 with no change, and one
hero home at 3 HP and 157 stress.

**What the seating was NOT.** The dead comp was checked against its own
ranks: 15 of its 16 equipped skills were launchable from where they stood,
the exception being the Houndmaster's `blackjack` at rank 3. Rank placement
did not kill them, and `launchable_equipped` -- added here so a hero is
scored on their OWN four rather than their class's seven, which also tells
Fran's two Bounty Hunters apart -- is an accuracy fix, not the cause.

## The torch gesture, measured against the game's own tooltip (2026-09-21)

Fran, watching a raid: *"the model didn't snuff torch, in fact, it increased
torch before killing the last enemy just now"*. The bridge had been raising
the light on the killing blows it was supposed to darken, one torch per
click, while its log reported a snuff.

**The instrument that settled it is DD's own light tooltip.** Clicking the
dial pops a label naming the bracket and the exact value -- `RADIANT LIGHT
( 93 )`, `SHADOWY ( 43 )`, `BLACK AS PITCH ( 0 )`. It is live, unlike
`persist.raid.json`, which is written per TURN: during this work the save
read 68 while the screen read 0. Every number below is off that tooltip.

**The gesture table, and the dwell is part of it.**

| gesture | MOD_DWELL 0.12s | MOD_DWELL 0.35s |
|---|---|---|
| plain click | +25 | +25 |
| SHIFT+click | **+25** | **-25** |
| CTRL+SHIFT+click | -25 | -25 |

At 0.12s every gesture degraded one step toward "no modifier", so SHIFT+click
-- the reduce -- became a plain click and USED A TORCH. `GetAsyncKeyState`
reported the keys down in every one of those cases, so the OS was never
behind; SDL had not processed the key event when the click arrived. Fran
named the fix before the measurement did: *"we can hold ctrl or shift rather
than doing at the same time for better measure"*. `MOD_DWELL` is 0.35s now
and `tests/bridge/test_act.py` pins it with the table.

**CTRL never arrives at all, and the one-click full snuff does not exist
here.** `str_snuff_torch_tip` documents CTRL+SHIFT as a full snuff and Fran
reports it working by hand, but through `SendInput` it moves the light 25 --
`RADIANT LIGHT ( 100 )` -> `DIM LIGHT ( 75 )` on a click held 0.35s. **An
earlier reading in this same session called it a full snuff and is
RETRACTED**: it was taken at light 25, where -25 and -100 both land on 0 and
the two cannot be told apart. Injected input and a real keyboard differ here,
which is worth knowing beyond the torch.

So the snuff is now `ceil(level / 25)` SHIFT clicks in one plan. Verified
live through the shipped `plan_reduce_torch` and the resolved layout:
`DIM LIGHT ( 75 )` -> `BLACK AS PITCH ( 0 )`.

**What else changed, each because it hid this bug or made it worse.**

- `layout.TORCH_DIAL` -- the widget, measured off a raid frame at (641, 80)
  in client pixels, which is the round NUMBER at the centre of the light
  bar. Fran: *"the point of click is the number on the torch"*. It heads
  `TORCH_CANDIDATES`; the ten-candidate sweep that preceded it was hunting a
  pixel that was never far wrong.
- **A torch that went UP now stops the sweep dead.** That branch read a rise
  as "did not move" and tried the next candidate, so a failed modifier could
  spend several torches lighting the room. The candidate is recorded as the
  USE control and removed from the order rather than merely skipped.
- **A modified click whose modifier did not register is no longer sent.**
  `SendInputBackend._mods_are_down` asks `GetAsyncKeyState`, re-presses once,
  and refuses the click rather than send a bare one. It is necessary and NOT
  sufficient -- it said "down" all through the 0.12s failures -- so the dwell
  carries the rest.
- `TORCH_MAX_CLICKS` 6 -> 10, since a full ladder is four and the budget has
  to cover the ones the game swallows.

## A SLEEPING MONITOR reads as a black game frame, and it cost a night (2026-09-21)

The 09:15 unattended run was scored `no-game` over four iterations, with
`THE GAME IS NOT DRAWING` each time. The game was fine. Fran ended the
investigation in one line: *"I've moved the mouse to wake up the monitor"*.

**What the machine looked like while it was "not drawing":**

| instrument | reading |
|---|---|
| `screen.grab(win)` | `min=0 max=0` -- pure black |
| the same rect via the SCREEN DC | pure black |
| `PrintWindow` + `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` | the title bar, black client |
| the DESKTOP, 400x300 at (0,0) | mean 33.4, max 255 -- drawing |
| `IsHungAppWindow` / `WM_NULL` | not hung, pump answers |
| CPU | advancing, 176.8 -> 177.0 s across a second |
| **GPU 3D engine, the game's pid** | **16.9%** |
| `applog.entries()` | booted, PlayFab pinging on schedule |
| recogniser, after the wake | `title`, `the picture is changing` |

So the game was rendering a live title screen the whole time. Once Windows
powers the monitor down, a window's frames stop reaching the surface GDI
reads -- while the DESKTOP keeps answering from its cached composition. That
is what makes this so expensive: the obvious control, "is anything on this
machine drawing?", says YES, and it is measuring the wrong thing.

**Three wrong diagnoses were recorded on the way, and they are kept here
because each was defensible on the evidence then available.**

1. *Independent flip*, which is what the message asserted. Falsified by
   geometry: the window was 1296x759 at (26, 0) on a 1920x1080 monitor,
   neither borderless nor monitor-covering, so the remedy it named
   (`-fullscreen 0 -size 1280 720`) was **already in force**.
2. *A startup stall*, from app.log stopping at `OnGetBanCheckCallback` and
   never reaching a front-end state. Falsified by the GPU counter: a stalled
   process does not hold 16.9% of the 3D engine. app.log is simply quiet at
   the title screen -- `applog.state()` is documented as coarse, and returns
   `""` for everything before a campaign loads.
3. *A multiplane overlay or capture hook* promoting the windowed swapchain
   off the composited path -- `gameoverlayrenderer64.dll` and
   `nvspcap64.dll` are both injected. Not falsified, and not shown either:
   the window recovered after it was moved, resized and re-stacked, and the
   wake happened in the same minutes. **That correlation is why the nudge
   got credit it had not earned.**

**What changed.**

- `bridge/window.keep_display_awake()` / `wake_display()` --
  `SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS | ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED |
  ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED)`, plus a zero-distance `mouse_event` for the wake,
  because the request alone does not raise a monitor that is already down.
  The cursor does not move, so it cannot disturb a hover the recogniser
  depends on.
- `tools/overnight.py` asserts it for the whole night and prints
  `display  : kept awake` in the header. A refused request says so; it is
  not claimed.
- `Overnight._wake_and_recheck` treats a black frame as a QUESTION: wake,
  then re-sample for 45 s before scoring the iteration `no-game`.
- `screen._why_pure_black` names the sleeping display FIRST, stops telling
  the operator to apply a remedy already in force, and names GPU 3D time as
  the instrument that separates a blind capture from a dead game **without
  needing a frame** -- which is the check that would have answered this in
  one minute instead of an hour.

The old message's own docstring had already predicted this: *"the remaining
false positive is a still frame with no name -- a mid-load black screen, or
an alt-tabbed game that stopped rendering ... Sample again after the load."*
Nothing sampled again. It does now.

## The provision basket spends the REMAINDER now, and it is the third bite (2026-09-21)

Fran, after a raid that came home with gold unspent and the party hungry:
*"we should buy extra food and torches if we have money on the provisions
(max if possible)"*.

`data/provisions.top_up(want, gold, length)` adds torches and food in that
order, each capped by the shop's own stock for that dungeon length, spending
only `gold - cost(want)`. Measured on the crypts short guide basket: the
guide costs **3550**; at 5000 gold it returns food 12 -> 18 and torch 8 -> 18
for **4750**, and the ceiling is the STOCK, not the gold -- the provision
screen will not sell a 19th torch.

**The order it is applied in is the whole content of this entry.** The first
version called `top_up` inside `_basket_for`, which is where the basket is
priced for the reservation -- and that made the top-up eat the skill budget,
because `embarkplan` documents provisions as reserved FIRST and the skill
spend as *what is left*. Topping up inside the reservation meant the party
bought its 18th torch instead of a level-3 skill, which is the opposite of
the ranking in `PLAYBOOK.md` 1.x.

So `plan_embark` is now a thin wrapper over `_decide_embark`, and the top-up
runs in `_spend_the_remainder()` **after** the unlocks are chosen, against
`gold - basket_cost - unlocks_cost`. Three bites in a fixed order: the guide
basket, the skill unlocks, then whatever is still on the counter.

`tests/bridge/test_embarkplan.py::test_provisions_are_reserved_before_any_skill_is_bought`
now asserts the reservation on the DECISION (`_decide_embark`) rather than on
the final basket, plus the two things the top-up must never move --
`unlocks_cost` unchanged and `gold_after >= 0`. Pinning the reservation to the
final `basket_cost` was only ever right while nothing could legitimately spend
after it, and the assertion that fired (`4450 <= 3550`) was the test being
correct about an invariant that had moved, not a bug.

## What a deferral is actually FOR, and when it is worthless (2026-09-21)

Fran, reading a run that left a strongbox and 880 gold in the dungeon:
*"several chests were skipped even with skeleton key"*. They were not
skipped -- they were DEFERRED and then abandoned, which this module's own
docstring already names as the one thing a deferral must never do.

**The first fix was wrong and is recorded because the reasoning matters.**
`WALKS_HOME` was set False on the argument that `act.plan_leave_raid` now
ends a raid from wherever the party stands, so no corridor is ever re-entered
and every deferral is a forfeit. Fran corrected the premise:

> deferring a loot curio can be useful to maintain torches to have a safe
> number of torches for the dungeon, but when you beat the dungeon or cases
> like one way exits you should snuff torch to max loot value when
> backtracking

So the saving is **a torch**, not only the dark multiplier -- the curio is
opened later in the dark instead of light being held for it. And the red seal
is pressed at the END of the backtrack, not instead of it: that is true of
the EXIT and false of the plan. `WALKS_HOME` is True, deferral stays, and the
acceptance test that reproduces his 2026-09-06 route is pinned under the
premise it was given rather than edited to match a behaviour change it
predates.

### The real cut: a party already in the dark defers nothing

> if it's already a 0 torch there is also not a use on deferring that as well
> as you won't increase that value later, this covers cases like combat with
> chest rooms for example

The module cut deferral after a PLANNED snuff, and when no battle existed at
all. Both ask where the snuff falls; **neither asked what the torch reads
now** -- and walking in at 0 is exactly where a fight leaves you, since the
snuff lands before the killing blow. So a chest in that room was deferred for
a darkness it already had, and the deferral bought nothing while risking the
loot.

Narrow on purpose, and derived from what is already in scope: a relight is
only ever planned to clear the floor for a FIGHT and only if the pack can pay
for it, so "no fight ahead, or no torch to spend" is exactly "the light is
not coming back".

| torch | pack | verdict on the tent |
|---|---|---|
| 100 | 4 torches | **defer** -- the case deferral is for |
| **0** | **none** | **take now** -- dark either way |
| 0 | 3 torches | **defer** -- the light is coming back, so it buys a torch |

`tests/bridge/test_lootplan.py` pins all three rows.

## The bridge had no RIGHT BUTTON, so no curio could be opened with an item (2026-09-21)

Fran, watching a raid: *"the model seems to be unable to use inventory items
to right click with curio, specially with loot curios"*.

The game says the same thing in its own words, `[pc]str_help_raid_room_6`:

> *"While interacting with an object: **[RIGHT-CLICK] an inventory item** to
> apply it to the object. Some items will yield positive effects."*

**There was no right click anywhere in the actuator.** Every backend had
`click`, `drag`, `key` and `keys`, and nothing else -- so the verb the game
requires for a skeleton key on a locked chest, a shovel on a blockage or a
holy water on a fountain simply did not exist. `str_help_raid_combat_3` names
the same button for Anti-Venom and Bandages, so this is not a curio special
case.

And `_use_on_curio` was aiming at the wrong widget as well: it LEFT-clicked a
slot on the CURIO PANEL. Wrong button, wrong target.

### What changed

* `MOUSEEVENTF_RIGHTDOWN`/`_RIGHTUP`, and a `right_click` on every backend.
  The base implementation **refuses** rather than falling back to a left
  click: the two are different verbs to this game, and the wrong one on an
  inventory item is a discard or a no-op, never the intended use.
* `"rclick"` is its own `Step` kind rather than a flag on `click`, so every
  existing plan, test and count keeps meaning what it meant -- and the two
  can be asserted apart, which `test_using_an_item_on_a_curio_is_a_RIGHT_click`
  does.
* The target is now the PACK SLOT the save names, through
  `layout.inventory_point` -- the same 2x8 grid the discard and stack-merge
  paths already use. The save is exact about WHICH item is being spent; the
  panel's item row only ever said how many slots were lit, which is why
  `sole_item` refuses whenever more than one is. It stays as the fallback for
  a pack that cannot be read.

**Why this mattered more than its size.** `bridge/lootplan.py` prices the
whole route on loot, and `bandits_trapped_chest` is its worked example: `Blight
1` bare-handed, loot table `T` with a skeleton key, the same map flipping SKIP
-> TAKE on one item. Every one of those decisions was being computed
correctly and then thrown away at the actuator, because the input that
executes it did not exist.

## The night the loop ran itself: five fixes from the front end (2026-09-21)

Fran handed over the machine. Everything below was found by running
`tools/overnight.py` against the real game and reading what it did.

### 1. The raid exit is the RED SEAL, not the scroll under it

The 2026-09-20 fix pointed `plan_leave_raid` at `RAID_ABANDON_QUEST`, which
is the little scroll with an X. Live, it did nothing: the click went out, the
results page never came, and the party stood in `rooA` until the phase gave
up. Fran, watching it: *"do not click the scroll, click the red symbol"*.

Measured with the quest complete, at 1280x720:

| target | client | result |
|---|---|---|
| `RAID_ABANDON_QUEST` — the scroll | (52, 90) | nothing, over a six-second watch |
| **`RAID_QUEST_SEAL` — the wax seal above it** | **(55, 50)** | **`raid_results` within two seconds** |

They are 40 px apart and they are different widgets. `plan_retreat` keeps the
scroll -- that one is the in-combat retreat and the game's own
`[pc]str_help_raid_combat_4` names it -- and the test that used to assert the
two share a pixel now asserts the opposite, which is the fact that cost a
raid.

**And the seal takes no confirm**: the results page comes straight up when
the objective is met. The plan is ONE click, and `_leave_raid` answers a
modal only if it actually sees one, rather than sending `MODAL_OK` into
whatever is drawn next.

Confirmed live the same night: `[act] [raid] out of the dungeon
(raid_results)` -> `-- raid over --` -> the results chain -> the Hamlet ->
the estate map -> a crypts quest -> four heroes seated -> eight torches
bought -> embark -> a second raid.

### 2. A SCROLLED save list matched no reference, so the recogniser guessed

With four campaigns on disk the boot has to scroll to reach a free slot. The
scroll worked -- and the scrolled list is a different picture, which matched
no banked screen, so `recognise` returned its best guess: **`dlc`**. The boot
then clicked `dlc_ok`, `modal_ok` and `darkest` **into the save list**, and
those coordinates land on save rows.

Nothing was damaged -- they hit row name fields, and DD only commits on the
letter click plus its confirm chain -- but that is luck, not design: one of
the per-row icons is a DELETE button. `save_select@scrolled` is banked now
(separation 0.603 from its nearest other screen, and it collapses to
`save_select` through `VARIANT_SEP`), so the list is named whether or not it
has been scrolled.

### 3. The boot asked for slot 2 and the game made profile_7

`claim_slot` PREDICTS which `profile_<n>` a new campaign will land in, and DD
does not agree. The loop then owned a slot that was never created, its
teardown deleted nothing, and the real campaign became an orphan no later run
could claim or remove. **Both of this machine's orphans were made that way.**

`reset.claim_created(before)` owns what actually appeared, diffed against a
snapshot taken before the boot. The safety argument is unchanged and made
directly rather than by prediction -- *"a directory that existed before we
started can never be wrapped in an `OwnedSlot`"* -- and it refuses on
nothing-new (no campaign was made) and on more-than-one-new (the diff cannot
say which is ours, and guessing is how the wrong save gets deleted).

### 4. And an interrupted run left a campaign nobody could clean up

A run killed mid-raid never reaches its teardown, so the directory survives
with an unreleased claim against it -- and the next run, snapshotting before
it boots, correctly treats it as pre-existing and refuses to touch it.

`reset.orphans()` is the sweep nobody had written. The journal already held
the proof of ownership and `_open_claim` already read it, expiry and all;
`tools/overnight.py` now sweeps at startup, before anything launches. A
campaign a human made has no journal entry and can never be produced by it.
Verified live: `[night] swept an orphan this tool made: slot 0`.

### 5. The empty letter is FOUND, not computed

`scroll_plan` needs three numbers right at once -- how many rows the list
holds, how many are visible, and how far one arrow click scrolls -- and
measured live the last two are both wrong: `VISIBLE_SAVE_ROWS` says 2 where
the viewport draws 3, and one click moves about three rows. The arrival row
it computes is a guess, and the guard that catches the guess then refuses the
boot outright: *"slot 2 has no campaign on disk but visible row 0 draws
one"*.

None of it is needed for a NEW campaign now that `claim_created` owns
whatever the game makes. `_empty_letter_plan` reads the frame, finds a row
`row_holds_a_campaign` says is a letter, and clicks that -- the same
predicate the safety gate already uses, so the two cannot disagree. Loading a
NAMED save still goes through the arithmetic, because there the row
identifies *which* save and a wrong one is a wrong campaign.

### What the suite caught on the way

Two test blocks got pasted twice, and
`test_no_test_module_defines_the_same_function_twice` failed with exactly the
right message: *"Python keeps the LAST one, so every earlier definition is
dead code pytest never collects"*. That guard is there because this repo's
characteristic bug is a test that stops running, and it earned its keep.

## Reading the last live stall: two fixes, both from its own log (2026-09-21)

The overnight loop's final iteration before the machine was handed back ended
with the new stall detector firing correctly -- `-- the dungeon is not
responding -- 6 room plans in a row on a save that has not been written
once`. Reading that log afterwards produced two things.

### 1. The stall report named nothing, because `_say_once` had swallowed the cause

Under each of the six repeated room plans there was **no `[act]` line at
all**, which reads as though the actuator had died. It had not: it was
refusing for the same reason every 25 s, and `_say_once` -- which exists to
stop a turn's refusal printing several times a second -- was suppressing it.
The actual cause sat sixty lines earlier and looked unrelated:

    [act] [route] the game offers no move at or near rooE - not clicking blind

**A refusal that repeats forever is the one case `_say_once` gets wrong.**
`ActRunner.explain_again()` clears the suppression and `Session.on_stall`
calls it ONCE, at the give-up bound, so the next poll explains itself in full
and then goes quiet again. The noise the mechanism exists to prevent does not
come back.

### 2. And the refusal itself was an asymmetry the code already argues against

`_show_map` has two ways to fail. When the fit produces NO point,
`_ask_the_game_which_cell` runs -- it orders candidates by the SAVE (the
party's own cell found by its torch, plus the direction to the destination in
map units) and ends in a documented last resort: *"a click that misses costs
one poll, never clicking costs the raid."*

When the fit produces a point that the game then REFUSES, there was no such
path. The branch refused and returned, every poll, forever -- which is
exactly what the party did. **The layer that knows how to find the room was
never asked**, because it was wired only to the other failure.

It now runs in both, with the same save-derived ordering. The fit that was
just refused does not get to order the fallback.

**Neither fix is speculative** -- both are read directly off the one log, and
both are pinned: `explain_again` behaviourally, and the branch ordering by
asserting the refusal is no longer the last thing that happens in
`_show_map`.

## `.target ?1234` dropped the whole key, on a VANILLA skill (2026-09-20)

Found by auditing the stall fix above, which is the only reason it turned up:
measuring how often the new `_invalidates_stall` rule disagreed with the old
one flagged a **fourth** skill beyond the three the wiki's Talk page names --
`musketeer/sidearm` -- and a rule firing on something undocumented is either
a discovery or a regression. It was neither; it was a parse bug underneath.

**The value character class had `@` and `~` and not `?`.**
`decode_position_notation` was taught about `?1234` when the modded corpus
turned up 88 of them, and its docstring says so -- but **the value never
reached it**. `_parse_line`'s unquoted alternative could not match `?1234`,
and per that function's own note an unmatchable value drops the WHOLE key
rather than arriving malformed. So `target` was absent from the record
entirely, decoded as `[]`, and the skill had no targets at all:

    parsed keys: [_type, atk, crit, dmg, effect, id, is_crit_valid,
                  launch, level, type]        <- no 'target'

**It is not a mod-only shape.** `.target ?1234` is shipped on exactly two
skills, at all five levels each:

| skill | class |
|---|---|
| `blindfire` | **Arbalest** — one of the fifteen base-game heroes |
| `sidearm` | Musketeer (DLC) |

Both are rank-4 shots (`launch 4321`, `dmg -10%`, a self speed buff) that the
real game aims at any enemy rank, and in the simulator neither could be aimed
at anything. Fixed by adding `?` to the class; both now read
`target=[1, 2, 3, 4]`, `is_aoe False` (`~` is the AoE marker, not `?`).
Pinned at both levels in `tests/data/test_loader.py` -- the regex and the
built store -- because a parse fix that does not reach `SkillLevelData.target`
has fixed nothing.

**And it removed the stall rule's false positive.** Re-measuring after the
parser fix, `sidearm` no longer disagrees: with a real target list it counts
as invalidating under both the old rule and the new one, which is correct.

## The stall fix is a WORLD CHANGE, and here is its size (2026-09-20)

`CombatEngine._invalidates_stall` changes `stall_counter` and
`_stall_invalidating_actions_this_round`, and **both are observation
features** (`env/gym_env.py`, normalised by their `rules.json` thresholds).
The width does not move, so this is the silent kind of break `CLAUDE.md`
warns about: *"Changing what an existing feature CONTAINS is the silent one —
the width still matches and the model is fed numbers it never saw."*

Measured over 24 episodes on the shipped checkpoint, 2,267 hero turns,
counting every hero skill use where the old rule and the new one disagree:

    41 of 2,267 hero turns   =   1.81%

All 41 are Houndmaster's **Guard Dog**. Leper's Revenge and Plague Doctor's
Emboldening Vapours were never chosen in the sample, so in practice this is
one skill on one class.

**So the shipped checkpoint is now very slightly off-distribution**, on under
2% of turns, in a direction that makes the simulator match the game. That is
worth stating rather than discovering later from an unexplained eval delta --
it is not worth a retrain on its own, and it should be folded into the next
one rather than measured against the old numbers.

## A stall fidelity bug, from the wiki's own Talk page (2026-09-20)

Fran supplied the `Stalling` wiki page and its `Talk:Stalling` discussion.
The article confirmed what was already implemented; the **Talk page** carried
the bug.

`.is_stall_invalidating` **defaults to TRUE** and is written into the skill
files only where it is false. So a non-damaging skill that carries no tag
inherits "invalidating" by omission, and `data/hero_loader.py`'s
`rec.get("is_stall_invalidating", True)` reproduces that faithfully. Three
shipped skills fall in that hole:

* Houndmaster **Guard Dog**
* Leper **Revenge**
* Plague Doctor **Emboldening Vapours**

The Talk page records that the game charges a stall for all three regardless,
and proposes the missing condition: *"the skill must target an enemy."*

**Checked against the shipped files, that one test reproduces every
documented row** -- ten of ten, including both of the wiki's "well-known
exceptions", which had to survive untouched: Jester's **Solo** is the
non-damaging skill that DOES invalidate (it targets the enemy side and
carries no tag), Occultist's **Vulnerability Hex** the damaging one that does
NOT (its tag is explicitly false).

`CombatEngine._invalidates_stall` is the gate. It is two conditions, because
self-targeting and ally-targeting are different things in this loader:
`_is_ally_skill` reads the `@` in the raw `.target` notation, while a pure
self-buff like Revenge has an EMPTY target list instead.

**The bug ran in the dangerous direction.** Those three read as invalidating,
so a simulated party could dodge a stall penalty with moves the real game
does not accept -- and two of the three are pure support, which is exactly
what a stalling party is casting. Pinned by
`tests/engine/test_reinforcements.py` against the whole documented table.

### And the ALL-vs-ANY reading is CLOSED, in the repo's favour

`ASSUMPTIONS.md` had kept `accelerated` as **all** living enemies carrying
`accelerate_stall_penalty`, not any, on the strength of the key's own name
(`stall_round_accelerated_only_*`) and against the wiki's looser "some
enemies accelerate". The page's Accelerated Penalties section settles it in
as many words: *"**If all remaining enemies belong to the list below**, then
stalling penalties are incurred faster."* No code changed; an open reading
became a documented one, and the falsifier can be retired.

The same section also confirms the accelerated schedule already
implemented -- a bark after one stalled round with **no stress**,
reinforcements on the second -- which had been carried as a wiki-confirmed
"2" with a simulator convention standing in for the increment arithmetic.

### Still not modelled, and now named

The page lists two whole-expedition exemptions the engine has no notion of:
**any Farmstead expedition** and **the first tutorial expedition to the
Ruins** disable stall penalties outright. Video 1 had claimed the tutorial
one and it went down as unverified; the wiki states it. Neither is reachable
from anything the bridge currently plays, so it is recorded rather than
implemented.

## Two stalling videos, triaged: nothing to fix (2026-09-20)

Fran supplied two community stalling guides as "enhanced context". Read as
auto-captions via `yt-dlp` -- YouTube gives a plain fetch nothing but footer
links. Full triage in `docs/COMMUNITY_GUIDES.md`.

**The engine needed no change, and that is the finding.** Every mechanic the
videos describe was already implemented off shipped constants: two
invalidating actions per round (`stall_number_of_invalidating_skills_threshold`
2), corpse hits counting, a 1-2 hero party never stalling
(`stall_party_size_threshold` 3), size-2 enemies exempt, the accelerating
monsters read from `.accelerate_stall_penalty`.

Two details are worth writing down because they were checked rather than
assumed:

* **Misses count.** Video 2 states it explicitly, and
  `_track_skill_use` increments the counter on skill USE, before any hit
  roll -- so the outcome is irrelevant by construction rather than by
  intent. It is also hero-only, which is right.
* **The community's "skills without a −100% damage modifier" rule is
  wrong**, and the game file says so in both directions: Jester's **Solo** is
  `dmg_mod -1.00` and `is_stall_invalidating` **True**; Occultist's
  **Vulnerability Hex** is `-0.90` and **False**; Inspiring Tune and Divine
  Grace are `0.00` and **False**. There is no damage-modifier rule -- there
  is a shipped per-skill boolean, which `data/hero_loader.py` has always
  read. Video 1 names both counterexamples correctly and still states the
  rule that contradicts them.

**`ASSUMPTIONS.md` already named that same skill pair on 2026-08-23**, so this
is a corroboration and not a discovery; it is recorded in the triage file so
that nobody later "fixes" the loader to match the circulating rule.

### What the videos do NOT settle, despite looking like they might

`ASSUMPTIONS.md` keeps `accelerated` as **ALL living enemies carry the flag**,
not ANY, on the strength of the key name `stall_round_accelerated_only_*`.
Video 1 offers one hint toward ANY -- *"that's a reason why people often kill
the webbers first"*, which buys nothing under ALL -- but the same video lists
the Webber as a bad stall target for stunning and marking, so the advice is
over-determined. Recorded with the confound named; the falsifier is unchanged.

Likewise the two enemy-side size rows look like corroboration and are not:
all 61 size-2-or-larger monsters also carry `disable_stall_penalty`, which
short-circuits before the occupancy test, so the videos describe a path the
declared reading never reaches.

### What is genuinely new, and queued

* **"Each enemy has its own reinforcement timer"** (video 2, which then
  declines to explain it). The engine keeps one `stall_counter`.
* **A stall TARGET gate.** Both videos split a fight into a rush phase (kill
  what is dangerous or bad to stall against) and a stall phase, and name the
  same bad targets: damage-over-time, stress attackers, disease carriers.
  `PLAYBOOK.md` 3.4 has the comp gate; nothing in the repo models the target
  side, and the bridge has no notion of a good or bad stall target.

## A ruler was scoring correct play as a violation (2026-09-20)

`tools/check_playbook.py`'s 3.3 check counted the wrong situation, and the
number it produced was reported before anyone noticed. Fran did: *"the
recover part only matters when you are stalling properly per se, not whenever
every enemy is stunned."*

PLAYBOOK 3.3 opens **"En stall,** si X está aturdido y va a morir a manos de
Y o Z, y W juega antes, W gasta su turno en curar o quitar estrés". The stall
is part of the criterion. The check gated on `all_stunned and
base._party_needs_recovery()` -- and `_party_needs_recovery()` is only ONE of
the three tests inside `is_recovery_phase()`; the living-enemy count and
`_stall_is_still_cheap()` were missing.

So it counted turns where the party was **not** stalling. Outside a stall,
attacking a stunned enemy is correct play -- the point is to end the fight --
and every one of those turns was being scored as a violation for doing the
right thing.

    before (no stall gate)   75 turns   52.0% attack   37.3% recover
    after  (gated)           56 turns   50.0% attack   44.6% recover

**19 correct attacks had been counted against the policy.** The gap is real
and smaller than reported: 44.6% against an expert "most of them".

**Figures from before this are not comparable** -- the situation being
counted changed, not the policy. The docstring and the printed heading now
both say IN A STALL, so the next reader cannot take the old framing from the
tool itself.

**What this is an instance of.** A ruler exists to grade the policy against
something with no stake in it (`tools/CLAUDE.md`), and its value is entirely
in being right about the situation it claims to measure. A mis-specified one
does not merely lose resolution -- it **manufactures violations**, and those
get written into findings and queued as work. This one had been reporting
3.3 as INCUMPLIDA since the PLAYBOOK was first annotated. The proxy that
remains unverified is `all_stunned` standing in for "X is stunned and will
die to Y or Z"; inside a stall, where `STALL_PHASE_MAX_ENEMIES` usually
leaves one enemy, that is reasonable, and the correction did not touch it.

## PLAYBOOK 3.2 is enforced on the shortlist now (2026-09-20)

Fran, shown the measurement: *"go with the bridge-side shortlist filter for
3.2."*

> *"3.2 Curar -- Sólo en situaciones extremas o en stall. En el resto, jugar
> proactivo."*

`tools/check_playbook.py` on the shipped 1836 checkpoint, 2,469 hero turns:
**35.5% of 299 heals** are neither Death's Door, nor under 34% HP, nor stall
-- 106 turns, **4.3% of every hero turn**. They score **-0.0820** against
**+0.1021** for a justified heal, so the reward separates them by 0.18 and the
policy plays the negative one anyway. No reward knob reaches that.

`Advisor._apply_playbook_32` rewrites the ranked shortlist before anything
reads it, so the printed block, the journal's `decision` event, the agreement
marker and the actuator all see the same move. Putting it in `plan_battle`
instead would have made the log say one thing and the game receive another.

**Three properties it has, each for a reason that cost something elsewhere:**

* **DEMOTED, never dropped.** A hero can be in a position where every legal
  action is an unjustified heal -- a back-rank healer whose attack cannot
  reach. Dropping them leaves an empty shortlist and the actuator with
  nothing to send, which is a worse failure than a wasted heal. Reordering
  keeps a total order and the policy's own ranking survives intact when
  nothing is compliant.
* **It fires only on a case it can NAME.** A heal whose target cannot be
  identified counts as justified: refusing what you cannot see turns a
  seeding gap into a worse turn.
* **The note only speaks when the MOVE changed.** The first version printed
  a line for every demotion -- 11 on the replay corpus -- when only **3**
  moved the head. Demoting an alternative changes nothing that gets sent, and
  saying "demoted" for it reads as an override that did not happen. A log
  that overstates what the bridge did stops being evidence, which is the
  whole reason the line exists (`dd-decide-layer-not-the-policy`).

**The criteria are read from `PLAYBOOK.md`, never imported from the ruler.**
`tests/suite/test_rulers.py` forbids `bridge/` importing `tools/`, and a
ruler the graded thing is built from has stopped being independent. The 34%
is in the spec's own text; `check_playbook` reaching the same number has to
stay a coincidence of both reading it.

### What it does, measured on the capture corpus

`--replay archive/traces/raidsnaps2`, with and without
`--no-playbook-heals`: **3 of 56 combat decisions change**. All three are the
policy's top pick being a heal on a Highwayman at 73%, 67% and 85% HP outside
a stall.

Agreement with the recorded play goes **MATCH 30 -> 29**, and the honest
reading of that is in `tools/CLAUDE.md`: the corpus was recorded while Fran
was *testing the bridge and the live model*, not playing to win, so **MATCH is
agreement, not correctness**. Of the two graded decisions that flipped, one
moved TOWARD the human (was "advised heal, played attack"; now "same kind,
advice pointed at a different slot") and one moved away -- the human healed a
Highwayman above 34% outside a stall, which is precisely the behaviour the
PLAYBOOK calls INCUMPLIDA. A net -1 on an instrument that may not be used to
choose between checkpoints on combat is not evidence against the filter, and
would not have been evidence for it either.

`--no-playbook-heals` is on `run.py`, `advisor.py` and `overlay.py` so the
unfiltered arm is one flag away; that is how the A/B above was taken.

## The 90% window: the banner had no priced answer (2026-09-20)

DD finishes an `explore` quest at **90% of rooms** --
`quest.types.json`'s `explore_all_rooms.data.percentage`, and the objective
text on the HUD says so in words: *"Explore 90% of rooms."* So
`Quest Complete!` is drawn while `RoutePlan.targets` is still full. Observed
live 2026-09-06 at 7 of 8 rooms.

`run.py`'s raid-end branch only ran once `plan.targets` emptied, so for the
whole of that window the actuator had nothing priced and fell back to

    CONTINUE, because the route wants somewhere to go

which is not a decision about the raid at all. The route always wants
somewhere until the map is covered, so that branch continued into a won raid
with an afflicted party exactly as readily as with a fresh one -- it never
asked whether anything left was worth the risk, which is the question
`Layers._raid_end` exists to answer.

`RoomAdvice.raid_end_priced` is that verdict computed anyway. It arms
nothing: only `act._end_raid`'s banner branch reads it, and only when the
GAME has drawn the banner -- which is the game stating the objective is met,
and needs no arithmetic on our side.

**Deriving the 90% rule instead was considered and rejected.** One live
observation (7 of 8 completing, where 0.9 x 8 = 7.2) does not settle whether
the threshold floors, rounds or ceils, and guessing it would move the
raid-end decision on every map. The banner is the game's own answer.

### And the battle-quest half is VALIDATED, which closes the other worry

A raid save carries `raid_instance.battle_all_rooms` --
`{current_battle_count, target_battle_count}` -- the game's own progress
counter. `route.mandatory_targets` does not read it; it re-derives the
objective from the map as "every room with an unspent battle". If the map
demanded more fights than the quest, the bridge would be spending real risk
on fights nothing required.

Measured over the capture corpus: **263 paired (raid, map) snapshots, 263
exact agreements, 0 in either direction.** The derivation is right, and
`test_the_battle_quest_targets_MATCH_the_games_own_counter` re-runs the check
so a change to `_rooms_needing_battle` cannot quietly break it.

## Background play: the gamepad route measured, and closed (2026-09-20)

Fran cannot give up the machine for every live run, so the bridge needs an
input channel that does not own the cursor and the keyboard. Two candidates,
both now measured.

`PostMessage` was already settled in 2026-08-17 and is not worth re-testing:
`WM_MOUSEMOVE` lands and the button highlights, `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` does
nothing, because SDL takes mouse buttons partly from raw input.

The gamepad looked strong on paper: `Darkest.exe` carries an
`alwaysusecontroller` launch option, 104 `controller_` strings and 33 `BUTTON`
mentions across 836 `_PC` help entries, and its `SDL2.dll` contains
`SDL_JOYSTICK_ALLOW_BACKGROUND_EVENTS` -- the documented hint for a joystick
reaching an unfocused window, which SDL reads from the environment.

**It does not work.** `tools/gamepad_probe.py`, measuring the save-row block
of the front end (nothing in it animates, so a parked control reads 0.000):

| game | parked | five D-pad DOWN |
|---|---|---|
| background | 0.0 x5 | **0.0 x5** |
| focused | 0.0 x3 | **2.7, 16.9, 16.1, 16.0, 16.0** |

Everything upstream is fine -- `XInputGetState` sees the ViGEm pad on slot 0
with `A` reading `0x1000`, `app.log` echoes `option: alwaysusecontroller`,
and the game opens the joystick through SDL (`SDL_JoystickIsHaptic=0` on
every connect). Whether SDL ignores the env var or DD gates input on
`SDL_WINDOWEVENT_FOCUS_LOST` cannot be told from outside; the outcome is the
same. `docs/NEXT.md` carries what is left: a second Windows session, or
continuing to work in blocks.

### The probe reported the wrong answer first, and the reason generalises

It read the SCREEN NAME and asked whether it changed. Six `A` presses came
back "screen still title" -- and the game had in fact walked the front end to
the campaign list, drawn in **controller mode with (A) glyphs the banked
references have never seen**. A screen DD draws only for a controller scores
against the wrong reference, so `screen.recognise` mislabelled it, and the
probe's verdict was the opposite of the truth until a frame was actually
looked at.

The primary signal is now a region delta against a parked control, with the
name kept as a bonus. **A recogniser is a confirmation, not a primary signal,
for a screen nobody has banked** -- which is worth remembering for any future
controller work, since every DD screen changes in that mode.

And a negative background result is uninterpretable on its own: "the pad did
not reach an unfocused game" and "the pad does not reach this game at all"
look identical and want completely different work. The probe now runs the
focused control before printing any verdict, which is the same discipline as
the parked control one layer down.

## The raid exit was on the HUD all along, and three torch fixes (2026-09-20)

### RETRACTED the same day: "Continue Raid is a one-way door"

An unattended run pressed **Continue Raid** for a 646-gold deferred tent,
took the tent, walked back to the entrance and could not leave. The probe
that followed -- `W` x5, `A`/`D` as taps and as 0.9 s holds, the map panel on
the goal room and on the party's own room, four clicks around the room art,
and a pause menu that for the tutorial offers no Abandon Quest -- concluded
that the quest-complete banner was the only exit, and `_raid_end` was made to
downgrade every priced "continue" to a "return".

**That conclusion was wrong, and the thing it missed was already in this
repo.** `layout.RAID_ABANDON_QUEST` -- the little scroll with an X under the
quest crest, top left -- has been measured since 2026-09-01, is what
`plan_retreat` clicks inside a fight, and is named by the game's own
`str_help_raid_room_5` / `_hallway_7` for abandoning the quest outside one.
Fran: *"if you continue you can simply click the top left banner icon to end
the raid at any time outside of combat"*. Six probes, none of them the
control the HUD puts the exit on.

The downgrade is reverted. `plan_leave_raid` is the repair: a RETURN with no
quest-complete banner on screen now clicks that banner and confirms, instead
of printing "the quest-complete banner is not up - holding" until the phase
times out. A CONTINUE still requires the banner, because "continue" is only
ever an answer TO the banner. `tests/bridge/test_act.py` pins the two plans
against each other -- they must aim at the same pixel -- which is what stops
the exit being lost a second time.

**The asymmetry in `act.py` was also stated backwards, both times.** It
originally said "CONTINUE, because only the other button throws something
away"; the correction said the reverse. Neither was right: RETURN forfeits
what is on the map and cannot be undone, and CONTINUE costs nothing, because
the banner is still there whenever the bridge is ready.

### A partial snuff was being sent, and a partial snuff is worth almost nothing

`_snuff_torch` required a SAVE WRITE between every reduction click. A snuff is
sent inside a hero's turn, so the next write is the swing that has not
happened yet: the first read came back unwitnessed and the sweep stopped
after ONE click. Live, that left the torch at 20 while the log reported it had
not moved at all -- the game's own tooltip read `DARK ( 20 )` under the
cursor. Fran: *"it only clicks once ... usually you want full snuff of the
torch for best value"*.

The bracket is why one step short is not a rounding error: at 1-25 a fight
pays **+8 treasure draws and +15% gold**, at 0 it pays **+12 and +30%**.

The remaining steps are now sent as one plan and verified afterwards, looped
until the torch reads 0 or stops responding. Bounded three ways: by the torch
level (`ceil(level / 25)`), by `TORCH_MAX_CLICKS`, and -- for the blind case
where the save never answers -- to the FIRST candidate only, which
`_torch_order` makes the remembered winner when there is one. A candidate the
save has REFUTED never gets there.

`test_the_SWEEP_stops_at_the_first_thing_it_cannot_tell` was a source-text
assertion comparing the position of two lines inside `_snuff_torch`. It broke
on this change without the property it guards having moved, so it is now a
behavioural test: a silent save must confine every click to one candidate.

### A relight was undoing the snuff it had just paid for

The torch layer is asked before the loot plan is built and cannot see it, so
out of combat it read "below radiant, torches in the pack" and answered
RELIGHT in the very corridor `lootplan` had snuffed for. Fran: *"when you
beat a battle and immediately after there is a loot curio -- next box on a
hallway, or the curio on the same room, or 1st box on the next hallway -- you
should keep the torch low for that part of the dungeon"*.

`_dark_pays_here` is the guard and it invents no weight: `lootplan` already
records the light it intends for every stop, so a `take`/`defer` stop priced
below `RADIANT_FLOOR` **is** the plan asking for darkness. A relight on top of
it spends a torch to make the next pickup worth less and another to put it
back. Bounded to the area the party is in and the one the route is about to
enter -- darkness outside the killing blow is a straight loss, so this may
never hold the light across the map. `skip` stops are excluded: loot nobody
is collecting justifies nothing.

**And the bridge oracle caught the first version of that rule being too
broad**, which is the first time it has earned its keep on a decision change.
Asking only "is there a dark pickup nearby" held the light in `coHM` for the
heirloom chest in `rooH` -- on the far side of a FIGHT in `rooH`. That walks
the party into a dim combat and breaks Fran's other standing rule, *"try to
never combat on under 75 torch"*, which is the same 76 floor `lootplan` is
built on. The stops are walked IN ORDER now and a `fight` ends the scan, as
does a `relight` the plan has already queued. With that, the replay digest
matches the recording again: the corrected rule changes no decision in the
capture corpus, and fires only where Fran described it.

### "It started a combat at 0 torch" -- two bugs, and the second was silent

Fran reported a fight opening pitch black. Both causes were in the decide/act
chain and neither had anything to do with a missed pixel.

**1. The relight sent ONE `T`, whatever the level was.** One torch restores
25 (`torchlight.TORCH_RESTORE`) and the radiant floor is 76, so 0 -> the floor
is **four** presses. `plan_room`'s relight step was a single
`Step("key", vk=VK_T)` and `RoomAdvice` had no field to say otherwise, so a
party that had just snuffed for a kill walked into the next fight at 25 with
the log reporting a relight had been sent -- and the game agreeing, because
one torch had been used. `RoomAdvice.torch_count` now carries it, capped by
what the pack holds and computed against the FLOOR rather than 100, so it
never buys light nobody needs.

**2. At torch 0 the layer said HOLD, and that is the one that had been there
all along.** `advise_torch` opened with

    if gain_gold <= 0 and gain_draws <= 0:
        return hold("already in the darkest bracket - there is no more loot to buy")

which is a complete answer to *should the torch go DOWN* and no answer at all
to *should it go back UP* -- and it returned before the relight branch could
run. So the deepest darkness, the one case where relighting matters most,
was the one case that never recommended it. Moved after the `not
about_to_win` branch, where it still correctly ends the SHUTDOWN question for
a killing blow in the dark.

Audited over the whole grid of (torch level, torches held): every level below
the floor with a torch in the pack now says RELIGHT, and the only remaining
under-floor results are the pack genuinely running out.

**The replay corpus confirms it against real play.** Three new RELIGHT
recommendations appear in `raidsnaps2`, all at torch 0, where the bridge
previously said nothing -- and one of them resolves **`MATCH torch  the torch
went up`**: Fran relit at exactly that point and the bridge had had no
opinion. Torch-layer agreement 2/3 -> 3/4, total resolved 68 -> 69. The
oracle recording is re-emitted on that basis.

### "rooD is not drawn on the map" is usually the VIEW, and it got no reset

`_handle_room` reopens the map panel when `locate` refuses -- but only for
two of its four refusals. "Not drawn" was excluded, reasoning that an
unscouted room cannot be made to appear by reopening. True, and not the
common case: DD draws every room it has a cell for, unexplored ones included,
so the usual reason a target is not drawn is that the view is zoomed past it,
and the panel keeps its zoom and pan between openings.

Live 2026-09-20 in a crypts raid: `rooD is not drawn` at **5 cells**, the
hover phase confirmed nothing, the click went out anyway and missed, the
frame was banked -- and the reset on the NEXT poll found **12 cells of 12
frames** and the click landed. Four polls and one wasted click, every time,
for a two-keystroke fix that was already written.

Now gated per DESTINATION rather than excluded: the first refusal for a given
target spends one reset, later ones fall through to the hover exactly as
before. That keeps the old property for the room that really is not there --
it costs `MAP_RESET_WATCH` once, never once per poll -- and
`test_a_room_that_is_NOT_DRAWN_costs_at_most_ONE_view_reset` pins the bound
rather than the exclusion.

A naive discriminator was tried first and rejected: "fewer cells drawn than
the save knows tiles" reads TRUE on a perfectly good view, because
`tiles_of` counts every tile in the save and the game draws only the ones it
has cells for -- 8 cells against 56 tiles on `captures/03_ruins_map_panel.png`.

### `_leave_raid` needed its own once-per-raid key

`_end_raid` marks `raid-end` when it answers the banner, CONTINUE included.
Sharing that key would have made a raid that continued for a deferred curio
find the guard already set and refuse to leave -- the exact stranding the new
exit exists to end, re-created by its own guard, and silent. `raid-left` is
separate and a test pins the two apart.

### Keybinds: audited, and already keyboard-first

Checked against Fran's hotkey list. `T` (torch), `TAB` (map/inventory),
`SPACE` (take all), `A`/`D` (move), `1`-`5` (skills) and `W` (interact) are
all already what the actuator sends; the relight has never been a click. The
clicks left in a raid are the ones the game binds to no key: the curio
panel's hand-or-item choice, modal confirms, the map destination, drag-and-
drop, and the torch REDUCTION -- which DD binds to SHIFT+CLICK and
SHIFT+CTRL+CLICK on the meter (`str_reduce_torch_tip`,
`str_snuff_torch_tip`) and to nothing on the keyboard.

## The bridge was blind, and the cause was one missing launch token (2026-09-20)

An unattended run was started to play the game and read out what broke. It
broke immediately, twice, and both faults had the same shape: **a layer that
was working reported a failure somewhere else.**

### 1. The window covered the monitor, so every capture was a LIE

With no `-size`, Darkest Dungeon opens at the desktop resolution. On this
machine that is a **borderless 1920x1080 window covering monitor 1 exactly**,
which is the condition for DWM's independent-flip path: the game is scanned
out directly and the redirection surface GDI reads is never updated again.

`screen.grab` therefore returns one of two things, and the second is the
dangerous one:

* **pure black** (`mean 0`), which `display_is_live` already names correctly;
* **a frozen frame from whenever the window last changed geometry**, which it
  does NOT, because a still frame the recogniser can name is treated as a live
  game -- and that rule is right, it is what stops `raid_results` being read
  as a dead display.

Measured 2026-09-20 at 1920x1080: eight consecutive grabs **byte-identical**,
mean 11.07 every time, recognised as `title` -- while the game was in fact
sitting on the save-select screen, because three clicks the boot had sent had
all worked. The boot read `title` forever, concluded its own clicks had done
nothing, paused, and the night deleted the campaign. **Nothing was wrong with
the clicks, the layout, the recogniser or the display.**

The same run at `-size 1280 720`: `display_is_live` reads "the picture is
changing", frame-to-frame deltas 0.16-0.41, and the boot walks the whole
front end.

### `-size` is NOT ignored. It takes TWO tokens

`tools/overnight.py` carried, in capitals, "**`-size` IS ACCEPTED AND
IGNORED**", measured by passing `-size 1600x900` and watching the client come
up 1280x720 anyway. The plinth option parser splits on spaces like every other
option, so `1600x900` was consumed as one value and dropped. `app.log` echoes
the arguments it got, and that is the whole audit trail:

    option: size
     "1600x900"            <- the old test, one token, ignored
    option: size
     "1280"                <- 2026-09-20
     "720"

With two tokens the window comes up with a **1280x720 client and a border**,
composited normally. `LAUNCH_FLAGS` now carries it. `-fullscreen 0` alone was
never enough; it is the pair that works, and the option table in the binary
(`full_as_borderless`, `fullscreen`, `size`, `window_pos`, `vsync`, ...) is
next to the `SOFTWARE\Red Hook Studios\` key if anyone needs the rest.

**There is no video resolution in any settings file.** The registry key holds
`wposx`/`wposy` and nothing else, `remote/global.json` holds four unrelated
keys, and `persist.options.json` is not written until something changes it. So
the launch flag is not a convenience over the Video menu -- it is the only
handle a program has on this at all.

### `window.nudge_off_monitor` is the recovery for a game it did not launch

A borderless window that covers a monitor can be shoved off the boundary, and
DWM composites it again. Measured: window `(0,0,1920,1080)` captured mean
**0.0**; moved to x=300 the same frame read **11.1** and recognised `title`.
The size is deliberately untouched -- resizing from outside does NOT resize
the render target, it crops it (verified: a 1600x900 window showed the
top-left 1600x900 of a 1920x1080 frame, which would silently invalidate every
fractional layout point).

So `covers_a_monitor` is the predicate, `nudge_off_monitor` is the repair, and
the overnight loop runs it on every launch before it judges the display. It is
strictly second-best to `-size`: it gets one composited frame and does not
guarantee the next one, which is why the launch flag is the fix and this is
the net.

### 2. The named checkpoint slot was stale, so there was no combat policy

`models/dd_combat_ppo_full_expedition.zip` and `..._mods.zip` are **pointers a
retrain is copied into**, and every bridge entry point defaults to them.
2026-09-19 shipped the first 1836-wide checkpoint and repointed `CLAUDE.md`
and `docs/COMMANDS.md` at `models/obs53_worldfix_s0.zip` -- but not the
defaults in the code, which is what `bat/`, `tools/overnight.py` and every
bare `python bridge/run.py` actually use. Both slots still held the 1652 file.

`Advisor` refused it, correctly and loudly, and then said:

> no checkpoint exists at the current width ... until a retrain

which was **false** -- four did. A refusal that misdescribes its own cause
sends the reader to a retrain that has already been run, and meanwhile route,
torch, curio and camp keep deciding, so the night looks like it is working.

Fixed both ways round: the slots now hold the 1836 checkpoint (the 1652 files
are under `archive/models/pre_obs53_2026-09-19/` with their width in the
name), and `advisor.checkpoints_at_width` scans `models/` on refusal so the
message names one that would load and how to promote it. Reading the width out
of the zip's `data` member costs milliseconds per file, so the scan only
happens on the refusal path.

## The new world has a noise floor now, and it is band-dependent (2026-09-19)

`models/obs53_worldfix_s1.zip` -- the seed-0 recipe with `--seed 1` and
nothing else changed. 12M timesteps, 201.5 min, exit 0, peak 7,469 MB against
seed 0's 7,465. Both graded on `--all-comps --episodes 4`, the same 446 comps
and 5,352 episodes.

**Why this run and not the `--equivariant` arm that was proposed first.**
`docs/findings/transfer.md` already records the equivariant head at
**+20.6 vs +21.5 control, 0.9pp** -- a measured null, costing ~13% throughput
and 3.8pp on seen classes, with the transfer question closed after five
mechanisms. Proposing it was a failure to check the verdict first, which is
the rule that exists for exactly this. The seed replication answers a
question nothing had answered instead.

| metric | seed 0 | seed 1 | spread | 2xSE | reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| short succ | 97.4 | 97.0 | +0.4 | 1.24 | within noise |
| short qual | 90.2 | 89.5 | +0.7 | 0.88 | within noise |
| medium succ | 94.6 | 94.4 | +0.2 | 1.84 | within noise |
| medium qual | 85.4 | 85.0 | +0.4 | 1.10 | within noise |
| long succ | 84.1 | 81.5 | **+2.6** | 3.24 | within noise |
| long qual | 76.0 | 73.1 | **+2.9** | 1.72 | **EXCEEDS** |

### Short and medium replicate. Long does not.

Short and medium success moved **0.4pp and 0.2pp** across seeds, so those are
properties of this world and their printed SE is honest -- quote them.

The long band moves **2.6pp success and 2.9 quality**, roughly **2.5x its own
within-run SE**, and long quality's move clears 2xSE outright. That is the
number this run existed to produce, and it was not knowable from either run
alone: the +-1.12 and +-0.53 printed on a single arm describe comp-sampling
error, not how much a different seed moves the whole result.

**Operating rule for this world: on the long band, a delta under ~3pp success
or ~3 quality is indistinguishable from a different seed.** Short and medium
can be read at their stated SE. Anything smaller on long needs two seeds per
arm before it means anything.

It also sharpens the `best_model.zip` result from the same day: that
checkpoint lost the long band by **6.7pp**, about 2.6x the seed spread, so
that conclusion survives the noise floor rather than being explained by it.

**Seed 0 stays the shipped checkpoint.** It leads on all six metrics; five of
those gaps are within noise and none favours seed 1, so there is no reason to
switch and no basis for claiming it is genuinely better.

A second observation, not acted on: `best_model.zip` was stale on BOTH runs --
23:05 against a 23:26 final on seed 1, an hour early on seed 0. Two for two is
not proof, but it is consistent with the selector stopping before the policy
does.

## The first checkpoint at 1836, and `best_model.zip` is the worse one (2026-09-19)

`models/obs53_worldfix_s0.zip` -- the documented recipe, 12,017,664 timesteps
in 2:51:52, exit 0 under `safe_run` (peak 7,465 MB of a 16,268 cap, working
tree matching HEAD). The first checkpoint that loads at the new observation
width, which is the acceptance test for the whole 45 -> 53 payload widening.

### The EvalCallback picked the wrong one, and by a lot

Both candidates were graded on the reproducible ruler -- `--all-comps
--episodes 4`, now **446 comps x 3 bands x 4 = 5,352 episodes** per arm:

| band/metric | final | best_model | delta | 2xSE | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| short succ | 97.4 | 95.7 | +1.7 | 1.35 | REAL |
| short qual | 90.2 | 88.7 | +1.5 | 0.89 | REAL |
| medium succ | 94.6 | 92.3 | +2.3 | 1.98 | REAL |
| medium qual | 85.4 | 83.8 | +1.6 | 1.13 | REAL |
| long succ | 84.1 | 77.4 | **+6.7** | 3.46 | REAL |
| long qual | 76.0 | 71.7 | **+4.3** | 1.72 | REAL |

**The FINAL weights win on every band and every metric**, all six clearing
2xSE, and the long band is not close: +6.7pp success, deaths 0.15 against
0.27, stress 23.0 against 28.1. The independent-SE formula was used rather
than a paired one, so the margins are conservative.

**Why the selection failed.** `best_model.zip` is chosen by the training-time
`MaskableEvalCallback` on a small eval whose spread ran +/-90 to +/-107 on a
mean in the 60s-90s. It locked onto a lucky sample an hour before the run
ended and never beat it, while the policy kept genuinely improving. The
446-comp eval is a far better instrument than the thing doing the selecting,
so **`best_model.zip` is a guess made by a bad ruler** and the final weights
have to be graded against it rather than assumed worse.

`--out` also redirects the log directory, so `best_model.zip` landed in
`logs/obs53_worldfix_s0/` and `logs/full_expedition/best_model.zip` was never
touched. A warning given at launch that `--out` does NOT protect it was
wrong; the pre-emptive archive under `archive/models/pre_obs53_2026-09-19/`
was harmless but unnecessary.

### These numbers are a BASELINE, not an improvement

The winner reads short 97.4% / medium 94.6% / long 84.1% against archived
runs at 94.2/92.3 and 88.2/83.2 -- **and that comparison is invalid.** Four
world changes (`.launch`, `.move`, the sampled-path HP fill, comp pool 176 ->
446) and eight observation features separate this from every
`archive/logs/eval_*.log` figure. Quoting the gap would be exactly the error
`dd-eval-was-not-reproducible` exists to prevent. Whether today's work
HELPED is unmeasured and needs a second arm trained in this world.

`CLAUDE.md` and `docs/COMMANDS.md` had the bridge pointed at
`dd_combat_ppo_full_expedition_mods.zip`, which now refuses with
`Box(1652,) != Box(1836,)`. Both repointed -- a documented command that
cannot run is worse than no command.

## Everything green before the retrain (2026-09-19)

**2894 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed.** The one skip is `test_hot_paths`, which
cannot load a checkpoint at the new observation width and resolves on the
first retrain.

Eight failures were outstanding, seven of them older than this session. None
was fixed by relaxing an assertion -- each turned out to be the test being
wrong about the data, or the loader being fragile.

### A real engine fix

**Sampled parties did not open at full HP** when a hero's own trinkets or
quirks raised max_hp. `apply_trinkets_and_quirks` now keeps a full hero full,
which is the guard the district branch three lines below it already had.
`hp_targets` covered this on the real-party path and is `None` on the sampled
one -- so TRAINING never got the fix -- and it stayed invisible because no
sampled comp had fielded such a hero until the team-builder sync brought a
four-Jester comp whose fourth Jester opened 35/42, every combat, compounding.

### One typo in one mod took down every modded class

The Fury's `poised_cleave` carries `.crit -%` -- a minus with no number.
`float('-%')` raises, and because it runs inside the shared skill build, that
single character made the whole Workshop corpus unloadable: four red tests
and no modded class at all. Two more shapes were behind it, `?1234` (88
occurrences) and `1234.is_user_selected_targets` (79, a missing space gluing
the next key onto the value), both crashing `decode_position_notation`.

`_num` and a digits-only decode fix all three. **Shipped data never needed
either**; they exist so a third party cannot break the loader, the same
reason `_hero_class_map` keeps its hand-written rows first. **220 modded
classes now discover**, which is what the Sibyl comps need.

### Four tests that were wrong about the game

- **The Runaway's burn ladder is 2, 2, 3, 3, 4.** `RW CB Rank Effect 5` has
  `controlled_burn_amount 4`; the test asserted 5, reading the rank in the
  NAME as the amount. Wrong from the day it was written -- the whole ladder
  is now pinned so the next one cannot drift either.
- **Mods extend the town upgrade trees.** `stage_coach.numrecruits` is 5
  ranks shipped and 6 in a Workshop override, `upgraded_recruits` 3 -> 6,
  `nomad_wagon.numitems` 4 -> 5. A live save played with mods legitimately
  holds more purchases than the SHIPPED tree has ranks, and the heirloom test
  read that as corruption. It now takes the max across both roots.
- **`crusader#1295` leaves 6 unnamed trees against a vanilla 4**, and all six
  invert to real crusader camp skills (`zealous_speech`, `stand_tall`,
  `zealous_vigil`, `unshakeable_leader`, `pep_talk`, `first_aid`) -- mods add
  camp skills the store was not loaded with. The count was never the
  invariant; "the leftovers are camp trees and nothing else" was. Asserting
  that directly is stronger and survives a modded save.
- **A class with FEWER skills than slots is not malformed.** The block pads
  and the mask marks the rest illegal, so only MORE is a problem -- which the
  same test already handled correctly one branch up. It was asserting
  "exactly 7" while meaning "at most 7", and `littlepiglet` ships 4.

### Two guards that were measuring themselves

- `test_trajectory_oracle` reported the oracle blind to an action the walk
  could reach any time it tried: both policies were blind (`cycle` walks an
  index, `random` rolls), so coverage was left to luck. **Which action went
  unspent even drifted with unrelated engine changes** -- 27 before the
  `.launch` fix, 36 (PASS) after -- which is the tell. The walk now prefers
  an action nothing has taken yet, still seeded.
- `test_fires_edge` looked its comp up by NAME and **skipped** when it was
  missing, so the re-export renamed it and the test stopped running: green,
  silent, checking nothing. It resolves by shape now and RAISES, because a
  test that goes quiet is this repo's characteristic failure.

## The comp library came from the team-builder, and names are not identity (2026-09-19)

Fran maintains the comps in the `dd-team-builder` app and exported a much
larger library. `tools/sync_preset_comps.py` pulls it into
`metadata/COMPS/curated_comps/`: **178 files -> 477**, and the loaded pool
**176 -> 446**.

**Copied, not pointed at.** The comps are TRAINING DATA, and a world living
in another project's `src/` is one this repo cannot record, diff or restore.

### Matching on the name would have thrown the library away

By NAME the export shared **2** entries with the 177 already here -- it reads
as 175 deletions and 467 additions. By CLASS MULTISET it covers **all 169**
of them, with a median skill-set and trinket overlap of **1.00**. The same
builds, renamed: `Blood Money: Twin Beast` is now `Steady Nerves: Melee`.
That is the third re-export to rename wholesale, and it is why the sync
matches on shape and REFUSES to apply when a multiset here is absent
upstream.

### Two things a plain copy would have broken silently

**`store.comp_loadouts` is keyed by name**, and the export reuses one name
per theme -- 477 files carry 244 distinct names, `Black Humours: Sibyl` alone
appears 11 times. A straight copy loaded 360 comps instead of 593 and lost
233 without a word. The sync now appends the class multiset to any reused
name, and an ordinal where name AND shape collide (6 cases: the same four
classes built two ways, which is legitimate).

**`The_Old_Road.json` is PROTECTED.** It is the tutorial party and the one
comp validated against the game's own shipped save. The export kept its four
Highwayman skills and ROTATED them -- `Open Vein` moved from the first slot
to the last -- and the save carries the equipped slot order, so it silently
stopped matching ground truth and took `tools/eval_tutorial.py`, the one
exactly reproducible ruler here, with it.

### What the suite caught, and what it says about pinning

The sync broke 20 tests. **Every one was pinned to a comp NAME or FILENAME**
-- the failure `tests/_fixtures.py::comp_with_classes` was written for, whose
docstring already records two earlier re-exports doing the same thing. They
are repinned by SHAPE, or made to find their own case where the shape alone
is not the condition (a duplicated class whose members carry different kits).
Three counts were bumped deliberately (pool size 176 -> 446, townplan
reachability 9 -> 10) and one threshold was made relative: `rare` meant
"<= 20 slots", so a corpus 2.7x larger reported that it had FLATTENED when
it had merely grown.

Three comps sit in regions with no enemy pool -- The Hamlet, Darkest Dungeon
III and IV -- and are now declared drops rather than undeclared ones.

**One failure is left red on purpose**: a four-Jester comp opens one hero at
35/42, because `hp_targets` is passed only on the real-party path and never
on the sampled one. The test is right and the engine is wrong; see `NEXT.md`
1c. Silencing it would lose the only thing pointing at a defect that
compounds across every fight of a training run.

Suite: 2885 passed, 8 failed -- two FEWER than the 10 already red before the
sync, since repinning fixed pre-existing pool failures too.

## Seven more mechanics the payload could not describe (2026-09-19)

**This SUPERSEDES the widths in the corpse-clearing entry below.** That one
took 1644 -> 1667 and 1652 -> 1675; the final numbers for the day are
**1828 and 1836**, because the rest of the audit was batched in before any
retrain rather than moving the width twice.

The corpse flag was found by hand. The rest came from running the same
question over the whole record: **which `EffectDefinition` fields does a
shipped effect carry that `_mechanics_from_data` never reads?** 41 of 84,
and once metadata and the fields already covered through `buff_ids` are
removed, 25 describe real mechanics. Ranked by how many HERO skills carry
them, which is what `obs_layout.py`'s own 89.3% analysis did.

Seven were added, all as COARSE flags. That choice is evidence-led rather
than aesthetic: the forecast finding is that a coarse NEW signal pays
(+6.2pp, McNemar p=0.0132) while **every refinement of a signal already
present measured null**. So riposte chance/duration was left out -- riposte
is already v[11] -- and so were the other magnitude refinements.

| idx | feature | hero skills | monster skills |
|---|---|---|---|
| 46 | torch change (SIGNED) | 10 | 11 |
| 47 | summon | 0 | 71 |
| 48 | clears guard | 4 | 14 |
| 49 | clears control (mark / stun / stress DoT) | 3 | 7 |
| 50 | unstealth | 8 | 3 |
| 51 | grants bonus action | 1 | 0 |
| 52 | irresistible DoT | 3 | 0 |

**Torch is signed** because raising and lowering it are opposite decisions;
shipped values run +4..+10 and -5/-10. The torch LEVEL was already a global
feature -- what nothing carried was that a skill CHANGES it, and
`PLAYBOOK.md` 2.3 is entirely about torch.

**Summon is monster-only and is not dead weight.** The payload describes the
ENEMY capability pool as well as the acting hero's slots, and that pool is
the only place the observation can say *the thing in front of you makes more
of itself*. Before this a Necromancer read as a weak attacker. It is
declared in `EXPECTED_UNREACHABLE` beside `kill`, which is unreachable by a
hero for exactly the same reason.

**Bonus action is thin** -- one shipped hero skill -- and was included anyway
because action economy is the largest single lever in a fight and because a
modded class carrying it now arrives described instead of invisible. That is
the entire argument for a payload over a class one-hot, so applying it only
to mechanics that are already common would be applying it backwards.

### A hole in an existing channel, closed at no width cost

`.buff_type`/`.buff_amount` is a THIRD inline-buff mechanism, distinct from
`buff_ids` and from the specific-field pattern, and `_effects.py` has applied
it all along while this builder never read it. 29 effects: Arbalest's
stacking heal (`hp_heal_received_percent`) and the Grapeshot / Expose crit
vulnerability (`crit_received_chance`).

Both map onto buckets that already exist -- `healing` and `dmg_taken` -- so
this is not a new feature but a **hole in the channel the payload says
carries 60.7% of its signal**, and it closes without touching the width. Four
hero skills gained visible buff signal.

### What it costs, and what the guards caught

**Every feature was checked to fire on real data before being kept** -- a
permanently dead feature is pure width, and `obs_layout.py` already declines
to encode one (`monster_type_condition`). All eight are live.

Two guards caught incomplete work, which is the argument for having them:

- `test_the_feature_names_match_the_declared_width` -- `FEATURE_NAMES` was
  short of the declared width, twice.
