- `test_every_payload_feature_is_reachable_by_some_hero_skill` -- flagged
  `summon` as hero-unreachable, which is true and is exactly the question
  worth being forced to answer out loud rather than assumed.

`tests/suite/test_hot_paths.py` still skips its fast-path check: no
checkpoint loads at 1836. Dormant until a retrain, and recorded here rather
than left to be noticed.

Suite: 2884 passed, 1 skipped, and the same 10 already red on this branch.
**Nothing has trained against any of this.** The features are present, not
proven -- and they cannot be ablated on an old checkpoint either, because no
old checkpoint loads. Only a retrain at 1836 says whether the width was
worth it.

## The policy could not see which skill clears a corpse (2026-09-19)

The same shape as `.launch` and `.move` one layer further out: parsed
faithfully, resolved by the engine, and never described to the thing that
needed it. `EffectDefinition.is_clear_corpses` is read by
`data/effect_loader.py` and applied by `_effects.py:552`. The observation
never carried it -- `clear_corpse` appeared nowhere in `env/`.

**The policy was told a corpse is a problem and given no way to know which
button solves it.** It already saw the per-enemy `is_corpse` flag, the rot
timer, the blocking-corpse count and a `W_BLOCKING_CORPSE` shaping term. The
one missing link was skill -> clears.

Four hero skills carry it, and each read as its OTHER effect:

| skill | payload before | read as |
|---|---|---|
| Leper `focus` | 15, 30 | a push and a stat buff |
| Occultist `daemons_pull` | 16 | a pull |
| Plague Doctor `disorienting_blast` | 0, 14 | a stun and a shuffle |
| Runaway `ransack` | 16, 17, 30 | a pull and a stat buff |

Nothing distinguished them from an identical skill that leaves the corpse
standing, so the link was learnable only as a `(class, slot)` pair -- the
exact memorisation the 45-float payload was built to remove, per
`obs_layout.py`'s own rationale -- and a modded class with a corpse-clear
skill transferred nothing.

### The loud break was chosen over the silent one

`SKILL_MECHANIC_FEATURES` 45 -> 46, appended at `v[45]`, so every index below
still means what it meant -- the discipline burn and the heal headroom were
added under.

**There was no spare index to reuse**, and that is worth stating because the
first reading of the layout said there was: 0-17 are the flags and
magnitudes, 18-41 the two stat-bucket banks, and 42/43/44 are limited-use,
kill and damage-blocks. 42 looked free from the arithmetic alone and is not.

Had one been free, taking it would still have been the WRONG choice. The
width would have kept matching, the shipped checkpoint would have kept
loading, and it would have been fed a dimension whose weights saw nothing but
0.0 in training -- **the silent break**, which `CLAUDE.md` names as the
dangerous one precisely because nothing announces it. Widening announces
itself immediately.

### What it costs

**1644 -> 1667 and 1652 -> 1675**, +23 each: 7 skill slots, 4 heroes x 3
capability pools, 4 enemy slots. `tests/suite/test_obs_width_tripwire.py`
carries the new numbers and the reason.

**Every checkpoint trained before today stops loading.** That is the intended
consequence, not a regression. One visible effect in the suite:
`tests/suite/test_hot_paths.py` now SKIPS its fast-path check --

```
checkpoint does not load against this observation:
  Box(-inf, inf, (1652,), float32) != Box(-inf, inf, (1675,), float32)
```

-- through a `pytest.skip` that was written for exactly this case, so it is a
designed path rather than an accident. It is still **a test that has stopped
running**, which is this repo's characteristic failure, so it is recorded
here: that guard is dormant until something is trained at the new width.

`tests/env/test_skill_mechanics.py::test_the_feature_names_match_the_declared_width`
caught the half-done change immediately -- `FEATURE_NAMES` had 45 entries
against a declared 46 -- which is the cheap guard doing exactly its job.

Suite: 2884 passed, 1 skipped, and the same 10 failures that were already red
on this branch. **Nothing was retrained**, so no measurement here is
comparable with anything before it.

## The unread-key audit is code now, because as prose it went stale (2026-09-19)

Three gaps of one shape were found in a single session, each a field the game
ships, the HERO side reads, and the monster side did not:

| field | scale | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| `.launch` | 228 of 694 enemy skills usable from forbidden ranks | fixed |
| `.move` | 101 of 552 skill lines with a self-move nothing applied | fixed |
| `shared_health` | The Flesh: four bars where the game has one, ~4x too durable | documented, queued |

All three were found by reading community guides and noticing the simulator
disagreed. **None was found by the audit that exists to list exactly this.**
`ASSUMPTIONS.md` has carried an unread-monster-key audit for a long time and
is accurate about every key it lists -- `shared_health` was simply never on
it, and nothing noticed for the life of the project. Prose does not re-run.

`tools/monster_key_audit.py` enumerates every `(record, field)` the monster
files carry and diffs it against every field name `data/`, `engine/` and
`env/` mention. **115 pairs over 28 record types: 68 read, 47 declared-unread
with a reason each, 0 undeclared.**

### The sweep found nothing else of that severity

Which is the reassuring half, and worth recording so it is not re-run on a
hunch. The 47 unread keys classify into four groups, and the question that
collapsed most of them was simply *who carries this?*:

- **Corpse semantics (7 flags, 8 records each).** `can_be_random_target`,
  `is_valid_friendly_target`, `can_be_summon_rank`, `can_relieve_stress_from_
  killing_blow`, the two `..._for_monster_brain` flags and
  `does_count_towards_stall_penalty` carry ONLY on `corpse` and
  `corpse_large`. The engine models a corpse as its own thing -- `is_alive()`
  is False for one -- so it is already out of the stall count, the
  random-target pool and the guard pool without anyone reading the flag that
  says so. Honoured by construction.
- **Subsumed and already validated.** `personality: .prefskill` is on all 254
  monsters and was in no doc at all -- but it is `-1` (inert) on 227, and all
  21 non-inert carriers have brain `skill_desires`. Selection is governed by
  the brain, which `tools/brain_oracle.py` grades **46/46** against the
  game's own weight vectors. Nothing to fix.
- **Declared exclusions.** `captor_empty`/`captor_full` (Hag/Siren capture),
  `shape_shifter`, `companion`, `controller` -- all already in
  `ASSUMPTIONS.md`. `skill: .is_knowledgeable` is the Ancestor forms and the
  Collector, i.e. the Darkest Dungeon and roaming bosses, both declared
  absent entirely.
- **Non-combat.** Backdrops, turn-order icons, loot bookkeeping, trinket
  targeting metadata, animation sequencing.

The only item left with real weight is `battle_modifier:
`disabled_act_out_combat_start_turn_types`, on 13 bosses: it suppresses some
affliction act-outs at combat start. Act-outs ARE modelled; this narrowing of
them is not. Small, boss-only, and now on the list rather than invisible.

### The weakness worth knowing about

A key counts as read when its field name appears anywhere in the source.
That is deliberately generous -- it cannot raise a false alarm, only miss
one. **A field that is parsed and then never consulted reads as handled**,
which is exactly how `.launch` hid: `data/` decoded it faithfully into
`sd.launch` and `_monster_ai.py` never looked at it. So this tool finds keys
nobody has looked at and cannot find keys someone parsed and then ignored.
For those the instrument is `tools/pizzarat_oracle.py`, which grades
behaviour against an independent transcription instead of counting coverage.

`tests/suite/test_monster_key_audit.py` holds three checks: every key is read
or classified; the audit still goes red when a declaration is removed (the
`--self-check` question, asked of a guard that would otherwise read as
reassurance); and no declaration names a key the game no longer ships, since
a stale allowlist is how the prose version rotted.

## Monsters never moved: `.move` was parsed for heroes and hardcoded to 0 (2026-09-19)

The second half of the same bug as `.launch`, found the same way and on the
same day. `.launch` says where a monster may act FROM; **`.move` says where it
goes afterwards**, and the enemy skill builder in `data/hero_loader.py` passed
`move_self=0, move_target=0, push=0, pull=0` as literals. The execution path
never applied movement either, so both layers were missing it.

**101 of 552 monster skill lines carry a net self-move, across 71 monsters**
(18.3%). The Fuseman falls back three ranks lighting its fuse; the Cutthroat
lunges forward one; the Necromancer retreats one after every action.

**The convention is `.move <backward> <forward>`**, so `move_self = back -
fwd` and a positive value retreats -- the same parse the hero path at
`hero_loader.py:360` has always done. Confirmed against four independent
cases rather than assumed, because an inverted sign would have every monster
walking the wrong way and still looking plausible:

| monster | skill | raw | net | reads as |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Necromancer | `unholy_smite` | `.move 1 0` | +1 | the guide's "moves back with every action" |
| Brigand Fuseman | `light_fuse` | `.move 3 0` | +3 | lights the fuse and falls back |
| Brigand Cutthroat | `bandit_stabby_weak` | `.move 0 1` | -1 | lunges forward |
| Brigand Fusilier | `rushed_shot` | `.move 1 0` | +1 | fires from rank 1, then retreats |

### The two halves together are what makes disruption TEMPORARY

This is the part that matters for play, and neither half worked before today.
`rushed_shot` is `.launch 1 .move 1 0`. Rank 1 is the only rank the Fusilier
can fire it from, and firing it puts it back in rank 2 -- where
`blunderbuss_shot` (`.launch 432`, an AoE) is available again:

```
fusilier rank 1: usable=['rushed_shot']
fusilier rank 2: usable=['blunderbuss_shot']
```

So "shut it down by moving it to position 1" buys **one turn**, not the
fight. Had `.launch` landed alone, the shutdown would have been permanent,
which is a different game -- a good reason to fix a field and its counterpart
together rather than one at a time.

### A lone monster still cannot move, and that is the Necromancer's mechanism

`_shift_to_position` is a permutation over occupied ranks, per Fran's rule:
*"a single enemy can't be pushed as there isn't enough positions to rotate, a
displacement always keeps the gaps"*. `.move` is a displacement like any
other, so a lone monster stays put however often it uses a retreating skill.

That is not an edge case here, it is the mechanism: all three Necromancer
skills carry `.move 1 0` **and** `NecroSummon 1`, so the summons are what
give him something to rotate behind. The guide describes the summons and the
retreat as one strategy and the data agrees. Pinned by
`test_the_necromancer_retreats_behind_what_he_summons`.

Applied in `_finish_enemy_action`, the single hook all three enemy action
paths (self-target, friendly, enemy) already meet, and applied **hit or
miss**, matching the hero path where `_apply_movement` sits outside the
`if result.hit` guard. Called ONCE per action rather than once per target;
the hero path calls it inside its target loop, which walks an AoE user one
rank per target. That is a separate pre-existing bug and was not copied.

### What it moved

**Suite: 2876 passed.** Two tests failed that are not caused by the change
and one that was already red went green:

- `test_gaps.py::test_clear_corpses_removes_corpse_in_target_ranks` indexed
  `enemies[0]` -- a LIST index -- while asserting about a POSITION. `reset()`
  resolves a surprise action before the board is readable, and once
  `brawler_claw`'s `.move 0 1` advanced the actor, the corpse was no longer
  on the rank the effect targets. The test now pins the rank, which is what
  it always meant.
- `test_bridge_oracle.py` compares a recorded replay digest, and 6 of 8 cells
  changed with identical line counts. Attributed by stashing the fix and
  re-running: without it the digest reproduces the recording exactly
  (`3d75b051...`), with it the replay is `48070622...` and deterministic
  across fresh processes. Re-emitted, which is what that test's own text
  prescribes for an intended change. Previous digests, so the baseline can be
  audited or restored:

  ```
  raidsnaps2/plan   3d75b051cff1ed9487928a05a62e20e2 -> 48070622663b044f2f94747e338f8ef0
  raidsnaps2/model  29304820b22afc301d7dd73b7aade600 -> 067da8030d67c487a75dee450fa48300
  raidsnaps3/plan   d5ed9c139f27a08afe6607fa9d64f1b1 -> 98c7039cca4918484f8e7cdc0856f638
  raidsnaps3/model  77d03a768a61e3a33e569de83a35630a -> 4de4cc832343b8d949ab9de79f3da77d
  raidsnaps4/plan   abdcb3b8f041ebca6a3dd29c93b074b2 -> e23efac2b70fcfeba9af0c5da8aab597
  raidsnaps4/model  898fc65967100d6a21d37e0a5161d80e -> 8713417566a0e53de9f809a40513f0de
  ```

  `raidsnaps` (9 lines) did not change. The `.launch` fix earlier the same
  day changed none of them, which is why this is attributable to `.move`
  alone.
- `test_trajectory_oracle.py::test_the_walk_takes_every_action_the_env_allows`
  had been red on this branch since before the session and now PASSES. Not
  investigated; noted because a guard going green on its own is worth
  knowing about rather than discovering later.

**This is the second world change of the day.** Enemy positions now move
during a fight, so pre-2026-09-19 measurements are not comparable across it
and nothing here was retrained.

## The region heirloom bias is shipped data, and the Cove half is a myth (2026-09-19)

`data/heirlooms.py` could already price a building upgrade and solve the
trade that closes a shortfall. What it could not do is answer the question
before it: **where do I go to earn the heirloom I am short of?**

`loot/loot.json` answers it. Every heirloom entry carries a `dungeon` and a
`chances` weight, and the table is small enough to quote in full:

```
dungeon    bust  deed  portrait  crest
cove          1     1         1      3
crypts        3     1         1      3
warrens       1     1         3      3
weald         1     3         1      3
```

**The community claim is right for three regions and misleading for the
fourth.** Ruins->busts, Weald->deeds and Warrens->portraits are real: each
elevates exactly one heirloom to 3 against a baseline of 1. But **crest is 3
in every region**, so "Cove -> crests" names the universal baseline rather
than a Cove speciality -- the Cove is the one region that elevates nothing.
A router that believed the guide would send a party to the Cove for crests it
would have got anywhere, which is the kind of wrong that never announces
itself.

`regions_for()` therefore returns a LIST, and returns all four for crests.
That is the honest answer and the reason the signature is not "the region".

**The tier collapses, and only because the weights say so.** Each (dungeon,
heirloom) row repeats once per difficulty tier with an IDENTICAL `chances`;
it is `amount` that scales -- bust 2/3/4 and crest 4/6/8 at tiers 1/3/5. So
`heirloom_bias` takes `max` rather than summing, and
`test_the_heirloom_weights_do_not_vary_by_difficulty_tier` fails if a future
patch makes the weights tier-dependent, which would silently turn that `max`
into a wrong answer rather than a loud one.

**Not wired into the embark decision.** `PLAYBOOK.md` 1.2 fixes three
mission-choice criteria in a stated order, and inserting a fourth is a change
to Fran's spec rather than a data fix. The data and the query are available;
what weight an heirloom shortfall carries against the existing three is his
call. Queued in `NEXT.md`.

## Enemies ignored their own `.launch` ranks, so disruption did nothing (2026-09-19)

**One third of every enemy skill in the game was usable from a rank the game
forbids.** `.launch` is the rank a combatant must STAND IN to use a skill.
`engine/action.py:47-51` and `combat_engine.py:1179` had enforced it for
heroes since the beginning; `_enemy_skill_weights` never read it. Its usable
loop filtered on per-battle limits, valid mode and cooldown, and stopped
there.

**The Bone Courtier is the clean case.** Its game file is unambiguous:

```
skill: .id "tempting_goblet" .type "ranged" .atk 92.5% .dmg 2 4 .crit 0%
       .effect "Stress 2" .launch 432 .target 1234 .is_crit_valid False
skill: .id "dagger_jab" .type "melee" .atk 62.5% .dmg 2 4 .crit 6%
       .launch 21 .target 12
```

`.launch 432` means the stress attack cannot be thrown from rank 1. A lone
courtier stands in rank 1. Two hundred draws from `_select_enemy_skill`
before the fix:

```
skills chosen from rank 1: {'dagger_jab': 94, 'tempting_goblet': 106}
```

After: `{'dagger_jab': 200}`.

**Scale, measured against the loaded store**: 228 of 694 enemy skills are
launch-restricted (33%), spread over 113 of 298 enemies (38%), and **23 of
the restricted skills are stress attacks** -- the Crone's `fetid_censer`
(`.launch 12`), the Cultist Witch's `cultist_pull` (`.launch 234`), the
Drowned Pirate's `offkilter_jig`, the Brigand Sapper's `sapper_stress`.

**Why it mattered beyond fidelity.** Shuffling a stress caster out of its
firing rank is the standard answer to backline stress, and it was a no-op
here: the courtier kept throwing goblets from rank 1 forever. The policy
could not learn disruption because the environment did not implement it, and
every shuffle skill and shuffle-based comp was underpriced by exactly that
much. `PLAYBOOK.md` section 5 already lists "combates con shuffle" as an
expected weakness; this is at least part of the mechanism.

The data was parsed correctly the whole time -- `sd.launch == [4, 3, 2]`,
matching the file. Only the read was missing. It was found by decoding the
rank notation on a community reference (`pizzarat105.github.io`, which
renders launch/target as `<target>ally_<launch>enemy` icons) and checking one
disagreement against the game files.

**A size>1 monster passes if ANY rank it occupies is in `.launch`**, mirroring
how `.target` already resolves against its whole footprint in
`_execute_enemy_skill`. The hero-side shape (`position + 1 in launch`) is
wrong here: the big adder's `molt` is `.launch 23` and it keeps it while
standing in ranks 1-2, because its rear rank is 2. Both directions are
pinned by `test_launch_filter_covers_multi_rank_monsters`, using the
Swinetaur (`pig_spear`/`bull_rush` at `.launch 34`, barred at the front,
which is what makes it the "free stalling" enemy the guides describe).

**21 (enemy, rank) pairs are now fully blocked** -- `drowned_anchor` off rank
1, `formless_melee` in the back, `formless_ranged` at the front. That is
correct: a Formless Ranged shoved forward genuinely cannot act, which is why
shuffling it is worth doing. Both callers already handled `sd is None` by
passing the turn, so no new path was needed.

### Two tests were passing on luck, and this is what exposed them

Neither is caused by the fix; both used a **combat-contaminated quantity** as
a proxy for a reset-time charge, and `ExpeditionSequenceEnv.reset`
auto-resolves every enemy turn ahead of the first hero. Changing which skills
those monsters pick moved the seed search onto a contaminated seed.

- `test_the_EVAL_path_does_not_fire_the_penalty_that_TRAINING_does` asserted
  `stress_dmg_received_mult == 1.0`, under a header comment claiming the
  field is "written once at reset and combat cannot touch them". **That was
  already false**: `_buffs.py:377` and `:490` add to and subtract from it.
  Measured at baseline, with the fix stashed, **3 seeds in 120 come back with
  `level_shortfall == 0` and a moved mult**. The fix surfaced a 4th, seed 4.
- `test_the_sequence_envs_OPENING_combat_is_charged_too` asserted
  `stress == UNDERLEVELED_STRESS_PER_LEVEL` exactly -- and the sibling test's
  own comment warns that a first attempt at that shape "failed on seeds where
  a monster simply got there first".

**`level_shortfall` is the sound discriminator**: `combat_engine.py:418` is
its only writer, so nothing in a fight can move it, and the mult is derived
FROM it at line 417 -- a charge cannot leak into the eval path without the
shortfall assertion firing first. So the shortfall assertions were kept as
the guard, the mult is now checked only on seeds combat left alone, and the
stress assertion became `>=`, which still catches the regression it was
written for (the penalty not applying at all, i.e. stress 0).

`test_cultist_brawler_uses_both_skills_not_just_first` was rewritten rather
than relaxed. It asserted that a lone rank-1 brawler produces BOTH
`brawler_claw` (`.launch 21`) and `brawler_claw_weak` (`.launch 43`) -- two
disjoint sets, so no rank offers both, and it only ever passed because the
filter was missing. It now checks each skill at a rank where it is legal,
which still proves the original regression (a skill past `skills[0]` is
reachable) and pins the launch split as well.

**Suite**: 2863 passed, 11 failed -- the same 11 that were already red on
this branch, verified by stashing the change and re-running them. One of
them, `test_trajectory_oracle`, reports a different unspent action with the
fix (`[36]` against `[27]`); it was red either way.

**This changes the world every checkpoint trained in.** Enemy behaviour is
different now, so SUCCESS rates shift and pre-2026-09-19 measurements are not
comparable across it. Nothing was retrained here.

## The map panel was hiding the curio panel's X (2026-09-07)

Three attempts at `locked_strongbox` in `rooA` all reported
`no panel on screen after W`, the curio was given up on for the raid -- and
ninety seconds later the cleanup path printed
`a curio panel is still open - closing it at (1008, 339)`. **Two readers of
one screen disagreeing is the tell**, and there were two separate faults
behind it.

**1. The panel animates in, and `_work_curio_panel` looked once.** A single
grab straight after `W` catches the frame before DD has drawn anything.
`CURIO_PANEL_READS` is 4 looks `PANEL_WAIT` apart, ~1.8 s; the cost of an
extra look is a screen grab and the cost of one too few is a curio abandoned
for the whole raid.

**2. `_red_blocks` clustered on X ALONE.** It sorted every red pixel in the
right half by x and cut where the gap exceeded 20 px -- so the curio panel's
X merged with whatever red sat under it, and something always does: the map
panel's quest tokens and the raid pack's red trinkets are both right-half and
both overlap the panel's x range. The merged block spanned y 295 to 700, its
aspect collapsed, the size filter threw it out, and `find` reported "no red X"
at a panel that was plainly open.

Fran opened the panel by hand so it could be measured: **144 red pixels in a
28x28 box at (974, 308)**, clearing every filter -- colour, width, aspect,
mass -- and no block offered. So it was never the dark and never the
thresholds.

`_split_by_row` cuts each x-group again wherever it jumps in y, by the same
20 px. A strict refinement: a group already tight in y comes back unchanged.
`_in_map_panel` was the earlier attempt at this problem and cannot work from
where it stands -- it judges the CENTROID of a cluster that has already been
merged, so the answer it is given is about neither blob.

**Two tests changed rather than being made to pass**, and both got more
honest:

* the no-panel control now yields a `hand=None` result instead of `None`,
  because the split separates a second blob the merge had hidden. The contract
  is restated where every caller actually reads it -- **nothing CLICKABLE** --
  since `_work_curio_panel` and `_dismiss_stray_curio_panel` both gate on
  `readable`, which needs the hand's gold plate two pitches left.
* the button-floor test measured the SMALLEST red fragment in a real frame and
  required it to clear the floor. That was only true because the merge had
  swallowed the fragments; it now measures the block `find` would keep.

**Still open, and it is Fran's next ask:** *"it should open inventory and
check for the key"*. With the panel detected, a curio that wants a provision
reads its own item slot as `empty or greyed out` -- the slot is a DROP TARGET,
and the key is in the raid pack, whose panel is not even the one on screen
(the map was). The save knows exactly which slot holds it (`inventory.read_pack`)
and `layout.inventory_point` knows where that slot is drawn, so nothing has to
be guessed -- but the backend has no right-click verb yet, and Fran's
constraint is precise: *"we can't spam out as right clicking on an inventory
provision will waste it if it isn't the right one"*.

## Gathering the loot after the quest is won (2026-09-07)

Fran, on the first real crypts raid: *"the bridge has opted to dodge the loot
and successfully completed the mission, now it should click continue the
mission and gather the loot at minimal torch for most value"*. Four things
stood between it and that, and the first three are all the same shape.

**1. The Quest Complete banner is a MODAL, and nothing treated it as one.**
`run.py` only decides return-or-continue once `plan.targets` is empty, and
DD's own objective completes at 90% of rooms (`quest.types.json`,
`explore_all_rooms.data.percentage`) -- so a map with a room still to open
finishes the QUEST while the route is still planning. Live: 7 of 8 rooms
explored, `Quest Complete!` drawn over the map, and the route layer spent
every poll clicking `rooF` **through** the banner and reporting that the game
offered no move. Third time this session that an unanswered modal read as a
dead input path, after the hunger check and the exchange overlay.

`_end_raid` now runs on every room update and answers the banner itself when
`run.py` has no opinion -- **CONTINUE, and only while the route still has
somewhere to go.** That inverts `_raid_end`'s "leave by default" on purpose:
that rule is about a map with nothing left on it, and here the bridge has
already decided it wants to be somewhere. Of the two buttons only RETURN
forfeits anything. It is best-effort like the hunger read, because it now
looks on every update and a frame it cannot parse must cost nothing.

With that in, the raid did exactly what he asked: Continue, walk to `corE`,
snuff to torch 0, take the last fight in the dark, and start pricing the
strongbox in `rooA` at 571 gold.

**2. The loot walk aimed at the head of its own plan.**
`[a for a in loot.walk if a != here]` is a filter, not a position, so a party
halfway along was always sent to the plan's first entry -- which is behind it.
`rooC -> corE -> rooC -> corE`, twenty seconds a lap. It takes the step after
the LAST occurrence of `here` now; last, because a deferred curio's revisit is
the walk naming one area twice, and the first occurrence is not where the
party is.

**3. ...and the plan itself changed its mind.** Fran diagnosed this one
outright: *"wants to go left side room, model detects downside room on the map
first, and goes back like it was wrong the first time, both ways are good in a
sense as both have loot, but it needs to maintain the pathing consistent to
not loop"*. The plan is rebuilt every poll from wherever the party now stands,
so two comparably priced stops swap places as it walks.

`Layers.loot_goal` holds the chosen stop and `_loot_outstanding(prefer=)`
returns it while it is still outstanding and still worth something. **Not a
tie-break** -- a tie-break still flips when the numbers cross. It costs
nothing real: the two were within a few gold of each other, and walking back
and forth between them is worth less than either. The hold never revives a
curio the actuator gave up on, which is checked, because that would restore
the exact stall `abandoned` was added to end.

**4. A room curio got exactly ONE press.** The cap was `not corridor`, argued
from "in a room there is nowhere to walk" -- true, and beside the point: the
retry is not for walking, it is for the panel taking a moment to be drawn.
Live in `rooF`, with the quest done and three keys in the pack:
`no panel on screen after W`, given up on for the whole raid on the first
attempt, and a curio panel closed by the cleanup two minutes later -- so it
did open, after the one look. `ROOM_CURIO_TRIES` is 3: nothing is being
approached, so a panel that has not appeared by the third look is not coming.

### Not every curio is a loot curio

Fran, the same evening: *"healing or boosting curios like the fountain should
be used during the dungeon, as they aren't loot curios, same for curios that
mostly produces provisions like torches, food or blood"*.

The deferral rule -- hold a curio back and open it at torch 0 -- was decided on
POSITION alone: an area the walk home passes through anyway. It never asked
whether the dark pays anything for this particular curio, and both halves of
the answer were already in the data:

* **`Option.loot_chance`** is the share of a curio's outcomes that draw loot at
  all, and the darkness bonus is an extra draw on a draw. `holy_fountain`
  reads **0.50** bare-handed and **0.00** for its holy water -- half of it is a
  heal or a buff, worth most while the party is hurt and worth nothing banked
  at the entrance.
* **`LootValue.supplies`** is new, and it is the half that was missing: how
  much of a draw's gold is really `supply`/`provision` items. `sconce` prices
  at 5 gold and **all five of them are a torch**. A torch taken on the way home
  is never lit. It shadows `gold` rather than adding to it, so `worth()` is
  unchanged and every existing caller sees the same number.

`pays_for_the_dark` requires an interaction that draws loot on **every**
outcome and whose loot is not mostly provisions. The first half needs no
threshold -- it is a structural fact about the curio. The second is a half,
because "mostly" is Fran's own word. Measured against the live map:
`holy_fountain` False, `sconce` False, `shambler_altar` False,
`travellers_tent_tutorial` True, `locked_strongbox` True, `tutorial_shovel`
True -- and the Old Road acceptance test, which turns on deferring the tent,
is untouched.

Worth flagging rather than hiding: **`tutorial_food` prices out as
deferrable** (97 gold of which 12 is food, so 88% ordinary loot). Its name
says provisions and its table says otherwise; the rule follows the table.

## The hunger check has two looks and the answer had no focus (2026-09-06)

Fran, mid-raid: *"right now the bridge is stuck on the hunger step"*. The
modal handler shipped earlier the same day and it failed twice over on its
first real dungeon, at torch 11 in the crypts.

**1. DD tints the whole scene by the light level, and the recogniser hashes
the whole frame.** The banked `hunger.png` was taken in the lit tutorial; the
crypts panel at torch 11 scored **0.459** against it, which is not a match by
any margin this recogniser uses. So `_answer_hunger` read "not hunger" and
returned, and every layer below spent its retries against a modal that takes
all input -- the curio approach burned all ten of its presses on a tent that
was never the problem.

`name@variant` is the shipped answer to a screen with more than one look, and
`hunger@dark.png` is the second look: self 0.001, nearest other 0.677.

**The GEOMETRY did not move, only the tint.** Measured on the dark frame, the
existing `HUNGER_EAT` lands at the peak of the food box's gold (warm-lit
fraction **0.335**, and a row scan peaks at the same x) and `HUNGER_REFUSE` on
the other one (**0.091**). So Fran's rule -- *"It should click always the
yellow box if possible"* -- picks the same answer at torch 11 as at torch 100,
and the constants are untouched. That is why this is a banked variant rather
than a second pair of points.

**2. `Actuator.execute` does not check focus, and this path does not go
through `_send_and_confirm`, which is where that check lives.** The panel was
recognised, both boxes measured, both answers "sent", and the panel did not
move -- because the clicks went to whatever window was in front, which during
a supervised session is the operator's own terminal. `_answer_hunger` now
takes focus first and **refuses rather than sending blind**: a click into
somebody else's window is worse than no click. It still takes the poll, since
the modal is up either way and nothing below it can act.

The three tests that already existed covered detection, geometry and the two
ways of doing nothing -- the same shape of gap that let a `NameError` ship in
the clicking half a few hours earlier. The new pair covers the dark frame and
the unfocused window, both of which only occur against a live game.

## The save lags, and three layers read that as "it did not happen" (2026-09-06)

First real expedition after the tutorial -- crypts, explore, difficulty 1, the
post-tutorial party. It played 34 graded decisions and came home, and the same
root cause bit three separate layers on the way.

**`persist.raid.json` is written per TURN.** That is the acknowledgement
channel the whole actuator rests on, and it is exactly wrong for the two
things that happen where there is no turn: a torch relight in a corridor, and
a snuff sent inside a hero's turn before the killing blow.

**1. The relight was reported missed and had landed, twice over.** `T` at
torch 70, three attempts at 15 s each, all three "the input did not land",
`giving up on this turn`. Fifty-eight seconds later the marker printed
`MATCH torch the torch went up` and the torch read **100**. One torch restores
25, so a single press caps at 95: **at least two were spent**, and the retry
is what spent them.

`_send_and_confirm`'s retry rests on a stated rule -- *"Re-sending is only
safe when the plan means the same thing the second time -- a keystroke does, a
coordinate measured off a panel does not."* `T` is the exception, because it
CONSUMES something. Torch actions are now sent with `attempts=1`, the same
rule the SHIFT-CLICK discard already follows: an input that destroys something
is sent once and reported, never re-sent on the word of a channel that has not
answered.

**2. The snuff sweep refuted all eight candidate widgets and learned
nothing.** At torch 70, mid-fight, `layout.TORCH_CANDIDATES` was walked end to
end -- sixteen live clicks -- and each one reported `did not move ... is not
the torch widget`. Every read happened inside the same silent save.

`_torch_settled` returns `(level, witnessed)` now, and `witnessed` is the half
that did not exist:

* the torch **moving** is a confirmation;
* the save being **rewritten with the torch unchanged** is a refutation;
* the save **not moving at all** is neither, and the sweep stops there and
  says so.

Sixteen live clicks become one. **The geometry question is still open** and is
now labelled as open rather than answered wrongly: nothing in that run
established which of the eight is the widget, and a previous session's
"70 -> 45 while every candidate was refuted" is withdrawn as evidence -- the
torch decays as the party walks (`tile_light_loss`, 1 per tile lit and 6
dim), so a drop across a sweep is not a click landing. **The experiment that
settles it** is to bank a raid HUD frame and find the torch widget by eye,
which nobody has done because the sweep was believed to be answering.

**3. `W` through a door was reported as a closed door.** `at the end of corA -
W into rooB` -> `the door did not open ... stepping back and trying again` x3
-> `still in corA after 12 steps each way - stopping` -- and twelve seconds
later the party was standing in `rooB`. Same lag, same misreading, and the
step-back is worse than doing nothing because it walks the party away from a
door it has already gone through. **Not fixed here** -- unlike the torch,
`W` costs nothing to repeat, so the retry is merely wasteful rather than
destructive, and the fix wants the same `witnessed` distinction rather than a
longer timeout. Recorded so the next person does not diagnose the door.

### The survival layer's verdict did not need the policy's permission

Fixed earlier the same day: a RETREAT verdict was carried to the actuator by
scanning the policy's shortlist for a candidate holding `RETREAT_SLOT`. That
was written against a live turn whose alternatives happened to include
`RETREAT on self  7%`, and it reads as though the shortlist were the menu of
legal actions. **It is the policy's top four, and the policy is the half that
has already been outvoted.**

Measured two combats from the end of this raid:

```
-> BATTLEFIELD MEDICINE on rank 2 (Vestal)   99%
   alt: EMBOLDENING VAPOURS / MOVE to rank 3 / BATTLEFIELD MEDICINE
2 combats left: P(finish intact) 33% +-14, expected deaths 0.7
leaving costs ~4,333 gold, staying ~-2,031 - RETREAT
```

Six consecutive polls printed RETREAT and every one of them healed. No
candidate carried the slot, so the scan fell through in silence.
`plan_battle` now plans `plan_retreat` directly when the shortlist has none,
and the game still gets the last word: it refuses without a calibrated
control, exactly as any other missing button does.
`test_only_a_BATTLE_can_reach_the_retreat_plan` keeps its structural guard --
out of combat the same click throws the whole expedition away -- and now
admits both battle-path callers by name.

## The overlay the Hamlet is drawn under (2026-09-06)

The tutorial finished, and Fran asked to carry on into the post-tutorial
phase: the coach visit, then the first real embark. It went wrong in a way
worth keeping, because two of the three faults were the SAME fault the file
already documented, one branch and one function further down.

**1. `do_exchange` never closed the panel it opened.** The heirloom exchange
is not a building panel -- it does not replace the Hamlet, it is drawn ON it.
So every guard in `visit_stage_coach` reads correctly and says nothing is
wrong: `recognise` still returns `hamlet`, `_on_town_screen` is True,
`_panel_is_open` is right that no building panel is up. And the building
scene points are underneath a live widget.

The live sequence, first post-tutorial visit: buy `numrecruits.a` (confirmed,
crest 24 -> 20, deed 10 -> 7), close the panel for the trade, trade 3 crest ->
1 deed (confirmed, crest 17 deed 8), then click the stage coach's scene point
-- which landed on the still-open exchange panel's header. The re-open could
not confirm, and the two hire drags went out anyway.

The close is a `finally` in `do_exchange` rather than a line before each of
its six returns, because the paths that would get missed are the failure
paths, and those are exactly the ones that leave the overlay up. It is the
same toggle that opens it, and `_exchange_spinner` is the acknowledgement --
no save is written when a panel closes.

**2. The re-open failure `break`s where its sibling `return`s.** Twenty lines
above it, the misfired-trade branch carries this comment:

> **Return, do not fall through to the hires.** Breaking out of the purchase
> loop alone left the coach panel closed [...] and the hire drags then went
> into a live Hamlet. Every one was correctly reported UNCONFIRMED, and none
> of them should have been sent at all.

The re-open branch did the thing that comment forbids. Identical consequence,
measured the same evening. Fixed the same way, and
`test_a_reopen_that_did_not_confirm_sends_NO_HIRE` pins that the visit stops
there rather than carrying on.

**3. A leftover overlay survives the visit that made it.** The next run of the
same command opened with a blind click into the panel the previous run left
up, and banked the frame that proves it -- Trade Heirlooms over the Hamlet,
crest 3 showing, the coach's scene point inside the panel's title bar. So both
coach entry points (`visit_stage_coach` and `recruit_all`) now shut the
exchange before the first scene click, the way they already refuse to re-open
a building panel that is already open. `test_BOTH_coach_entry_points_shut_it`
exists because a fix applied to one of two identical blocks is a shape this
repo has hit before.

With all three in, the visit ran clean: numrecruits.b, 2 portrait -> 3 deed,
rostersize.a, and both recruits -- six of six confirmed against the save,
roster 2 -> 4.

**The design that made this cheap to find is the save-acknowledgement rule.**
Nothing inside a building panel changes the screen, so every action is checked
against the wallet or the roster. Both bad visits reported UNCONFIRMED on
everything that missed and claimed nothing that had not happened.

### And the fallback seated the party backwards

`bridge/embarkplan.py` falls back to "the four lowest-stress heroes" when no
curated comp is fieldable, and it used `enumerate` on that sort as the
formation. On the first post-tutorial roster that is **Plague Doctor rank 1,
Vestal rank 2, Crusader rank 3, Highwayman rank 4** -- the healers in front of
the front-liner, and precisely the arrangement `viable_ranks` exists to call
illegal (Vestal and Plague Doctor 2-4, Crusader 1-2).

It is not an edge case: no curated comp is fieldable from a four-hero roster,
so this branch is the ONLY path the whole early campaign takes.

`best_formation` maximises skills launchable in place over all `n!`
assignments -- 24 for a party of four -- with ties keeping the order handed
in, so the stress sort still decides between two equally good formations and
the result is deterministic. On the live roster it returns **Crusader 1,
Vestal 2, Highwayman 3, Plague Doctor 4**: it reproduces common knowledge from
nothing but the shipped `.launch` lists, which is the same check
`CompSpace.viable_ranks` passes and the reason to believe it measures the
right thing.

**The derivation is duplicated on purpose and pinned against its twin.**
`tools/comp_ga.py::CompSpace.usable_from` is the search's copy and `bridge/`
should not import a 3,600-line search to answer one question about launch
ranks. So `launchable_from` is fifteen lines in the planner, and
`test_the_derivation_AGREES_with_the_search_on_every_class` asserts the two
agree on every class at every rank, and that the viable-rank sets they imply
are identical. Two copies of one rule is how this repo has twice ended up with
two answers; a test is cheaper than a shared import here, and it fails loudly.

## Four ways to stand still, found by driving one tutorial (2026-09-06)

Fran plugged the monitor back in and asked for the whole thing to be run.
The bridge booted a fresh campaign, fought both Old Road battles, took the
first battle's loot, skipped the trapped chest, correctly refused to bank the
quest while a deferred curio stood on the map -- and then failed to get home,
four separate ways. Every one of them was a layer that KNEW the answer and
could not spend it, which is the shape this project keeps finding.

**1. The heartbeat had a bound, and past it there was no `else`.**
`STUCK_SECONDS` fires only while `_battle_skip < MAX_TURN_SKIPS`. Past that
the branch did nothing at all -- and `take_replan` is the only thing that
dispatches on a save that has not moved, which is every poll of a stuck raid.
The fight stopped for six minutes with the party at 13/23 hp.

The cruel part is what the log's last line said:

```
the game is asking the crusader and the save says highwayman
    - not sending; asking about the other hero on the next poll
```

`note_turn_missed` records that class BEFORE it checks the budget, so the
bridge knew who the game was waiting on and the gate that would have used it
had already closed. `note_turn_stuck` resets the walk and re-opens the gate,
rate-limited by the clock rather than by a count: a recovery that refuses
before it sends anything cannot make a raid worse by being tried again in
half a minute. **A retry budget is a bound on guessing; it must never become
a bound on trying.**

**2. Solving the skip moved the deadlock instead of ending it.** With the
class named, `battle()` found the crusader -- and published him as "after 1
enemy turn -> Crusader", because `enemies_first` comes off the save's turn
queue, the one quantity here that is KNOWN to lag. The actuator then refused,
correctly: a timing refusal must never fall through to another candidate.

A hero is not asked for input while enemy turns are pending, so the queue and
the panel cannot both be right and the screen wins. `enemies_first` is zeroed
when the panel named this very hero -- gated on that, because zeroing it
unconditionally would act a turn early, which is the bug the provisional label
exists to prevent. It also un-sticks the dedupe, since `enemies_first` is in
the turn key. The next turn landed and the fight was won.

**3. THE WALK HOME WENT THE WRONG WAY, and the loot layer is why nothing had
met it before.** Sent back through `coAB` for the deferred tent, the party
pressed `D`, then `W`, and arrived in `rooB` -- the room it had come from --
then oscillated, ten torch a lap.

`_walk_toward` derives its direction from `_walk_progress`, which cross-checks
`areatile` against the dark run `Area.standing_on` reads. **A corridor already
walked dark end to end leaves no run to read**, so that returns None and the
direction falls back to `D`. The door logic at the bottom of the same method
rests on the derivation having happened -- its own comment says `W` at the
arrival gate "cannot arise here ... the direction was derived from which end
the party entered by". It arose, because **a deferred curio is the first thing
in this project that ever sends the party back through a corridor it has
already cleared.**

`raid.last_room_id` is the game's own answer and needs no inference: the area
the party came from is the end it entered by, so a target there is behind it.
`route.read_last_area` reads it; it does not saturate, because the game writes
it rather than leaving it to be inferred.

**4. A deferral was made that could not be kept.** Standing in `coAB` with the
objective met and the tent underfoot, the plan deferred it again -- and the
collector loop walks `homeward[1:]`, so a party already in the last corridor
gets no return stop. The plan told `run.py` to walk past the curio for a trip
that was not in it.

What makes this hard to see is that **the deferral had been correct one area
earlier**; what expired is the return leg that justified it, and nothing
re-examined the promise when the route under it changed. `plan_loot` now ends
with a self-consistency pass: every `defer` either has a `take` for the same
tile later in the plan, or it was never a deferral and is re-priced at the
light it will really see. `tests/bridge/test_lootplan.py` asserts the invariant from
all three Old Road positions -- skip means never, defer means not at this
light, and a plan must not make a promise it cannot keep.

**What is still open.** The snuff never fired, and correctly: `about_to_win`
needs one living enemy whose hp is at or under the acting hero's damage FLOOR,
and the last blow of battle 2 was 4 hp against a floor of 3. So the torch
actuator remains unproven live -- the sweep in `bridge/act.py` has still never
been reached in a fight. That is a gate question, not a geometry one, and it
should not be loosened without deciding whether "likely to kill" is good
enough to go dark on.

## Two adjacent lines of one block disagreed, and the party went home (2026-09-06)

Found by re-reading the same live log, not by a new run. Verbatim, from one
`Layers.block` on one state:

```
LOOT    346g + 0.02 bust + 0.10 crest + ... still on the map
        then torch  70  skip in rooB tile0 (bandits_trapped_chest)
RAID    RETURN  - objective met and nothing left is priced positive
```

The party walked out leaving a curio it had **deliberately deferred**, which is
the one thing a deferral must never do: `lootplan` only defers because the walk
home comes back through that corridor, so banking the quest early turns "worth
three times as much later" into "lost" -- strictly worse than never having
deferred it at all. Reported by Fran as *"it went back to the hamlet"*.

**Cause: `_raid_end` could not see the layer printed one line above it.** It
reads `plan.detours` (from `route.py`) and `mine` (curios underfoot), and
`route.optional_targets` excludes corridor curios **by design** -- its own
docstring says a corridor is a property of the route rather than an alternative
to it. So a tent standing in a hallway is invisible to both, which is precisely
the gap `bridge/lootplan.py` was written to fill. The loot plan was computed in
that same block, printed, and then not consulted.

Two halves, and the second is easy to forget:

- **`_loot_outstanding(loot)`** -- the most valuable `take` or `defer` still
  standing. A `skip` buys nothing, because that verdict means never at any
  light; keeping the two words distinct is what makes this decidable at all.
  It is asked AFTER the affliction / Resolve-Check gate, so loot can never keep
  a party that has gone wrong in the dungeon, however rich the curio.
- **The loot plan supplies the WALK.** With the objective met `plan_route` has
  no targets, so `next_step` is empty and a "continue" is a decision with no
  way to act on it -- the party stands in the room agreeing it ought to go and
  fetch something. That is the `_LayerDone` rule in a new place: a layer that
  *can* act blocked by the layer that usually moves having nothing to say. The
  route still owns movement outright while the objective is unmet, because a
  detour taken before the quest is won is a different and much worse trade.

Checked against the shipped Old Road from all three positions: standing in
`rooA`, `coAB` or `rooB`, the outstanding item is the tent at ~646 gold and the
first area ahead is the hallway it is in.

## The torch snuff, and two ways to refute a working click (2026-09-06)

The one open defect from the loot layer's first raid. Fran, asked whether the
meter is interactive during a fight: *"the meter is interactive, if you spam
shift+click on the torch icon the torch will lower, I think it also works for
shift + T"*. So the gesture and the widget are both fine, and the failure is
entirely on our side of the wire.

**Three causes, and none of them could be told from the others.**

- **`MOD_DWELL`.** The modifier was held **20 ms** before the button went down.
  SDL takes SHIFT and CTRL from its own key events, so a modified click only
  reads as modified if that keydown has been *processed* — and one frame at
  60fps is 16.7 ms. The dwell sat inside the margin. It is 120 ms now.
  Generalises past the torch: **no modified click in this project has ever been
  proven to land**, which `_discard_slot` already says about SHIFT-click
  discarding in its own docstring.
- **Reading the save too eagerly.** A raid save is written per TURN. A torch
  reduced between turns is not on disk 0.35 s later, so one read afterwards
  records a *working* widget as a dead one. That is the cheapest possible way to
  throw away the right pixel, and it may well be what happened to
  `TORCH_METER` — which was refuted on exactly one read.
- **The pixel was measured when it could have been derived.** `screen.raid.darkest`
  ships `flamepos 960 100`, `mouseOverAreaSize 200 130` and a
  `stripMouseOverArea` — and says nothing about what any of it is anchored to,
  which changes the answer by 100 design px in x. Both readings are now offered
  and the game picks, verified against `torchlight` per attempt.

**What is settled offline:** `TORCH_FLAME` samples `rgb=(247,160,54)` on
`captures/01_0_Starting_room.png` — the click *was* on the lit flame. So the
geometry was probably never the problem, which is why the two refuted points
stay at the HEAD of the sweep: if one answers now, the dwell was the bug. The
log names the winner either way.

**Method note.** Nothing here is verified live yet, and that is deliberate
rather than incomplete: the display was unplugged mid-session. Two of the three
fixes can only be confirmed by the game, so they ship as a sweep that *reports
which one worked* rather than as a claim that any of them did.

### ...and "the game is not running" was a third thing again

`run.py --boot` refused with *"the game is not running, or there is no
calibrated layout"* while the game was running and the layout was fine. With no
display attached `Darkest.exe` keeps its top-level windows and every one of them
reports an **empty client rect**, so `find_window` — which wants something
drawable — returns None, exactly as it would for a game that had never started.

`window.diagnose(process)` separates the three (on screen / not running /
running but owning nothing drawable) and says that the last one's remedy is
physical, so nobody retries it. Same family as `screen.display_is_live`, one
layer earlier: that one tells two *capture* faults apart, this one tells an
*enumeration* fault from absence.

## The loot layer's first live raid (2026-09-06)

Driven on a fresh campaign the bridge created itself, in slot 0, because every
save had been deleted. Four defects, three fixed and verified live in the next
raid, one isolated and still open.

**The plan never included the area the party was STANDING IN.** The outbound
walk started at `outbound[1:]`, so a party in a corridor was told neither to
fight the battle beside it nor to defer the curio under its feet -- and the
deferral is the whole point of the layer. Live on the Old Road it opened the
tent at torch 94 for 190g instead of deferring it to torch 0 for 558g. It hid
behind the entrance being empty: from `rooA` there is nothing to lose, and
every offline test planned from a room.

**...and the walk home only collected what had been DEFERRED.** An area the
outbound pass never reached has nothing to defer -- there was no "now" to weigh
against -- so a party in `rooB` with the tent still standing in `coAB` planned a
run worth 454 instead of 1186. The homeward sweep now takes anything still
standing in an area it passes.

**The post-battle loot window was being closed as a stray curio panel.**
`_dismiss_stray_curio_panel` ran before the `_was_in_battle` block, and
`curiopanel.find` matches the loot window -- same red X, same gold hand plate.
The log read "a curio panel is still open - closing it", then "SPACE (take all)
after the fight", then "15 slots free - there was no loot window, nothing to
take". Reported live by Fran as *"it refused to take the loot from the first
battle"*. After a fight the modal IS the thing we want, so the loot is taken
first and anything still standing is dismissed after.

**OPEN: the torch snuff sends, and the meter does not move.** The DECISION is
right and now fires for the right reason (`battles_left=0`: "no fight left
after this one -- the light would never be bought back, whatever the pack
holds"). The INPUT does nothing. `TORCH_METER` was banked and, by its own
comment, never sent; the frame it was measured on has the brazier's dark base
at y=151 and the lit flame at y=111, so `TORCH_FLAME` was added and
`_snuff_torch` now tries both and names the one that worked. **Neither works.**
Both landed (client 640,76 and 640,56) and the torch sat at 70 through a full
SHIFT+CTRL snuff and a SHIFT step at each.

It is not clicking in general and not the coordinate: the very next line of the
same log is `CLICK 912,365 enemy rank 2` -> `confirmed by the save after 7.8s`.
So it is the MODIFIED click specifically, or the meter is not interactive
during combat. Two candidates, one experiment, and the two-target probe is
worth keeping either way: it converted "the snuff silently did nothing" into
"both of these points are not the widget", which is the difference between a
mystery and a question.

**And two more tests turned out to read the developer's own save directory.**
`test_menus.py` asked the disk how many campaigns exist (three flow tests went
red the moment the saves were deleted), and `test_hero_skills.py` asserted an
invariant taken from Fran's Week-22 profile: that a hero's camp trees are the
leftover UNNAMED hashes in `persist.upgrades.json`. A campaign created minutes
ago names them -- `crusader.encourage` sits in `purchases` beside
`crusader.smite` -- so nothing is left over. Both readings are the game's, and
a bridge that makes its own saves now produces the fresh one constantly, so the
assertion is the weaker true one: the leftovers are camp trees or there are
none.

## The route planner reads the loot tables (2026-09-06)

Three shipped files priced what `bridge/route.py` had always said could not be
priced, and two real bugs fell out of wiring them in. Full account in
`BRIDGE.md`; this is the evidence.

**`loot/loot.json` was invisible to a `loot/*.loot.json` glob.** The main
table file is `loot.json`, which does not match that pattern, so the first
version of `data/loot_loader.py` loaded only the three `*_overrides` files and
reported every code as unpriced. It is the failure mode `CsvLoader.find_csv`
already warns about -- a missing file here is a zero, not an error -- and the
only defence is a test asserting the tables arrived. There is one.

**A corridor's endpoints were taken from its NAME, and on a generated map the
name is wrong.** `DungeonMap.graph()` has derived connectivity from tile
geometry since the phantom-hub bug, but `Area.rooms` was left as the raw split
and `route.plan_route` was still using it to decide which way to leave a
corridor. Measured on `maps/tutorial_crypts.dm`: `corE` really joins
`rooC`-`rooF`, and the name split answers **`rooE`** -- a room that exists and
is not an end of that corridor, so the wrong answer looks entirely plausible.
`DungeonMap.corridor_rooms()` is the same answer `graph()` uses, made
reachable.

**The shutdown was priced in the wrong currency.** `advise_torch` charged the
torches needed to relight, so a party holding none could never snuff -- and
the Old Road hands out no torches at all, which makes the tutorial's single
best play unreachable. A relight buys light for a FIGHT; with no fight after
this one there is nothing to buy, whatever the pack holds. `battles_left=0` is
the repair.

**...and `battles_left` is NOT `remaining_battles - 1`.** Deriving it inside
`advise_from_live` re-broke a bug this module had already paid for: that
counter is the fights the SAVE knows about, and an unexplored area can hold
more. Two existing tests caught it immediately
(`test_one_fight_left_is_not_a_free_shutdown_while_there_is_dungeon_to_walk`).
Only a caller holding the map can answer it, so `run.py::_battles_left` counts
unresolved battle tiles -- which the save carries for every area from turn
zero, scouted or not.

**Three bugs in the planner itself, all found by running it rather than by
reasoning about it**, and all now pinned:

* an exact cover doubles back through cleared areas, so events were resolved
  twice and the loot counted twice;
* a DEFER marked resolved on the outbound pass made the walk home skip
  everything it had gone back for -- about 2,000g on one captured map;
* the darkness bonus was credited to interactions that draw no loot at all,
  which valued the Old Road's `Blight 1` trapped chest at +165g and made the
  planner take it bare-handed;
* and planning from a CORRIDOR returned an empty cover, because `_all_pairs`
  BFSs room to room and a corridor is not a node in it -- so the block read
  "0g still on the map" for the whole length of a hallway, which is where the
  party spends most of a raid. Found by replaying a capture through
  `bridge/run.py` rather than by a test, which is the argument for replaying
  one after every change to this layer.

**And deleting the saves turned three `test_menus.py` tests red, which is a
defect in them.** `menus.total_save_rows()` counts the campaigns in the real
save directory, so `plan_for("save_select", slot=1)` with no `total_rows`
silently asked the developer's own disk -- and those tests passed only because
two campaigns happened to be on it. Starting a fresh campaign, which is the
ordinary thing to do here, produced a refusal that says nothing about the flow
under test. Pinned by an autouse fixture that overrides **only the default
root**: replacing the function outright hides a regression in it, and
immediately did.

**What it is worth, measured.** On the shipped Old Road, with no torches and
no key, the plan comes out at 1,012g plus heirlooms: fight lit in the hallway,
defer the tent, snuff before the killing blow of the guarded chest (+454g on
that fight alone), skip the trapped chest, take the tent at torch 0 (+558g
against 190g lit), finish at the entrance. **That is the route Fran described
in prose, produced by an optimiser that was never told it** -- which is the
acceptance test in `tests/bridge/test_lootplan.py`.

## THE SAVE KEEPS THE MARCHING ORDER, NOT THE FORMATION (2026-09-05)

Two unattended iterations of `tools/overnight.py` ended the same way: `play
ended: stalled 180s`, 25 inputs sent and 3 landed, the party standing in a
Crypts fight nobody was playing. The log said the same thing forty times:

```
[act] [battle] Plague Doctor: NOXIOUS BLAST on rank 1 (Cultist Brawler, 15 hp)
  KEY  4      select slot 4
  CLICK   812,365   enemy rank 1
[act] no save change in 15s - the input did not land (attempt 1/1)
```

**The frame it banked is the whole diagnosis.** The Plague Doctor's four skill
icons were drawn *greyed*, and slot 5 (MOVE) was lit. The game was refusing the
digit, so the click landed with nothing selected. Her kit launches from ranks
2, 3 and 4; she was standing at rank 1.

And the save said she was at rank 2. `persist.raid.json` read
`party.heroes = [2, 37, 15]` -- shieldbreaker then plague_doctor -- for the
whole fight, while `layout.hero` (rank 1 is the rightmost, closest to the
monsters) put her plainly at the front of the screen.

**The same plan had WORKED one round earlier**, which is what makes this a
staleness bug and not an inverted convention: at R3 the identical Plague Doctor
/ slot 4 / rank 1 plan was `confirmed by the save after 1.8s`. Between R3 and
R4 the party was reordered -- she is `masochistic`, and `change_pos` is one of
that trait's act-outs -- and nothing wrote it down.

### Proved by making it happen

Through the actuator, on the live game: select MOVE, click the ally rank. The
save then reported the mover's `turns_taken` advanced from 3 to 4 and one of
the three enemies dead, so it had certainly been written. `party.heroes` still
read `[2, 37, 15]`.

The whole raid save was walked for a field that does carry it and there is
none: `battle.initiative` is ordered by initiative, `battle.enemies` is the
monster side, and the roster records no position at all.

### The ordering itself is right, and that had to be checked first

The obvious reading of the frozen frame is "index 0 is the REARMOST", which
would have been a one-line change and wrong. Scored over the capture corpus --
118 snapshots where a hero is due to act, asking only whether that hero has any
skill it could launch from where each convention puts it:

| reading `party.heroes` | legal skills | acting hero had NONE |
|---|---|---|
| front-first (index 0 = rank 1) | 389 | **0 / 118** |
| back-first | 244 | **31 / 118** |

Every disagreement is one shape: a Vestal at index 3 of a four-hero party,
handed the turn by a game that would not have let her do anything. So index 0
is rank 1, the bridge's convention has always been right, and the defect is
that it goes stale.

### The repair reads the game's own answer -- `bridge/actionbar.py`

Darkest Dungeon draws an unusable skill through a **grayscale shader**, so the
discriminator is not a threshold anybody had to choose. Measured at the six
calibrated `layout.skills` points on a Shieldbreaker standing at rank 1:

| slot | skill | `.launch` | drawn | saturation | p95 brightness |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | pierce | 3,2,1 | LIT | 0.583 | 187 |
| 2 | adders_kiss | 1 | LIT | 0.162 | 136 |
| 3 | expose | 3,2,1 | LIT | 0.351 | 139 |
| 4 | single_out | 3,2 | **GREY** | **0.000** | 56 |
| 5 | MOVE | | LIT | 0.125 | 120 |
| 6 | PASS (the X) | | LIT | 0.302 | 112 |

**Four predictions from shipped `.launch` data against four icons on a real
frame, and nothing was fitted** -- that agreement is the evidence that this
reads legality rather than brightness. Both frames are tracked
(`captures/battle_bar_*.png`) at half resolution, which is not a saving dressed
up as a principle: the verdict is identical at both sizes *because* a greyed
icon is exactly zero, so interpolating grey with grey stays grey.

Wired in three places, each with a rule:

* **`plan_one_move` refuses a greyed slot**, which makes it a SCREEN refusal --
  so the shortlist walk `plan_battle` already had finds a skill the game will
  take. The timing refusals are untouched and still refuse outright, because
  every candidate for a turn that is not due shares them.
* **`plan_reposition` is the rescue** for the case that has no alternative: the
  4-bit lit/greyed signature recovers where the hero really is, and the swap
  goes to the nearest rank it could launch something from. On the frozen frame
  that answers rank 1 and picks rank 2 -- exactly the swap that unfroze the
  live game by hand.
* **`read` returns `None` when it cannot see, and `None` is "no opinion".**
  `plan_battle(update, resolved)` with no bar plans byte-identically to before,
  which is what keeps `--replay` over the whole capture corpus valid.

`_reset_formation` -- the click on the game's own "Default Party order" after a
fight -- was already there and is the right repair *between* fights. This is
the one *during* a fight, where that button cannot be used and the save cannot
be trusted.

## Endless mode: battles held before the party loses someone (2026-09-03)

Fran's idea: *"what if another metric is how many battles can the model hold
without someone dying, what if instead of dungeons of X battles, we go endless
essentially until the model loses someone?"*

**SUCCESS had run out of resolution and this says exactly where.** Shipped
checkpoint, 24 runs, cap 40 combats:

| still whole after | 8 | 12 | 20 | 30 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| | **96%** | 92% | **54%** | 33% |

Median **22 battles** before a death; 19 of 24 runs lost someone, range 7-38.
A real expedition is 3-18 combats, so **every eval this project runs sits in
the 96-92% region -- the flat top of the curve.** The discriminating region is
12-30 and nothing currently visits it. That is the whole reason SUCCESS reads
96.4 / 94.7 and cannot resolve an improvement.

**It should also be where the expert tier list finally pays.** A stress heal, a
buff, a control skill and an HP-conserving line all cost a turn now for value
later: over eight combats that value barely arrives, over forty it is the whole
game. `tools/style_probe.py` and the tier list have said for weeks that the
policy under-uses exactly those; every explanation has come back null, and the
fight-length one was retracted the same day it was written. This gives the
prediction a metric with room to show.

Three choices that are load-bearing:

- **`allow_abandon=False` is the exploit being closed, not a default.**
  `expedition_sequence_env.py`'s own notes record a probe that abandoned 62% of
  runs "at a mean 61% party HP and **0.05 deaths**". A metric counting battles
  survived without a death is trivially maximised by quitting at combat one.
  The report shouts if any run abandons anyway, so re-enabling it cannot pass
  quietly.
- **Censoring is why the headline is Kaplan-Meier and not a mean.** A run that
  reaches the cap says the party lasted AT LEAST that long: averaging it in
  biases the answer down, dropping it biases it up. Censored runs stay in the
  denominator for as long as they were at risk.
- **Only the LENGTH is overridden.** Comp, zone, tier, gear, torch and
  provisions are the ordinary sampled draw, and provisions are deliberately NOT
  topped up, so the run is a test of attrition. That makes the absolute number
  depend on `LENGTH_BAND_PROVISION_MULT`, which is a simulator convention.

**A comparative ruler, and unusually so.** The policy trains on 8-18 combat
expeditions and its value function assumes an end, so past that it extrapolates
and a low number can mean "never trained for this". Unlike fight length it can
never be validated against real play either -- Darkest Dungeon has no endless
dungeon. Ruler only, with the usual import guard: training for a mode the game
does not have is precisely what `tools/check_class_play.py`'s rule exists to
prevent.

Noticed and unexplained: **2.83 retreats per run.** A retreat does not advance
the combat count (a fled room is re-taken), so it is not a way to survive the
metric.

## The fight-length gap did not survive being controlled (2026-09-03)

Written and retracted the same day. The claim was that simulator combats run
**2.0 rounds against real play's 3.9**, with 9.2% lasting past round 3 against
50% — and it was read as the structural explanation for the policy passing over
buffs, control and stress heals: in a two-round fight none of them can repay
the turn they cost, so the policy would be CORRECT about the world it trained
in while the expert tier list is right about a different one.

**It compared two samples that differed in three ways at once.**

| arm | combats | mean rounds | >3 rounds |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — sampled tier and band (the 2.0) | 357 | 1.92 [1.8, 2.0] | 6.7% |
| B — tier 1 / short / crypts+weald / **4 heroes** at rank 0 | 119 | **2.68 [2.3, 3.1]** | 19.3% |
| C — the same comps cut to **2 heroes** | 100 | **4.19 [3.4, 5.2]** | 47.0% |
| real, **4 heroes, nobody fell** | 7 | **3.00 [2.3, 3.9]** | 28.6% |
| real, 2 heroes | 1 | 4.00 | — |
| real, a hero fell mid-fight | 2 | 6 and 8 | — |

Matched on tier and party size the two agree: **3.00 [2.3, 3.9] against 2.68
[2.3, 3.1]**, overlapping across most of their width. The 3.9 pooled 4-hero
combats with a 2-hero one and two short-handed ones, and set that against an
arm pooling every tier that never lost anybody. **Both differences push the
same way**, which is what made the gap look like a fidelity defect rather than
an artefact.

Party size is the effect that was hiding inside it — 2.68 → **4.19**, the
largest single number here — and the "independent replication" of 3.9 from an
agent recording was a **two-hero tutorial party**, which arm C puts at 4.19.
It replicated party size.

**The reconciliation is withdrawn**: why the policy skips buffs, control and
stress heals is unexplained again, and `tools/style_probe.py` and the tier list
still disagree with it with no structural excuse. What stays open is much
narrower — n=7 cannot exclude a modest difference — and it wants a bigger
recording, not an engine edit.

**How the arms are built, and why each choice is load-bearing.**
`bat/advise.jsonl`'s `raid_started` events name the world exactly
(`{"dungeon": "crypts"|"weald", "difficulty": 1, "length": 1}`), so it is
reproduced rather than approximated. Parties come from whole curated comps in
their written ORDER — `CombatEngine.reset` assigns `position = index`, so
filtering heroes out and closing ranks moves everyone behind the gap into ranks
their skills may not launch from, and the comps that survived that first
version were the degenerate ones (four Runaways), because a comp made entirely
of in-set classes is rarer than a comp with four of them somewhere. Kit is kept
and only resolve and gear are forced to 0: a bare class list understates damage
and lengthens fights, which is the direction that would flatter the claim under
test. Resolve and gear have to be set explicitly because the five randomisation
knobs, `resolve_sampling` among them, are unreachable from an explicit
scenario. Arm C cuts arm B's own comps to their front two, so B vs C is party
size and nothing else.

**Two counting traps, both pinned.** The round counter alone merges two
consecutive one-round fights (`1 < 1` is false), which drops a short combat and
raises the mean — the direction that flatters the claim — so a wholly different
enemy set ends a combat too. And the simulator side takes boundaries from
`_combat_idx`, which is exact, rather than inferring them the way a journal
must.

**THE INTERVALS ARE OVER COMBATS, AND COMBATS CLUSTER.** They are bootstrapped
as if each fight were independent, and they are not: fights share an episode,
and episodes share a comp, and comp identity moves fight length a lot at rank 0
(four Runaways is not four Crusaders). Measured: arm B at 40 episodes reads
2.68 [2.3, 3.1] and the same arm at 5 episodes on another seed reads **1.60
[1.3, 1.9]** — two intervals that do not overlap for one arm. So every interval
here is too NARROW, and the right reading of the headline is direction and
magnitude, never a decimal.

It does not change the conclusion, and it is worth being explicit about which
way: wider intervals make the matched arms agree MORE, not less. What it does
rule out is quoting 2.68 against 3.00 as though the difference were resolved.

**Every arm now carries a bootstrap interval, including the real one.** Ten
combats cannot support a bare mean and this comparison was quoted as one for a
day. The >3 SHARE is the headline because it is what PLAYBOOK 3.5's rule turns
on and it survives a small sample far better than a mean over a long-tailed
count.

## The live-bridge session that unblocked itself (2026-09-03)

Fran: *"let's try to work on that longer recording session for yourself, do
everything that you need to do to reach that"*. Getting there meant fixing four
things that only fail when the bridge is actually driving a game, plus
retracting one fix of my own.

### The black capture was the WINDOW, not the display

**A borderless window covering a whole monitor is eligible for DWM's
independent-flip path**: the game is scanned out directly and never lands in
the composited surface GDI reads. `screen.grab` returns `min=0 max=0` while the
desktop clock one pixel outside the window ticks to the second, which is
indistinguishable from a machine with no display attached -- and that is what
it was diagnosed as, twice.

Measured on one boot of one machine: `-full_as_borderless 1` pure black,
`-fullscreen 0 -size 1280 720` live, with the recogniser then reading `title`
at a separation of 0.15. **The size is the load-bearing half** -- it is what
stops the window covering the monitor -- and 16:9 keeps `overlay_layout.json`
valid unchanged, because every point in it is a fraction of the CLIENT rect
rather than a screen pixel.

`display_is_live` now names WHICH of the two faults it found, because they need
opposite fixes: **pure black blames the window** and prints the relaunch flags,
**bright (~70, a frozen copy of the desktop) blames the display**. The old
message only knew the second.

This supersedes "keep `full_as_borderless`", which was right that exclusive
fullscreen is unreadable and wrong that borderless is the cure.

### With Steam closed the game writes NO SAVES

It launches, loads a campaign, plays a raid and wins it -- and
`persist.raid.json` never appears, so `live_state`, `run.py`, the advisor and
every acknowledgement channel are blind. `app.log` carrying
`STEAM: AddCommandHandler` is the discriminator. This looks like nothing at all
going wrong, which is what makes it expensive.

The standing "CLOSE STEAM BEFORE AN UNATTENDED RUN" rule is about Cloud
restoring a **deleted** profile, so it binds the delete/claim cycle and not
`--resume`, which deletes nothing.

### Nothing could take the foreground

`act._force_foreground` has done the `AttachThreadInput` dance since the probe
was written, and neither focus wait was wired to it. Both sat waiting for a
click that was never coming: the boot gave up on the campaign, and
`_focused_or_wait` **loses the turn it is holding** rather than re-offering it.
Both now take the foreground first and verify second -- the order
`_wait_for_focus(auto=True)` already used -- with the wait loops untouched, so
a human clicking still works exactly as before.

### A corridor can be left three ways and only two judged the direction

`_walk_toward`'s walk loop calls `heading.observe` and flips on a mismatch; the
stall path flips through `_flip_heading`; the DOOR path -- walk to the end,
press `W` -- only reported the area it arrived in. So a `W` pressed at the
wrong end, which puts the party back in the room it came from, taught the
heading nothing, and `plan_room` republished the identical plan because
`Session.room` dedupes on (area, torch bracket) and both had changed back.

Measured live: `rooA` -> map click -> `coAB` -> `A` -> "at the end of coAB - W
into rooB" -> "entered rooA", once every 24 s for the whole 900 s play budget,
nine times over, with nothing else in the raid advancing. Same defect as the
`rooH`/`corH` oscillation `_forward_key` documents, in the one place it had not
been fixed. `tests/bridge/test_act.py` pins both directions and the wrong-room test
fails without the fix printing the two live log lines verbatim.

### `town_event` ended a campaign

"Hear Ye, Hear Ye!" -- the festival announcement DD raises on entering the
Hamlet -- swallows every click aimed at the town underneath and was refused as
an unmodelled screen. It is closed rather than read.

### RETRACTED: the save list is not compact

Mid-session I replaced `save_row(slot)`'s row==slot reading with a COMPACT list
-- one row per campaign, holes closed up -- on the strength of a boot that
clicked row 1 with `profile_0/8/9` on disk and loaded an existing campaign.
**That reading is wrong, and it fails silently in the worst direction**: on the
next run it clicked slot 1's empty letter and created `profile_1`, a campaign
nobody asked for.

Settled by scrolling the live list instead of inferring from outcomes. With
`profile_0`, `profile_1` and `profile_8` on disk, the bottom of the list reads
"Click the letter to begin...", "Click the letter to begin...", "Darkest -
Hamlet, Week 6". Those are slots 6, 7 and 8. **Every free slot draws its own
letter row**, so the row is the slot number and `profile_8` sits eight rows
down. That is exactly what `BRIDGE.md` already said; the inference was worth
less than one capture.

What survives is `campaign_row`, which answers only "is there a campaign at
this slot", because `--resume` must refuse a slot it cannot load rather than
click whatever letter happens to be there.

### Two save-directory traps, both live

- **`profile_9` is not a campaign.** It is the Butcher's Circus multiplayer
  profile -- `persist.circus_estate.json`, `persist.mp_progression.json`,
  `persist.rankings.json`, `persist.roster.network.json`, nine files and no
  `persist.game.json`. It is not drawn in the campaign list at all.
- **...and `read_town_state(profile_9)` returned `profile_8`'s roster**: 21
  heroes and 10,915 gold, byte-identical for both. That is the documented
  silent fallback firing in the open. A caller that trusts it will report on a
  campaign it is not looking at.

### A campaign fresh out of the tutorial has NO ROSTER PANEL

`profile_1` finished the Old Road, won it, walked the results chain and
returned to the Hamlet with 5,000 gold. Its save holds two heroes (Crusader 15
stress, Highwayman 66). **The game draws no roster panel on either the Hamlet
or the estate map** -- confirmed on a clean load, and confirmed not to be a
brightness artefact by amplifying the strip 3x, which shows only map artwork.

With no roster there is nothing to drag, so `embark_setup` seats nobody and the
estate map silently ignores `Provision` -- there is no party to provision for.
Every drag it sends comes back UNCONFIRMED, correctly. This is why the loop now
embarks through `embark_setup` rather than `to_raid`, and it is an open
question rather than a fixed bug: something about the post-tutorial state
suppresses the panel.

### What the raid layer actually recorded

One full tutorial raid, played end to end: 82 actions, 14 refusals, and a
